Probability: an open-problem working book

A twenty-hour research campaign, one worker

Checkpoint: 2026-09-06T22:55:18Z

Work began 6 September 2026 at15:13:26 UTC. The twenty-hour deadline is7 September 2026 at11:13:26 UTC. This is an evolving checkpoint, not a claim that the mandate is finished.

232 distinct questions admitted; 232 with a substantive recorded attempt; 0 original full solutions promoted. Target:1,000 distinct sourced questions. Shortfall at this checkpoint:768.

Primary sources supply the questions and historical open-status evidence. An old open-problem statement is not proof that the question remains open today. Current-status searches, known literature resolutions, author-checked partial arguments, and candidate proofs are recorded separately.

P0174 has a scoped independent analytic check that found no gap in the reviewed calculations; broader semantic review, novelty and registration remain pending. A successful ledger check, private repository push, PDF build, or math-brain retrieval test does not validate a proof. No routine exercise or cosmetic parameter variant is counted toward the target.

Current result limits

Seven full candidates are preserved: the Brownian prefix construction, the stationary nonergodic Galton-Watson flow, the sharp free-Brownian diameter constant, the rotation-algebra Brown-measure counterexample, the compact-initial-law multiplicative counterexample, the wreath-graph parameter counterexample, and the uniform-CLT counterexample. Their exact assumptions and review limits are in the proof appendices. None is promoted as an original solved open problem. Known theorem rederivations remain separate.

Persistent math brain

Sixteen private draft entries and 18 links have been ingested in two accepted bundles. Nine positive paraphrase searches retrieved the intended entries at rank 1; four negative controls were clean. A rejected intermediate bundle with invalid license metadata was corrected and is not counted separately. These are retrieval checks, not independent proof review or public promotion.

Operational record

The live wake for this thread reported a latest dream age of30.5 hours against a30.0-hour ceiling; the degraded finding was reported. The signed-project-contract cache warning remains unresolved. Work is confined to private research artifacts; no public publication, deployment, GPU training, or remote-server work is part of this campaign.

How to read each entry

Each question has a source and location, a paraphrased mathematical target, scope caveats, and its own attempted route. A failed route is reported as such; an example against a proof method is not relabeled as a counterexample to the source conjecture. Closely overlapping source formulations are grouped, while excluded literature resolutions appear separately.

source_says_open

The primary source presents this as unresolved at its writing date.

unverified

The current literature status is not established by this campaign.

no_resolution_found_in_documented_search

A dated search found no matching resolution; absence of a search hit is not proof of openness.

attempted_unsolved

An individual mathematical attempt is recorded, but the full target is not settled.

candidate proof

A full argument has been drafted, with assumptions and novelty/current-status checks still pending.

proved_here_author_checked

Reserved for a complete source-matched argument that survives repeated author checks; this is weaker than independent review.

resolved_in_literature

A pre-existing theorem or counterexample addresses the source question; it is not an original solution here.

Question

For every finite simple graph, does its uniform acyclic edge subset have pairwise nonpositive edge covariance?

P0001-A001 | Condition independent weighted Bernoulli edges on acyclicity; factor disjoint cycle constraints in cactus graphs

Argument or result

Complete author-checked proof of negative association for all finite cactus graphs and finite positive edge weights; target on arbitrary graphs remains unsolved here

Check

Direct subset counts for uniform cycles give covariance -2^(m-2)/(2^m-1)^2; triangle -2/49 and tree zero agree

Remaining obstruction

Overlapping cycle constraints do not factor; generic conditioning on a decreasing event fails. Need an inequality using graphic-matroid circuit elimination.

P0002  Self-avoiding-walk counting exponents

Source: Selected problems in probability theory | Conjecture 2.1, PDF page 3 and footnote

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.07318

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Determine the asymptotic correction to c_n=mu^n for lattice self-avoiding walks in dimensions two, three, and four.

Scope

The source predicts gamma_2=43/32 and explicitly requires logarithmic corrections in dimension four. High-dimensional cases already proved are excluded from the unresolved target. This remains one source problem, not separate parameter rows.

P0002-A001 | Submultiplicativity and its limits

Argument or result

On Z^d, splitting a walk gives c_(m+n)<=c_m c_n. Positive-coordinate walks give d^n<=c_n, and the nonbacktracking bound gives c_n<=2d(2d-1)^(n-1). Fekete determines the exponential growth rate. However a_n=mu^n exp(sqrt(n)) is submultiplicative and has no fixed polynomial prefactor.

Check

The example is a real sequence, not a self-avoiding-walk counterexample. The square-root subadditivity checks the claimed property directly.

Remaining obstruction

Submultiplicativity cannot determine the critical exponent or the dimension-four logarithmic correction.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0003  Hexagonal self-avoiding-walk scaling limit

Source: Selected problems in probability theory | Question 2.2, PDF page 3

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.07318

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does the rescaled uniform n-step hexagonal-lattice self-avoiding walk converge to SLE_(8/3)?

Scope

The source leaves the precise rescaling, curve topology, parametrization and limiting SLE geometry implicit; these are proof obligations, not permissions to change the question.

P0003-A001 | Exact finite-domain conditional law

Argument or result

For a finite-graph a-to-b self-avoiding path with weight z^length, condition on an initial simple prefix. The remaining path has the same fugacity law on the graph with earlier prefix vertices removed. The factor z^prefix_length cancels between numerator and partition function.

Check

The endpoint stays in the graph; deleting it would invalidate the argument. Fixed-length conditioning also fixes the remaining length and is a different ensemble.

Remaining obstruction

This domain Markov property supplies neither tightness nor conformal invariance and does not identify an SLE scaling limit.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

Question

On the infinite supercritical bond-percolation cluster of Z^2, does c_n(v)^(1/n) converge almost surely, independently of v?

P0004-A001 | Adjacent-root walk decomposition and a Markov/Borel-Cantelli bound

Argument or result

Root-independent limsup on every infinite connected bounded-degree graph; annealed upper bound p mu

Check

Walks avoiding the neighboring root inject by prepending; walks visiting it inject into reversed-prefix/suffix pairs. These are disjoint cases and preserve lengths.

Remaining obstruction

The cut-walk inequality has an environment-dependent endpoint maximum. No stationary scalar subadditive process or liminf lower bound was obtained.

P0005  Ehrenfest mirror escape

Source: Selected problems in probability theory | Question 3.1, PDF pages 4-5

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.07318

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Which mirror densities p give positive escape probability in the square-lattice model with equally likely NE/NW mirrors?

P0005-A001 | Invertible routing and finite-volume diagnostics

Argument or result

The local mirror pairings define an invertible map on oriented light states. Its orbits are cycles or bi-infinite trajectories. On a finite periodic torus every orbit is a cycle. A mirror-free initial straight run of N sites has probability (1-p)^N.

Check

The finite-state bijection proves the torus assertion independently of sampling. The straight-run bound tends to zero for each fixed p>0.

Remaining obstruction

Finite periodic loop counts cannot decide infinite-volume escape; the N-to-infinity and p-to-zero limits cannot be interchanged from this bound.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0006  Poisson mirror escape threshold

Source: Selected problems in probability theory | Question 3.2(a-c), PDF pages 5-6

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.07318

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For nondegenerate Poisson-mirror orientation laws, characterize escape below the vacant-set threshold, including uniform orientations.

Scope

All three source subquestions are grouped to avoid counting nested parameter variants separately. The direction quantifier for theta_mu must be resolved from the model before a theorem claim.

P0006-A001 | Exact first-collision distribution for Poisson mirrors

Argument or result

For a straight test ray segment of length L in direction alpha and mirrors of total length epsilon with orientation law mu, the centers whose mirror crosses the segment form a parallelogram of area epsilon L |sin(alpha-beta)| for angle beta. Unit-intensity marked Poisson counting gives no-collision probability exp(-epsilon L integral |sin(alpha-beta)| mu(dbeta)). Thus the first free flight is exponential whenever this rate is positive.

Check

The factor is total mirror length, not half-length. Parallel mirrors contribute zero area and endpoint coincidences have zero intensity. The rate is positive for every alpha if mu is not concentrated on a single unoriented direction.

Remaining obstruction

After reflection, previously examined empty regions and previously hit mirrors make the flight history dependent. The first-flight computation gives no independent-flight construction or escape threshold.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0007  Unbounded mirror component trapping every ray

Source: Selected problems in probability theory | Question 3.3, PDF page 6

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.07318

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Can an unbounded component of a mirror complement contain an origin from which every reflected ray stays bounded?

P0007-A001 | Attempted reduction to straight visibility

Argument or result

An unbounded unobstructed straight ray from the origin immediately rules out the proposed all-directions trapped configuration. However an unbounded connected planar open set need not contain any unbounded straight ray: a sufficiently narrow unbounded spiral corridor is a topological counterexample to that inference.

Check

The corridor is only a counterexample to the topological inference. It is not asserted to be a configuration of the source mirror model.

Remaining obstruction

Unboundedness of the vacant component gives a continuous route, not a realizable billiard route. No mirror configuration trapping all angles, or theorem preventing one, was constructed.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0008  Diffusive Poisson mirror trajectories

Source: Selected problems in probability theory | Question 3.4, PDF page 7

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.07318

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For rationally supported nondegenerate mirror orientations, do escaping trajectories have finite positive diffusivity and a Gaussian limit?

P0008-A001 | Green-Kubo variance and a failed CLT shortcut

Argument or result

For a stationary mean-zero scalar velocity with integrable covariance C(s), Var(integral_0^t V_s ds)/t = 2 integral_0^t (1-s/t) C(s) ds tends to 2 integral_0^infinity C(s) ds. Covariance control alone does not imply a Gaussian limit: let Y_i=A epsilon_i with independent fair signs and independent A equal to 1 or 2 with probability 1/2. Distinct increments are uncorrelated, but n^(-1/2) sum Y_i converges to the equally weighted mixture of N(0,1) and N(0,4).

Check

Conditioning on A proves the mixture limit; its fourth moment differs from that of a Gaussian with the same variance. This is not a unit-speed mirror-model counterexample.

Remaining obstruction

Need mixing or a martingale approximation for the billiard environment, including the effect of conditioning on an unbounded trajectory.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0009  Manhattan pinball localization

Source: Selected problems in probability theory | Question 3.5, PDF page 7; model Figure 3.2

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.07318

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does every positive mirror density localize the Manhattan-oriented pinball trajectory?

P0009-A001 | Small-density straight paths and lack of an order argument

Argument or result

At q=0 a ray moves indefinitely along its Manhattan street. The event that its first N potential collisions contain no mirror has probability (1-q)^N. This only controls a finite initial flight and gives no positive lower bound as N increases at fixed q.

Check

The finite-run probability agrees with independence and has the correct q=0 endpoint. Adding a mirror changes routing rather than merely deleting traversable states, so an edgewise percolation monotonicity proof has not been supplied.

Remaining obstruction

The known localization interval near and above one-half cannot be extended to every q>0 using this finite-run bound or an unproved monotonicity assumption.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0010  Biased random square-lattice orientations

Source: Selected problems in probability theory | Question 5.1, PDF page 9

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.07318

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does every nonzero orientation bias give positive probability of an infinite directed path from the origin?

P0010-A001 | Directed-percolation subgraph comparison

Argument or result

Reflecting both coordinate axes maps p to 1-p, so the infinite-path probability is symmetric about one-half. North-east paths use independent edges pointing north/east with probability p; survival of the usual north-east directed percolation therefore implies survival in the full oriented graph. The p=0 and p=1 endpoints have deterministic infinite paths.

Check

The comparison is inclusion of path events, not equality of the two models. It proves a region above the directed-percolation threshold and its reflected counterpart only.

Remaining obstruction

It does not settle the interval between one-half and that threshold. The critical p=1/2 result does not extend by continuity alone.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0011  Brownian SIR survival in dimensions at least two

Source: Selected problems in probability theory | Question 6.1(ii), PDF pages 9-10

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2205.07318

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does the fully diffusing Poisson Brownian SIR model survive for sufficiently small positive removal rate when d>=2?

P0011-A001 | Counterexample to the usual pathwise lifetime monotonicity coupling

Argument or result

Consider a deterministic contact schedule with A initially infected: A-C at time .1, A-B at 1, C-B at 9, B-D at 10, and no other contacts. Baseline infectious lifetimes are L_A=.5, L_C=12, L_B=2. Then B is infected at 9 and infects D at 10. Multiply every lifetime by 3: A now infects B at 1; B is removed at 7, cannot be reinfected at 9, and D is never infected.

Check

Check both timelines: C remains infectious at 9 in each case; A has already died at 1 only in the baseline. The example refutes infection-set inclusion under scaled lifetimes on a fixed contact schedule.

Remaining obstruction

This is not a counterexample to survival monotonicity or to the Brownian epidemic conjecture. It blocks a direct attractiveness proof; a Brownian contact-support realization was not proved.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

Question

For symmetric order-r tensors T_i on R^d, p>=2 and independent standard Gaussians g_i, is E||sum_i g_i T_i||_(I_p) bounded by polylog(d,n) times O_(r,p)(d^(1/2-1/p) sqrt(sum_i ||T_i||_(I_p)^2))?

Scope

The unresolved range is p<2r apart from known matrix and other special cases. The source says p>=2r is proved. All parameters remain one problem.

P0012-A001 | Diagonal-tensor Gaussian estimate

Argument or result

For a diagonal homogeneous form T(x)=sum_j a_j x_j^r and 2<=p<=r, its supremum over the l_p unit ball is max_j |a_j|. Indeed sum |x_j|^r<=1 and a coordinate vector attains the maximum. For independent standard g_i, each diagonal coefficient of sum_i g_i T_i has variance at most sum_i ||T_i||^2, so the Gaussian maximum bound gives E||sum_i g_i T_i|| <= sqrt(2 log(2d)) (sum_i ||T_i||^2)^(1/2).

Check

The upper bound follows by the exponential-moment union bound and minimizing its parameter; coordinate vectors prove the norm identity even for even r by taking absolute value outside the form.

Remaining obstruction

This verifies a restricted diagonal family. Off-diagonal terms and the full p range require the tensor geometry absent here.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0013  Expected Lovasz theta of a dense independent-edge graph

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2025 | Conjecture 17, Entry 9

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29571

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For G distributed as G(n,1/2), is E theta(G)=(1+o(1)) sqrt(n)?

Scope

Theta is the SDP with PSD matrix, trace one, and zero entries on graph edges.

P0013-A001 | Complement symmetry and uniform-integrability obstruction

Argument or result

The theta product inequality theta(G) theta(complement G)>=n and complement symmetry of G(n,1/2) give E theta>=sqrt(n) by arithmetic-geometric mean. A proof of concentration on a logarithmic scale would still need tail control: set Z_n to plus or minus (log n)/2, each with probability 1/(2 sqrt(n)), and zero otherwise. Then Z_n tends to zero in probability and variance, but E exp(Z_n) tends to 3/2.

Check

The example has a symmetric law and exp(Z_n) in [n^(-1/2),n^(1/2)]. Its expectation is 1-1/sqrt(n)+(1+n^(-1))/(2), which tends to 3/2. It is not a random-graph construction.

Remaining obstruction

Need a sharp upper bound and uniform integrability at the sqrt(n) scale; complement symmetry only yields the lower bound.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0014  Expected Lovasz theta of a random circulant graph

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2025 | Conjecture 18, Entry 9

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29571

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For the graph on Z/nZ formed by independently including each unordered nonzero generator pair {k,-k} with probability 1/2, is E theta(G)=(1+o(1)) sqrt(n)?

Scope

The inverse-pair convention includes the order-two generator once when n is even; this differs substantively from independent edges.

P0014-A001 | Vertex-transitive complement identity

Argument or result

A circulant graph is vertex transitive, so theta(G) theta(complement G)=n. Its random model is complement invariant. For Z=log(theta(G)/sqrt(n)), Z has the law of -Z and E theta/sqrt(n)=E cosh Z. The conjecture is therefore equivalent to E(cosh Z-1) tending to zero.

Check

The exact identity is stronger than the general product inequality. The symmetric rare-tail example in P0013 has Z tending to zero in probability yet E cosh Z tending to 3/2, so concentration alone is insufficient.

Remaining obstruction

Must estimate the exponential tails of the logarithmic theta fluctuation for the dependent circulant adjacency structure.

P0015  Random complex phase-retrieval injectivity at 4M-5 measurements

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2025 | Conjecture 19(a,b), Entry 10

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29571

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Let p_M be the probability that a complex Gaussian (4M-5)-by-M matrix gives an injective phaseless measurement map modulo global phase. Is p_M<1 for each meaningful M, and does p_M tend to zero?

Scope

M>=2 is needed for the matrix size to be meaningful. The two clauses are retained as one source conjecture, not counted twice.

P0015-A001 | Complete M=2 obstruction via Hermitian lifting

Argument or result

For M=2 the matrix has three complex Gaussian rows. Almost surely these span C^2. The real-linear measurement map from the four-dimensional Hermitian 2-by-2 space to R^3 has a nonzero kernel element H. H cannot be positive or negative semidefinite: a_i^* H a_i=0 for a spanning set would force H=0. Thus H has one positive and one negative eigenvalue and can be written xx^*-yy^* with noncollinear x,y. Their three intensity measurements agree. Hence p_2=0.

Check

Dimension gives a nonzero H without assuming generic rank three; the spanning condition rules out semidefinite kernel elements. Spectral decomposition independently verifies equal squared magnitudes.

Remaining obstruction

For M>2 a nonzero kernel matrix need not have rank at most two with one eigenvalue of each sign. The requisite low-rank intersection probability remains unresolved.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0016  Worst square-submatrix conditioning in a Gaussian frame

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2025 | Open Problem 21 and Definition 10.1, Entry 10

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29571

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For a (2M-1)-by-M matrix of independent standard real Gaussians, determine the asymptotic size of omega(A), the minimum smallest singular value over row subsets whose complement has rank below M. In particular determine whether its decay is exponential and identify the exponential rate.

Scope

Almost surely every M-row subset is nonsingular, so the minimum reduces to M-row subsets. Interpret asymptotic size in probability; the source does not specify a convergence mode or a normalized logarithmic limit.

P0016-A001 | Union small-ball lower bound and one-normal upper bound

Argument or result

For a standard M-by-M real Gaussian matrix A, sigma_min(A)<=t implies that some column has distance at most sqrt(M)t from the span of the other columns: project a minimizing unit vector equation using a coordinate of magnitude at least 1/sqrt(M). Each such distance has the law |N(0,1)|, so P(sigma_min<=t)<=sqrt(2/pi) M^(3/2)t. There are at most 4^M M-row submatrices of a (2M-1)-by-M frame. Therefore its minimum omega exceeds exp(-(log 4+epsilon)M) with probability tending to one. Conversely fix M-1 rows and a unit normal u. The M remaining row projections are independent N(0,1), and omega is at most their minimum absolute value, giving omega<=C log(M)/M with probability tending to one for sufficiently large C.

Check

Full spark holds almost surely. Adding rows increases the Gram matrix, so the minimum relevant frame bound is attained among M-row subsets. Both bounds use union/inclusion inequalities and never assume independence among overlapping minors.

Remaining obstruction

The exponential lower bound and polynomial upper bound are far apart. Sharp extremes of the strongly dependent minors remain uncontrolled.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0017  Dimension-free log-concave Poincare or Cheeger constant

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2025 | Conjecture 30, Entry 13

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29571

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Is there a universal positive lower bound for the Euclidean Cheeger constant of every isotropic log-concave probability measure, in every dimension?

Scope

Use outer boundary liminf in the standard definition; the source's displayed halfspace formula has an S/H notational mismatch. Thin-shell and slicing results are separate and reported resolved by this source.

P0017-A001 | Gaussian special case by orthogonal expansion

Argument or result

For standard Gaussian measure, expand a polynomial f in orthonormal multivariate Hermite polynomials with coefficients a_alpha. Then Var(f)=sum_(|alpha|>=1) a_alpha^2 <= sum |alpha| a_alpha^2 = E||gradient f||^2. Closure in Gaussian Sobolev norm extends the inequality to its natural domain.

Check

The constant is attained by any nonconstant linear function, checking both normalization and dimension independence.

Remaining obstruction

A general isotropic log-concave measure lacks this diagonal Hermite generator. This special case does not prove its uniform Poincare or Cheeger bound.

P0018  Sharp polynomial-logarithmic expected norm of graph matrices

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2025 | Conjecture 31 and Definition 14.2, Entry 14

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.29571

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For each fixed graph shape alpha with specified left and right index sets, form M_alpha by summing injective labelings with products of independent Rademacher edge variables. Must E||M_alpha|| be Theta(n^f(alpha) (log n)^g(alpha)) for some shape-dependent exponents?

Scope

The target is the general existence of sharp exponents, not a separate problem for every shape. Definitions of overlapping or internal vertex sets must follow the full cited graph-matrix convention.

P0018-A001 | Moment expansion with a crude operator-norm bound

Argument or result

For every finite random graph matrix, E||M|| <= (E||M||_F^2)^(1/2). Expanding each entry as a sum of Rademacher edge monomials, cross terms vanish unless the two monomials use every edge with even combined multiplicity. For a fixed shape the remaining label identifications can be counted to obtain a polynomial upper bound in n.

Check

Orthogonality concerns equality of edge monomials, not equality of vertex labelings. Distinct labelings can produce the same edge set and must not be discarded.

Remaining obstruction

The Frobenius bound can lose powers of n and cannot decide the sharp operator norm or the existence of one power-log asymptotic form.

P0019  Brownian exit approach directions in higher dimensions

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 1

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For Brownian motion exiting a domain in dimension d>2, is the set of limiting approach directions at the exit point almost surely a sphere or a hemisphere?

Scope

The question is meaningful on finite exit; either condition on finite exit or restrict to bounded domains. Do not call infinite exit a counterexample to the intended geometric question.

P0019-A001 | Smooth-boundary directional containment

Argument or result

At an exit point z where the domain boundary is C^1 with outward unit normal n, local differentiability gives n dot (z-x)>=-o(|z-x|) for x in the domain approaching z. Thus every limit of (z-B_s)/|z-B_s| as s increases to the exit time lies in the closed hemisphere {u:n dot u>=0}.

Check

The sign is checked on a half-space, where the vector from an interior point to the exit point has positive outward-normal component.

Remaining obstruction

Containment does not prove that all hemisphere directions occur, and there is no supporting tangent half-space at the arbitrary boundaries in the full question.

P0020  Finite-crossing arcs through a planar Brownian trace

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 2(i)

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Almost surely, can every two points outside the trace of planar Brownian motion on [0,1] be connected by a Jordan arc meeting the trace at only finitely many points?

Scope

The quantifier is simultaneous over all pairs of outside points, not a single deterministic pair.

P0020-A001 | Reduction to component adjacency and access

Argument or result

If both endpoints lie in the same component of the complement of the Brownian trace, an open connected planar set is polygonally connected; removing loops gives a simple arc avoiding the trace entirely. If an arc crosses the trace finitely many times, its complementary intervals give a finite sequence of complement components with touching closures at successive crossing points.

Check

A closure contact alone does not ensure an accessible common boundary point. This is why the necessary finite-chain condition was not promoted to an equivalence.

Remaining obstruction

Need finite accessible chains for every pair of components, simultaneously for the Brownian trace.

P0021  Interior remainder of planar Brownian trace

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 2(ii)

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Delete from a planar Brownian trace on [0,1] all boundaries of connected components of its complement. Is the remaining set totally disconnected almost surely?

Scope

This is a different topological property from Problem 2(i), although a negative answer is stated to imply a negative answer there.

P0021-A001 | Failure of transferring total disconnectedness through the path map

Argument or result

A possible approach is to identify a totally disconnected set of Brownian times surviving boundary removal. That would not suffice: the Cantor function restricted to the middle-third Cantor set maps this totally disconnected compact set continuously onto [0,1]. Continuous images can be connected.

Check

The restriction is onto because every value of the Cantor function is attained on the Cantor set, including plateau values at endpoints.

Remaining obstruction

The spatial remainder needs direct Brownian geometry. Showing it contains no simple arc would also be weaker than showing every connected component is a singleton.

P0022  Percolation dimension of a planar Brownian trace

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 3

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Is the infimum of Hausdorff dimensions of nontrivial Jordan arcs contained in a planar Brownian trace on [0,1] equal to one almost surely?

Scope

A nontrivial arc contains at least two distinct points. The lower bound one alone is not a solution.

P0022-A001 | Universal dimension lower bound for connecting sets

Argument or result

Any connected set containing distinct x and y has Hausdorff dimension at least one. Orthogonal projection onto their joining line is Lipschitz and has connected image containing the interval between the projections, so its dimension is at least one.

Check

This argument does not require rectifiability or a parametrized simple curve, and hence applies to the connectivity notion in a percolation-dimension lower bound.

Remaining obstruction

Polygonal approximations of a Brownian path are not subsets of its trace. The missing step is constructing sufficiently thin connecting subsets inside the trace itself.

P0023  Efficient reflected-Brownian coupling in acute triangles

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 4

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

In any acute triangle, can reflected Brownian motions from distinct starting points be coupled so their meeting-time tail decays at every exponential rate strictly below the first positive Neumann spectral rate?

Scope

Check the generator convention in Burdzy-Kendall (2000): the page calls its rate a Laplacian eigenvalue. A factor-two convention discrepancy is not a solution of the coupling question.

P0023-A001 | Spectral lower bound on coupling tails

Argument or result

Let phi be a bounded nonconstant Neumann eigenfunction with semigroup P_t phi=e^(-lambda t) phi. For any coalescent coupling with meeting time T, |P_t phi(x)-P_t phi(y)|<=2||phi||_infinity P(T>t). Choosing phi(x)!=phi(y) yields P(T>t)>=c e^(-lambda t).

Check

The exponent is the eigenvalue of the actual reflected-Brownian generator: using Delta rather than Delta/2 changes it by a factor of two.

Remaining obstruction

This proves that the spectral rate is an upper limit on coupling efficiency, but gives no coupling attaining arbitrarily close to that rate in an acute triangle.

P0024  Failure of synchronous contraction in a bounded planar domain

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 5(i)

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does some bounded connected smooth planar domain admit synchronously coupled reflected Brownian motions whose distance has positive limsup with positive probability?

Scope

The page states that any such domain must have at least two holes by existing results.

P0024-A001 | Synchronous contraction on convex domains

Argument or result

For two reflected motions driven by the same Brownian path, the noise cancels in their difference. With inward normals, d|X-Y|^2=2<X-Y,n(X)>dL_X-2<X-Y,n(Y)>dL_Y. Convexity makes both contributions nonpositive, so the distance is nonincreasing.

Check

Each sign follows from the supporting half-space inequality at the boundary point. The calculation uses finite-variation reflection terms and has no missing quadratic-variation term.

Remaining obstruction

Nonincrease alone does not prove convergence to zero. Nonconvex domains can have both signs and require control of the long-run reflection geometry.

P0025  Synchronous reflection outside a disk

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 5(ii)

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For synchronously coupled reflected Brownian motions in the exterior of a nondegenerate closed disk, can their distance have positive limsup with positive probability?

Scope

This unbounded exterior geometry differs from the bounded-domain existence question.

P0025-A001 | Exterior-disk reflection has both distance signs

Argument or result

At x=(R,0) on the inner circular boundary of the exterior domain, its inward-to-domain normal is (1,0). For y=(r,0), r>R, <x-y,n>=R-r<0; for y=(-r,0) it is R+r>0. Hence a reflection at X can either shrink or enlarge the synchronous separation.

Check

Both test locations lie in the exterior and the normal points into that same domain, fixing the otherwise easy sign error.

Remaining obstruction

A global monotone-distance proof is unavailable. The question turns on boundary occupation and angular correlations over indefinitely long times.

P0026  Non-explosion of Fleming-Viot Brownian particles in arbitrary domains

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 6 with 2024 update

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For N>=3 Brownian particles in any bounded open connected domain, killed at the boundary and immediately cloned from a uniformly selected survivor, are the successive death times almost surely non-accumulating?

Scope

N=2 is explicitly reported solved by Kwasnicki (2024). The earlier general proof had an irreparable error; it is not admissible evidence for the general result.

P0026-A001 | Attempted boundary-distance Lyapunov proof

Argument or result

The candidate Lyapunov function -sum_i log dist(X_i,boundary D) becomes singular when particles approach the boundary, but an Ito argument needs derivative bounds. Even for a smooth domain, the distance is nonsmooth on its medial set; for arbitrary domains there may be no uniformly controlled smooth substitute. At a cloning event another particle may be still closer to the boundary, so the jump is not uniformly favorable.

Check

The known N=2 result is excluded from this target. A pair comparison does not transfer automatically to N>=3 because a particle can clone the third particle rather than its proposed comparator.

Remaining obstruction

No Lyapunov drift bound uniform over arbitrary boundary geometry and all cloning configurations was obtained.

P0027  Rigidity of shy reflected-Brownian couplings

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 7

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

If reflected Brownian motions in a bounded connected domain can remain a positive distance apart forever with positive probability, must there be such a coupling with one path a fixed deterministic function of the other at each time?

Scope

Regularity sufficient to define the intended reflected Brownian motion needs to be fixed from the cited background. A metric-graph counterexample on the page is not a Euclidean-domain counterexample.

P0027-A001 | Explicit deterministic shy coupling on an annulus

Argument or result

If an isometry F preserves a reflected-Brownian domain and has inf_x |x-F(x)|>0, take Y_t=F(X_t). Orthogonal transformation of Brownian noise and equivariance of inward reflection show Y has the correct reflected law, while separation stays positive. On R<|x|<R_out, F(x)=-x yields |X_t-Y_t|>=2R.

Check

The annulus excludes the origin; a disk would have zero minimum displacement and would not satisfy this check.

Remaining obstruction

This verifies a nonempty deterministic class. It does not turn an arbitrary shy coupling into a single state map with the required path consistency.

P0028  Bounded independent Brownian pieces and two-sided Brownian motion

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 8; compare Burdzy-Scheutzow (2014) Problem 7.7

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Concatenate independent Brownian path segments cut at stopping times indexed by all integers, with lengths uniformly bounded by a deterministic constant and divergent sums in both directions. Is the resulting path two-sided Brownian after some random time shift?

Scope

The paper's related backward-Brownian question and its almost-sure random bound are recorded as related variants, not an extra count. Independent lengths alone are not the same as stopping times depending on their Brownian paths.

P0028-A001 | Stationary Palm construction in the identically distributed finite-mean case

Argument or result

For iid stopped Brownian segments with 0<E T<infinity, the cycle sequence is ergodic under shifting its index. A stationary occupation law is Q(F)=E[integral_0^T F(theta_s X) ds]/E T. Shifting by t changes the numerator by E[integral_T^(T+t) F(theta_s X) ds-integral_0^t F(theta_s X) ds]=0 because the concatenation rooted at the next cycle has the same Palm law. Tilting the iid cycle sequence by T_0/E T and choosing a uniform time in that cycle realizes this law; the forward filling lemma supplies that tilt by a finite randomized index shift.

Check

For each deterministic s, {s<T} is measurable from the segment up to s. Future Brownian increments followed by independent stopped segments retain the Brownian law and are independent of the past. Integrating over s gives a Brownian future under Q; stationarity then supplies a Brownian past and its independence. This is a proof outline pending full artifact and earlier-literature comparison, not a newly claimed theorem.

Remaining obstruction

The actual question allows independent non-identically distributed segments. There is then no invariant iid index law or common Palm mean to which this argument applies.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0028-A002 | Primary-source check of the iid finite-mean reduction

Argument or result

Burdzy-Scheutzow (2014), Theorem 5.1 explicitly proves that strongly decomposable processes with finite mean segment length are two-sided Brownian motions. P0028-A001 rederives this known special case and is not a novel solution.

Remaining obstruction

The independent non-identically distributed bounded-length question remains unresolved here.

Verification: source_theorem_checked | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0029  Separation of two peaks in random square-grid labelings

Source: Krzysztof Burdzy: My favorite open problems | Problem 9

https://sites.math.washington.edu/~burdzy/open_mathjax.php

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Uniformly label an n-by-n square grid and condition on exactly two local maxima. Does their graph distance divided by n converge in distribution to zero?

Scope

This is the literal displayed question, despite the heading about repulsion. Verify against Twin peaks (2020) before correcting the sign or reporting a counterexample.

P0029-A001 | Exact small-grid count and an orientation reformulation

Argument or result

On the 2-by-2 grid (a four-cycle), exactly two peaks must be opposite and carry labels 3 and 4. There are two choices of diagonal, two orders of the top labels, and two orders of the bottom labels: eight of the 24 labelings. Their graph distance is always two. More generally orient each edge toward its larger label. Two peaks means precisely two sinks of the induced acyclic orientation; the number of labelings yielding a fixed orientation is its number of linear extensions.

Check

Adjacent vertices cannot both be peaks. In the four-cycle, a proposed peak with label 2 would have to exceed both other nonpeak labels, which is impossible; this checks the count without enumeration software.

Remaining obstruction

The small-grid calculation gives no large-n scale. The orientation sum requires sharp weighted counts of acyclic orientations with two prescribed sinks. The source title says repel while its displayed question asks distance/n -> 0; that wording discrepancy is retained, not silently corrected.

P0030  Compatibility of forward and backward Brownian decompositions

Source: Forward Brownian motion | Problems 7.1-7.3, PDF pages 25-26

https://math.washington.edu/sites/math/files/documents/research/1302.6958.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does a decomposable forward Brownian motion which is also backward Brownian necessarily become two-sided Brownian after a random shift? The source also asks weaker related formulations using independence of past and future at a random time.

Scope

The three linked formulations are grouped as one compatibility problem. A general process that is both forward and backward Brownian without the extra assumptions is already counterexampled in the paper.

P0030-A001 | Common-splice compatibility obstruction

Argument or result

Two independent standard Brownian half-paths attached at the same deterministic time give a two-sided Brownian motion. For a general process certified separately as forward and backward Brownian, the certifying random times can differ. Moving either certificate to another random time can bias its marginal law, as the selection-density calculation in P0032 demonstrates.

Check

Independence of two halves at a proposed joining time is insufficient if their Brownian marginal laws have been lost there.

Remaining obstruction

No construction aligns both certificates at one time while preserving both marginal laws and their independence.

P0031  Sparse first-passage pieces in a forward Brownian concatenation

Source: Forward Brownian motion | Problem 7.4, PDF page 26

https://math.washington.edu/sites/math/files/documents/research/1302.6958.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

In a two-sided independent concatenation, use unit-length Brownian segments except at increasingly rare negative-time indices where segments stop on first hitting -1. Characterize frequencies giving backward or two-sided Brownian motion.

Scope

The exact deterministic or random schedule is part of the classification; do not invent a schedule and count it as another sourced question.

P0031-A001 | Sparse exceptional hitting-time clock estimate

Argument or result

For a standard Brownian first hitting time tau of -1, reflection gives P(tau>t)=2 Phi(1/sqrt(t))-1 ~ sqrt(2/pi)t^(-1/2), hence E tau=infinity. If exceptional segment indices are 2^k, then sum_k P(tau_k>epsilon 2^k)<infinity. Borel-Cantelli yields tau_k/2^k ->0 almost surely. Splitting the sum into finitely many early terms and a geometrically weighted tail gives sum_(k<=K) tau_k/2^K ->0.

Check

Independence is not needed for this Borel-Cantelli upper bound. Infinite means do not contradict the almost-sure sparse-clock estimate.

Remaining obstruction

A negligible exceptional time fraction is an asymptotic statement; it does not construct an exact backward Brownian law after a finite random shift. The full frequency classification remains open here.

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: candidate_counterexample

Question

If a consistent equivariant Borel flow rule has a stationary probability measure equivalent to Galton-Watson law, must the associated subtree Markov chain be ergodic?

Scope

The definition permits zero child weights. Strict positivity is a separate sufficient hypothesis in Proposition 17.17. Exercise 17.22 only treats a merely absolutely continuous stationary measure.

P0033-A001 | Endogenous AND/OR tree coloring and conditional subtree selection

Argument or result

Candidate counterexample: GW itself stationary, with two invariant color classes of mass one half

Check

Exact second-moment recursion proves color measurability; full conditional subtree law proves stationarity; source definition expressly permits zero child weights

No mathematical gap currently identified; full author recheck and prior tree-automaton literature pending

P0034  Recurrence of the planar horizontal-new vertical-old walk

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 2.27, printed p.56

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Start at the origin in Z^2; after first visiting a site step horizontally with either sign equally likely, and after a repeat visit step vertically with either sign equally likely. Does any site receive infinitely many visits with positive probability?

Scope

The source indexing omits the first step; use its intended new-site rule also at time zero. The analogous four- and three-dimensional results do not decide this planar model.

P0034-A001 | Martingale estimates for the axis-switching walk

Argument or result

The axis of the next step is predictable from the past, while its sign is fair. Both coordinates are martingales, their cross quadratic covariation is zero, and E|X_n|^2=n. Bounded increments and Azuma give summable bounds for P(|X_n|>epsilon n), so X_n/n tends to zero almost surely.

Check

The predictable quadratic variation of the horizontal coordinate counts first visits, and the vertical one counts repeat visits; their sum is exactly n.

Remaining obstruction

Zero speed and a diffusive second moment do not imply recurrence. The occupation-dependent balance of the two coordinate clocks remains uncontrolled.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0035  Positive speed of inward-biased walks below the Cayley growth rate

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 3.11, printed p.91

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For a Cayley graph of exponential growth rate b and 1<lambda<b, does the distance from the identity divided by time for RW_lambda converge almost surely to a strictly positive speed?

Scope

RW_lambda uses the source's radial conductances, favoring motion toward the identity for lambda>1. The case lambda=1 may have zero speed and is excluded.

P0035-A001 | Exact radial speed on a regular tree

Argument or result

On the (b+1)-regular rooted tree, away from the root the radial walk steps out with probability b/(b+lambda) and in with probability lambda/(b+lambda). For lambda<b its speed is (b-lambda)/(b+lambda)>0: the root is visited finitely often and the bounded-difference martingale law of large numbers applies to the radial drift.

Check

The speed tends to zero at lambda=b and to one at lambda=0, checking the normalization.

Remaining obstruction

General Cayley graphs have dead ends and nonuniform distance-level neighborhoods. Transience alone does not control time spent in traps.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0036  Percolation threshold on arbitrary geodesic Cayley spanning trees

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 3.12, printed p.91

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

If T is any geodesic spanning tree rooted at the identity of a Cayley graph of growth rate b, must br(T)=b, equivalently must its Bernoulli bond percolation threshold be 1/b?

Scope

The branching-number/percolation equivalence is a theorem of the source. The question is stated there geometrically; its probability formulation is recorded transparently. Lexicographically minimal spanning trees satisfy it.

P0036-A001 | Equal level growth does not determine branching number

Argument or result

A geodesic spanning tree has exactly the Cayley graph sphere sizes at each level. But growth can exceed branching number for a bounded-degree tree: take a single infinite spine and at its nth vertex attach a finite b-ary tree of height n. Its level sizes have exponential rate sqrt(b)>1, while its only infinite ray is the spine and arbitrarily deep singleton cutsets give branching number one.

Check

At level m the largest attached contribution starts around n=m/2 and has order b^(m/2), up to constant factors and a geometric sum. Every attachment is finite, so no additional infinite ray appears.

Remaining obstruction

This is not a geodesic Cayley spanning-tree counterexample. It proves that matching sphere counts alone cannot establish the conjectured branching number.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0037  BK disjoint occurrence for weighted uniform spanning trees

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjecture 4.10, printed p.125

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For increasing events A,B on edge subsets, is the probability of their disjoint occurrence under any weighted uniform spanning-tree law at most P(A)P(B)?

Scope

Disjoint witnesses use disjoint edge-coordinate sets. Arbitrary events are excluded; negative association for fixed disjoint coordinates is weaker. Goldman 2023 proves a special class generated by simple points.

P0037-A001 | BK for fixed conjunctions and a nonincreasing-event obstruction

Argument or result

For disjoint fixed edge sets F and H, the spanning-tree determinantal formula and Hadamard-Fischer inequality give P(F union H subset T)<=P(F subset T)P(H subset T). Thus fixed increasing conjunctions obey the desired inequality. The increasing condition matters: on a triangle, A={e in T} and B={f not in T}, e!=f, have disjoint coordinate witnesses but P(A intersection B)=1/3>2/9=P(A)P(B).

Check

There are three equiprobable spanning trees of a triangle, which checks the latter probabilities directly. The determinantal argument is for a positive semidefinite transfer-current kernel.

Remaining obstruction

General increasing disjoint occurrence is a union over adaptive witnesses. A fixed-coordinate negative-association inequality cannot simply be applied after that union.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0038  Transience preserved in Cayley percolation clusters

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjecture 6.44, printed p.216

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Is every infinite Bernoulli percolation cluster on a transient Cayley graph almost surely transient?

Scope

The claim concerns all infinite clusters simultaneously. Nonamenable and Euclidean cases have substantial existing results.

P0038-A001 | Percolation on regular trees

Argument or result

For bond percolation on the (b+1)-regular tree, descendants of a nonroot vertex form a Galton-Watson process with Binomial(b,p) offspring. Conditional on survival when bp>1, its branching number is bp almost surely, so the electrical-network branching-number criterion gives transience.

Check

The root has b+1 possible children but a finite root modification does not change transience. At bp<=1 the nondegenerate branching process dies out.

Remaining obstruction

The reduction uses independent descendant trees and does not extend to clusters with overlapping paths in a general transient Cayley graph.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0039  Positive-speed equivalence for Cayley percolation clusters

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjecture 6.45, printed p.216

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does simple random walk on a Cayley graph have positive speed if and only if simple random walk on its infinite Bernoulli percolation clusters has positive speed almost surely?

Scope

Use graph distance in the cluster for the cluster speed as in the source context; compare with ambient speed carefully. Nonamenable cases are reported known.

P0039-A001 | Zero speed on every bounded-degree subexponential-growth subgraph

Argument or result

Let H be a connected subgraph of a bounded-degree graph G with |B_G(x,n)|=exp(o(n)). The Carne-Varopoulos bound gives P_x(X_n=y)<=2 sqrt(deg_H(y)/deg_H(x)) exp(-d_H(x,y)^2/(2n)). Summing over reachable vertices yields P(d_H(x,X_n)>=epsilon n)<=C exp(o(n)-epsilon^2 n/2), which is summable. Thus the intrinsic speed on H is zero almost surely.

Check

Reachability in H implies membership in B_G(x,n), but distances in the exponential bound remain intrinsic to H. Bounded degrees control the prefactor. This is a consequence of the classical heat-kernel bound, not a novelty claim.

Remaining obstruction

It settles the zero-speed equivalence for subexponential-growth Cayley graphs and their clusters only. Exponential-growth cases need different information.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0040  Uniqueness threshold below one in one-ended transitive graphs

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjecture 7.27, printed p.258

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does every locally finite quasi-transitive graph with exactly one end have Bernoulli percolation uniqueness threshold p_u<1?

Scope

A one-ended finite-presentation class is known. The assertion p_c<1 alone is weaker and now resolved in the transitive setting.

P0040-A001 | Amenable Cayley special case and a cutset bottleneck

Argument or result

For an amenable Cayley graph with one end, uniqueness gives p_u=p_c. The known strict p_c<1 theorem for transitive graphs not roughly isometric to Z therefore gives p_u<1 in this special case. One-endedness excludes the two-ended roughly-Z case.

Check

This use is restricted to Cayley graphs; no unverified extension of the strict-critical-threshold theorem to every quasi-transitive graph is presumed.

Remaining obstruction

The remaining nonamenable quasi-transitive setting needs bounds on separating cutsets or cycle structure. A bounded cycle-basis argument is unavailable without its hypotheses.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0041  Nonuniqueness phase for nonamenable quasi-transitive graphs

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjectures 7.31 and 7.41, printed pp.260 and 265

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Must p_c(G)<p_u(G) for every nonamenable quasi-transitive graph? The source's related formulation asks whether the property p_c<p_u is preserved by rough isometries among quasi-transitive graphs.

Scope

The closely linked rough-isometry conjecture is grouped here to avoid counting a reformulation separately. Nonunimodular and many other cases are established.

P0041-A001 | Strict gap on regular trees

Argument or result

On a (b+1)-regular tree with b>=2, branching-process survival gives p_c=1/b. For every p in (1/b,1), infinitely many separated descendant subtrees contain infinite open clusters while their attachment edge is closed, using independent positive-probability events. Thus there are infinitely many clusters and p_u=1.

Check

The attachment events can be placed on an infinite collection of disjoint descendant cones. For p<=1/b there is no infinite cluster.

Remaining obstruction

This proves the strict gap in a fundamental nonamenable family. Nonamenability by itself does not provide the same independent cone decomposition.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0042  Bootstrap thresholds as a characterization of amenable groups

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 7.42, printed p.266

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Is a finitely generated group amenable exactly when every Cayley graph from every finite generating set has bootstrap complete-occupation critical probability p(G,k) in {0,1} for every threshold k?

Scope

Initially each vertex is occupied independently with probability p; vacant vertices with at least k occupied neighbors become occupied. Criticality is for positive probability of eventual occupation of all vertices.

P0042-A001 | Bootstrap thresholds on the line and the three-regular tree

Argument or result

On Z the one-neighbor bootstrap threshold is zero, whereas the two-neighbor threshold is one because any initially vacant adjacent pair stays vacant. On the three-regular tree with threshold two, the final vacant set is the two-core of the initial vacant percolation. If its density q<=1/2, all vacant clusters are finite almost surely and leaf pruning empties them. If q>1/2, with positive probability a vertex has two surviving vacant branches, giving a bi-infinite vacant path that remains vacant. Hence the complete-occupation threshold is p=1/2.

Check

An infinite bi-infinite path supplies a stable set since each vertex has two vacant neighbors. Finite trees have an exposed leaf and are exhausted by the pruning process. Critical Binomial(2,1/2) clusters die out.

Remaining obstruction

This illustrates both the amenable endpoint behavior and a nonamenable interior threshold. It does not classify every generating set or every group.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0043  Absence of infinite clusters at critical percolation

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjecture 8.15, printed p.285

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

If G is quasi-transitive and p_c(G)<1, is there almost surely no infinite Bernoulli cluster at p_c(G)?

Scope

Do not count each lattice dimension as a separate problem. Known nonamenable and high-dimensional cases do not settle the entire assertion.

P0043-A001 | Critical branching-process extinction

Argument or result

For the regular tree at p=1/b, an infinite descendant cluster would have survival probability x satisfying x=1-(1-px)^b. For b>=2 strict concavity gives 1-(1-x/b)^b<x for every x>0, so only x=0 is possible.

Check

The strict inequality can also be obtained from the binomial generating function lying strictly above its tangent at one. This excludes the deterministic b=1 exception, whose p_c is one.

Remaining obstruction

Critical clusters in a general quasi-transitive graph are not independent branching trees. Their loop dependence is not captured by this fixed-point proof.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0044  Invariant monotone coupling of free and wired uniform spanning forests

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 10.6, printed pp.348-349

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For an infinite network, is there a coupling (W,F) of its wired and free uniform spanning forests with W subset F almost surely whose joint law is invariant under all network automorphisms?

Scope

Strassen gives an unconstrained monotone coupling. Sofic Cayley groups admit invariant couplings by Lyons-Thom 2016; arbitrary invariant percolations can fail this property.

P0044-A001 | Invariant monotone coupling under an amenable action

Argument or result

Start with a monotone coupling of the two invariant forest laws under a countable amenable action. Average its translates over a Folner sequence. The product space of two edge configurations is compact, the prescribed marginals are unchanged, and the order relation is closed. A subsequential weak limit is therefore still a monotone coupling and is invariant because translation changes each average by at most its Folner boundary ratio.

Check

The argument averages probability measures, not individual configurations, so convexity preserves the marginals and the monotone support. Compactness is available for a countable locally finite edge set.

Remaining obstruction

For a nonamenable acting group there is no Folner averaging sequence. Existence of an arbitrary monotone coupling does not by itself produce an invariant one.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0045  Rational expected free-forest degree for finitely presented groups

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 10.11, printed p.351

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For a Cayley graph of a finitely presented group, is the expected degree at the identity in its free uniform spanning forest rational?

Scope

This is the first l2-Betti-number question via E degree=2+2 beta_1. Counterexamples to broader Atiyah statements must be checked for both first degree and finite presentation before exclusion.

P0045-A001 | Rational finite-volume values do not force a rational limit

Argument or result

For the standard free-group Cayley tree the free spanning forest is the entire tree, so its expected degree is the integer 2r. Finite exhaustion spanning-tree expectations are rational under unit conductances, but rational approximants can converge to an irrational number, as floor(n sqrt(2))/n demonstrates.

Check

A finite tree has only one spanning tree, so the free-group calculation is exact and requires no limiting interchange.

Remaining obstruction

The general finitely presented-group question needs the algebraic structure behind the first l2 Betti number; rationality of finite combinatorial counts is insufficient.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0046  Connecting a free uniform spanning forest by arbitrarily sparse invariant additions

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 10.12, printed p.351

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For any Cayley graph, its free uniform spanning forest F, and epsilon>0, is there an invariant connected random spanning subgraph omega containing F with P(e in omega minus F)<epsilon for every edge e?

Scope

Added edges may depend on F. Independent Bernoulli sprinkling is explicitly known to fail for some nonamenable graphs.

P0046-A001 | Connection to the cost equality obstruction

Argument or result

If the free forest is already connected, taking its own configuration gives the requested extension with no added edges. Otherwise, an invariant connected extension with each edge added with probability at most epsilon would have expected root degree at most E deg_F(o)+deg_G(o) epsilon. The standard invariant-graphing bound would then imply cost(Gamma)<=E deg_F(o)/2=1+beta_1(Gamma) after epsilon tends to zero.

Check

The root-degree estimate is the finite sum of per-edge addition probabilities. Combined with the known opposite cost inequality, the proposed construction would prove equality, not merely a weak approximation statement.

Remaining obstruction

No dependent sprinkling construction was found. This reduction explains why independent sprinkling or a generic connectivity argument does not settle the question.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0047  Deterministic component count of the free uniform spanning forest

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 10.28, printed p.359

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

On every infinite connected locally finite network, is the number of free uniform spanning-forest components almost surely a constant?

Scope

Automorphisms with an infinite orbit give a positive answer. The remaining arbitrary-network setting lacks that ergodic shortcut.

P0047-A001 | Why tail triviality alone does not fix the component count

Argument or result

An ergodic action under which the forest law is invariant makes its component count deterministic. On an arbitrary network there need not be such an action. Trying to substitute tail triviality fails at the logical step: adding one edge between two components can change the component count, so this statistic has not been shown to be measurable with respect to the edge tail sigma-field.

Check

The finite edge change can preserve acyclicity when it joins two different tree components, so acyclicity does not remove this obstruction.

Remaining obstruction

A forest-law-specific argument making the count constant is still needed in the absence of a useful invariant ergodic action.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0048  One or infinitely many free-forest components without unimodularity

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 10.29, printed p.360

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

On every transitive network, is the free uniform spanning forest almost surely connected or composed of infinitely many components?

Scope

The unimodular case was solved by Hutchcroft-Nachmias 2017 and Timar 2018. The unresolved scope retained here is nonunimodular.

P0048-A001 | Invariant ergodicity permits a finite component count larger than one

Argument or result

On the Cayley graph of Z with generators +/-1 and +/-2, retain exactly the edges of length two. The resulting deterministic spanning forest has two bi-infinite components, is translation invariant, and its point-mass law is ergodic. Thus those two properties alone cannot exclude a finite count of two.

Check

The two components are the even and odd integers; every retained vertex has degree two and neither component contains a cycle.

Remaining obstruction

This forest is not asserted to have a uniform-spanning-forest law. The target requires extra free-forest structure in the nonunimodular case.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0049  Infinitely many ends in all free-forest components without unimodularity

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 10.52, printed p.377

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

On a transitive network with distinct free and wired uniform spanning-forest laws, must every free-forest component have infinitely many ends almost surely?

Scope

The source's subsequent indistinguishability theorem settles the unimodular case. Haar-light components alone do not automatically refute an ends statement.

P0049-A001 | Missing mass-transport step in the nonunimodular case

Argument or result

In the unimodular setting, an expected-degree argument can establish the presence of infinitely ended components, and indistinguishability can promote a component property to all components. On a nonunimodular graph the unweighted root mass balance used in that argument is invalid; modular weights enter and light components need not receive the same balance.

Check

An existence statement about some components would remain weaker than the source assertion about every component even if the first step survived.

Remaining obstruction

No replacement weighted transport and no counterexample with the required FUSF/WUSF inequality were obtained. This is a failed proof route, not a new theorem.

P0050  Singularity of distinct free and wired forest measures

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 10.58, printed p.385

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

If the wired and free uniform spanning-forest measures differ on an infinite network, must they be mutually singular?

Scope

Known for trees and networks with an infinite automorphism orbit. Distinct probability measures need not be singular in general.

P0050-A001 | Distinct invariant ergodic laws are mutually singular

Argument or result

For a fixed countable group action, two distinct invariant ergodic probability measures are mutually singular. In the Lebesgue decomposition of one relative to the other, the absolutely continuous part is invariant. Its density is invariant and hence constant under the second ergodic law; ergodicity of the first forces its absolutely continuous mass to be either zero or one. Nonzero overlap therefore forces equality.

Check

The singular and absolutely continuous parts are preserved by the action by uniqueness of Lebesgue decomposition; this is the load-bearing invariance step.

Remaining obstruction

This handles forest laws admitting such an ergodic common action. Arbitrary networks may lack it; two distinct probability laws need not be singular in general.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0051  Zero-one law for all forest components having one end

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 10.59, printed p.386

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For both free and wired uniform spanning forests of an arbitrary infinite network, is the event that every component has one end of probability zero or one?

Scope

Both boundary conditions are grouped. Changing finitely many forest edges can merge components, so tail measurability needs a proof.

P0051-A001 | The all-one-ended event is not automatically a tail event

Argument or result

Take two disjoint one-ended infinite trees and add one edge joining them. Before the addition every component has one end; afterwards the connected tree has two ends, since deleting the joining edge separates two infinite pieces.

Check

The added edge creates no cycle and the change is finite. Thus the property can change within the class of spanning forests under a finite edge modification.

Remaining obstruction

Uniform-forest tail triviality cannot be invoked until a separate tail representation of the event is proved. The example is a logical obstruction, not a uniform-forest counterexample.
