P0052  Limiting bushes along a wired uniform-forest ray

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjecture 10.60, printed p.386

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Along the unique ray from the identity in its wired uniform spanning-forest component, do the finite bushes at successive ray vertices, translated back to the identity, converge in distribution?

Scope

Restrict to Cayley graphs where the stated unique ray exists. The source records solutions for growth at least five and standard Z^2,Z^3,Z^4; the remaining low-growth Cayley cases must be checked individually before any promotion.

P0052-A001 | Stationarity along a chosen spine is an additional requirement

Argument or result

The WUSF viewed from an ordinary invariant root and the WUSF viewed from the nth vertex on its distinguished spine are different rooted laws. Moving along the spine is a configuration-dependent selection and need not preserve the original root law. Compactness or tightness, even if proved for the bush sequence, would only give subsequential limits.

Check

The elementary sequence of probability laws delta_0,delta_1,delta_0,delta_1 is tight but has no full limit, illustrating precisely the missing implication rather than modeling WUSF bushes.

Remaining obstruction

Need regeneration or asymptotic stationarity along the loop-erased spine. Known high-growth and standard-lattice cases in the source must be removed before claiming a new remaining case.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0053  One-ended wired minimal forests without unimodularity

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 11.13, printed p.401

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does each wired minimal spanning-forest component of a nonunimodular transitive graph have one end almost surely?

Scope

The accompanying request for useful nonhomogeneous sufficient conditions is retained as a related extension, not another count.

P0053-A001 | A failed largest-edge argument on an infinite path

Argument or result

A finite-cycle minimum-spanning-tree proof deletes the largest edge of a cycle. A proposed extension to a bi-infinite path fails because its supremum need not be attained: labels 1-1/(|k|+2) along k in Z approach one and have no maximum.

Check

The deterministic sequence only disproves the logical inference that every infinite path has a largest label. It is not an iid weighted-graph realization or a counterexample to the wired forest question.

Remaining obstruction

One-endedness needs an invasion-percolation or mass-transport argument that treats unattained suprema and the nonunimodular setting.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0054  Infinitely many ends in free minimal forests distinct from wired

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjecture 11.16, printed p.402

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

If a quasi-transitive graph has distinct wired and free minimal spanning-forest laws, does every free minimal-forest component have infinitely many ends almost surely?

Scope

The source and later Timar results establish unimodular cases; retain the genuinely remaining scope. All edges have independent continuous labels.

P0054-A001 | Regular-tree special case and resampling obstruction

Argument or result

On a regular tree every edge is a bridge and there are no finite cycles, so the free minimal spanning forest is the whole tree. It has infinitely many ends, providing a direct special case where free and wired forests differ. On a graph with cycles, inserting an edge can force deletion of another edge by the cycle-maximum rule.

Check

The free forest statement follows directly from its finite-cycle definition; it is not the wired definition, which also sees connections at infinity.

Remaining obstruction

A minimal forest is not a Bernoulli insertion-tolerant process. Arguments proving end properties for percolation do not transfer without controlling the forced deletions.

P0055  Minimal-forest connectivity and uniqueness at p_u

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjecture 11.18, printed p.403

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

On a nonamenable quasi-transitive graph with p_u<1, is the free minimal spanning forest connected almost surely exactly when Bernoulli percolation has a unique infinite cluster at p_u?

Scope

The source's stronger bijection between forest components and p_u clusters is grouped as a strengthening. One direction is already proved there.

P0055-A001 | The easy direction of the uniqueness connection

Argument or result

Under the p_u>p_c hypothesis in the source proposition, each FMSF component meets exactly one infinite p_u cluster. Consequently a connected FMSF can meet only one such cluster, so connectivity implies uniqueness at p_u in that setting.

Check

The condition p_u>p_c is retained; assuming it for every nonamenable graph would silently solve another admitted conjecture. The proposition does not say that one p_u cluster meets only one forest component.

Remaining obstruction

The reverse direction needs to exclude several free-forest components meeting the same infinite p_u cluster. The stronger component-bijection formulation remains unproved here.

P0056  One or infinitely many minimal-forest components

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 11.19, printed p.403

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Must both free and wired minimal spanning forests on a quasi-transitive graph have either one or infinitely many components almost surely?

Scope

Free and wired are grouped. Distinct-law unimodular cases are reported resolved by Timar 2018.

P0056-A001 | Ergodic factor laws give constancy but not the value of the count

Argument or result

Minimal spanning forests are equivariant functions of iid continuous edge labels, so an ergodic label action makes their component count constant. That alone permits a finite constant larger than one: the invariant deterministic two-component forest on Z with generators +/-1,+/-2 from P0048 is a counterexample to the purely ergodic inference.

Check

The example is explicitly not a minimal-spanning-forest law. It separates the zero-one statement for invariant events from a classification of possible constant values.

Remaining obstruction

The usual insertion-tolerance route must account for cycle-breaking deletions. No argument excluding a finite count between two and infinity was obtained in the full quasi-transitive scope.

P0057  Uniqueness-critical percolation versus invariant spanning trees

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 11.31, printed p.408

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For a Cayley graph with p_u<1, is there an implication in either direction between multiple infinite clusters at p_u and the nonexistence of an invariant random spanning tree?

Scope

Each condition implies the free minimal spanning forest is disconnected, but a common consequence does not establish either implication.

P0057-A001 | Conditional implication through the component-bijection conjecture

Argument or result

If there is no invariant random spanning tree, the invariant FMSF cannot be connected, because a connected spanning forest would itself be such a tree. If the stronger conjecture in P0055 gave a bijection between free-forest components and infinite p_u clusters, that disconnection would imply multiple clusters at p_u.

Check

The first implication uses only the definition of an invariant spanning tree. The second is explicitly conditional on a separate unproved component correspondence.

Remaining obstruction

Two properties sharing FMSF disconnection as a consequence do not thereby imply one another. Neither unconditional implication in the source question is established here.

P0058  Indistinguishability of minimal spanning-forest components

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjecture 11.33, printed p.409

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Are the components of both free and wired minimal spanning forests on a unimodular transitive graph indistinguishable by every invariant Borel component property?

Scope

Timar 2018 settles the free case when free and wired differ. The remaining equal-law or wired scope is the target.

P0058-A001 | Invariant ergodicity does not imply component indistinguishability

Argument or result

Construct a periodic forest on Z^2 using blocks of four horizontal rows. The first row is a bi-infinite horizontal path. The next three rows form a comb: the middle row is a horizontal spine, with each vertex in the two outer rows attached vertically to that spine. Repeat and choose a uniformly random vertical phase. The law is translation invariant and ergodic on its finite phase orbit, yet its components are distinguishable as lines and combs by the existence of degree-four vertices.

Check

Every vertex belongs to exactly one component and there are no cycles. If full square-lattice automorphism invariance is desired, also choose a uniform horizontal/vertical orientation; the full automorphism group is transitive on the resulting finite orbit.

Remaining obstruction

This is not a minimal-spanning-forest construction. It disproves only the shortcut from invariance and ergodicity to indistinguishability.

P0059  Limiting bushes along a wired minimal-forest ray

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Question

For a Cayley graph not roughly isometric to Z, does the sequence of translated bushes along a ray in the identity's wired minimal spanning-forest component converge in distribution?

Scope

Uniqueness of that ray is itself tied to critical percolation. Keep the conditional structural assumptions explicit rather than selecting an unmentioned canonical ray.

P0059-A001 | Unique-ray and limiting-law issues must be separated

Argument or result

A bush decomposition first needs an almost surely unique infinite ray in the relevant component. Assuming that ray before proving the applicable one-endedness result would import another open assertion. Even when uniqueness is available, rooting successively along the ray is a configuration-dependent operation and does not automatically preserve the original rooted law.

Check

A convergent subsequence of bush laws would not establish a full limit. The period-two sequence example in P0052 shows the logical distinction.

Remaining obstruction

No regeneration theorem or coupling proving convergence along the wired minimal-forest ray was obtained. The preliminary one-endedness assumptions must be checked for each claimed case.

P0060  Dimensions with a connected Euclidean minimal spanning forest

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 11.35, printed p.409

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For which dimensions d is the minimal spanning forest on Z^d connected almost surely?

Scope

Competing predictions put a transition near dimension six or eight. These dimension variants form one classification problem.

P0060-A001 | Exact one-dimensional connectivity

Argument or result

On Z every edge is a bridge, so the free minimal spanning forest retains all edges. For an edge with label u<1 to be excluded by a wired connection at infinity, both infinite half-lines would have to supply paths of edges with labels below u. Each such event has probability zero for iid uniform labels, since the probability for its first N edges is u^N. Thus the wired forest also retains every edge almost surely.

Check

There are only countably many edges, allowing the probability-one statement to hold simultaneously for all of them. The argument uses the unique paths on Z.

Remaining obstruction

The higher-dimensional connectivity classification depends on intersections of invasion structures and is not determined by the one-dimensional calculation.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0061  Full scaling limit of the square-lattice minimal spanning tree

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 11.36, printed p.410

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does the minimal spanning tree on epsilon Z^2 have a full distributional scaling limit as epsilon tends to zero, in the embedded-tree topology used for its known subsequential limits?

Scope

The source leaves the exact topology to the cited Aizenman-Burchard-Newman-Wilson framework. A triangular-lattice theorem does not settle square-lattice universality.

P0061-A001 | Exact universality under a monotone change of edge labels

Argument or result

On every finite graph with distinct edge weights, a strictly increasing transform preserves their order and hence preserves Kruskal selection and the minimum spanning tree. Independently, the cycle characterization retains exactly the same nonmaximal edges under that transform. For iid continuous distributions this identifies the law through ranks, or through the probability integral transform almost surely.

Check

For a continuous CDF that is not strictly increasing everywhere, flat intervals have zero probability of a sampled weight, so the rank argument still applies almost surely.

Remaining obstruction

This is label-distribution universality on a fixed graph. It does not compare triangular and square lattice geometries or prove tightness and uniqueness of a continuum scaling limit.

P0062  Connectivity of minimal forests on graphs roughly isometric to trees

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 11.37, printed p.410

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

If a connected locally finite graph is roughly isometric to a tree, must its free minimal spanning forest be connected almost surely?

Scope

This concerns minimal forests, not uniform forests; Pete-Timar's uniform-forest counterexample cannot be substituted.

P0062-A001 | Connected FMSF on trees of finite cycle blocks

Argument or result

Suppose an infinite cactus graph is built from bridges and edge-disjoint finite cycles. Its free minimal spanning forest retains every bridge and removes exactly the unique largest-weight edge of each cycle. Each resulting cycle block remains connected. A finite block path between any two vertices remains connected after these deletions, so the forest is globally connected and acyclic.

Check

A simple cycle in a cactus is one of its cycle blocks; there are no additional cycle constraints coupling two blocks. Continuous iid labels make every finite maximum unique almost surely.

Remaining obstruction

Uniformly bounded block diameters give a class roughly isometric to trees. General roughly-tree graphs can have overlapping cycles and need not admit this block decomposition.

P0063  Singularity of binary and ternary harmonic codings of the Cantor set

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 16.7, printed p.542

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For the middle-thirds Cantor set E, let nu_b be harmonic measure on the source's base-b coding tree T[b](E), pushed to E. Is nu_2 mutually singular with the Cantor-Lebesgue measure nu_3?

Scope

Use the coding-tree construction and boundary map in Section 16.2; distinct harmonic measures or dimensions alone do not prove singularity.

P0063-A001 | Likelihood-ratio strategy and its exact missing input

Argument or result

For product Bernoulli measures with different parameters, the strong law gives disjoint full-measure sets of limiting digit frequencies and hence mutual singularity. A possible analogous strategy for the two Cantor measures is a likelihood-ratio martingale along a common binary coding, with cumulative conditional relative entropy forcing its limit to vanish.

Check

Merely proving that two measures are unequal is insufficient: two different positive bounded densities relative to the same measure are still equivalent. The binary digits of Cantor-Lebesgue measure are not assumed iid.

Remaining obstruction

No divergent conditional-entropy estimate or martingale tail control was established for the actual harmonic measure versus base-three Cantor measure.

P0064  Dimension drop for every nonuniform Galton-Watson flow

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Conjecture 17.23, printed p.580

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For a nondegenerate supercritical Galton-Watson law of mean m under the chapter's moment assumptions, must every consistent equivariant flow rule different almost surely from UNIF have boundary-measure Hausdorff dimension below log m almost surely?

Scope

The source proves a stationary-measure sufficient condition. Difference on a GW-null set is not an admissible counterexample.

P0064-A001 | Exact dimension drop for the nonergodic same-color flow candidate

Argument or result

For the AND/OR color construction of P0033, let K be the number of children of the parent color and choose uniformly among them. Conditional on root color zero, P(K=1,2,3)=(3/5,3/10,1/10); conditional on color one it is (3/10,3/5,1/10). The full conditional-child selection lemma implies these K variables are iid along the selected ray within each color class. Hence the local dimension is h_0=(3/10)log 2+(1/10)log 3 or h_1=(3/5)log 2+(1/10)log 3 by the strong law. Both are strictly less than log(12/5), the Galton-Watson dimension.

Check

For color zero the unconditioned masses are .30 for two mixed children (K=1), .15 for two zero children (K=2), and .05 for three zero children (K=3); divide by color probability .5. For color one the corresponding masses are .15,.30,.05. Cylinder mass is exactly the product of reciprocal K values; Fubini transfers the ray statement to almost every tree and almost every flow ray.

Remaining obstruction

This author-checked special construction supports dimension drop and is not a counterexample to the full question. It inherits the P0033 coloring proof and remains without independent review.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0065  Monotonicity of harmonic dimension under inward bias

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 17.29, printed p.582

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For a nondegenerate supercritical Galton-Watson tree without leaves and 0<=lambda<m, does harmonic-measure dimension of RW_lambda increase with lambda, and is the increase strict?

Scope

On a deterministic regular tree the dimension is constant, so retain the chapter's nondegeneracy assumptions for strictness. Both monotonic and strict clauses are grouped.

P0065-A001 | Regular-tree equality and a local-entropy countercheck

Argument or result

On a deterministic b-ary tree, symmetry makes harmonic measure uniform on children for every transient bias lambda<b, so its dimension is log b, independent of lambda. Thus strict monotonicity must exclude deterministic offspring. A local root-entropy proof also fails: attach regular trees of branching 2 and 4 as two root subtrees. Their escape probabilities are 1-lambda/2 and 1-lambda/4 for lambda<2; last-exit child probabilities are proportional to these values and become more unequal as lambda increases toward 2, so their entropy decreases.

Check

The two-child example is not an iid Galton-Watson tree. Its role is to disprove a pointwise local entropy monotonicity claim, not the global dimension conjecture.

Remaining obstruction

Global dimension averages local entropy under a bias-dependent law of the environment seen along the ray. Controlling that changing law remains necessary.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0066  Monotonicity of biased-walk speed on Galton-Watson trees

Source: Probability on Trees and Networks, paperback electronic version 19 August 2026 | Question 17.33, printed p.592

https://rdlyons.pages.iu.edu/prbtree/book_pb.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For Galton-Watson trees with p_0=0, is the speed of RW_lambda nonincreasing as the bias parameter lambda increases?

Scope

The transient range is 0<lambda<m; beyond criticality the speed is zero. Positive-regeneration and large-bias partial results are not the complete monotonicity claim.

P0066-A001 | The changing stationary environment blocks a pointwise drift proof

Argument or result

For fixed offspring D, the radial drift (D-lambda)/(D+lambda) decreases with lambda, and on a regular tree it gives the exact speed. In a nondegenerate Galton-Watson tree the stationary distribution of the environment seen by the walker also changes with lambda, so differentiating the speed has an additional measure-change term.

Check

An elementary comparison shows the logical gap: drift at lambda=1,D=2 is 1/3, while at lambda=2,D=100 it is 98/102. Pointwise monotonicity in lambda cannot compare averages taken under different offspring laws.

Remaining obstruction

The example is not a Galton-Watson speed counterexample. No sign bound for the measure-change term, or alternative monotone coupling of speeds, was proved.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0067  Global Kuramoto synchronization of random cubic graphs

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2024 | Conjecture3, PDF p.3

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.20539v1

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Is a uniformly random simple 3-regular graph globally synchronizing with probability tending to one? Global synchronization means that every local minimum of sum_(ij in E)(1-cos(theta_i-theta_j)) is a consensus configuration.

Scope

Use even n. Connectivity is necessary but not sufficient; degree-three deterministic examples do not refute an asymptotic random-graph assertion.

P0067-A001 | Global Kuramoto synchronization of random cubic graphs special-case or structural calculation

Argument or result

On the cubic prism C_m times K_2, m>=5, put theta_(j,a)=2 pi j/m for both layers. The sine forces along the two cycle neighbors cancel and rung forces vanish. The Hessian is a weighted Laplacian with cycle weights cos(2 pi/m)>0 and rung weights1, so it is positive definite modulo the global rotation. This is a nonconsensus strict local minimum modulo rotation.

Check

Both the vanishing gradient and Hessian signs were checked; the graph is connected and cubic. This is an exact deterministic obstruction to a connectivity-only proof.

Remaining obstruction

Must show that uniformly random cubic graphs avoid all such stable phase arrangements with probability tending to one; the prism family itself has vanishing probability.

P0068  Subspectral detection from a growing number of synchronization frequencies

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2024 | OpenProblem7, PDF p.7

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.20539v1

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For uniform U(1) signal x in C^n with |x_i|=1 and independent Wigner noises, observe Y_k=(lambda/n)x^(k)(x^(k))*+W_k/sqrt(n), k=1,...,L_n. Find a scaling of distinct frequencies L_n for which a polynomial-time algorithm reliably detects signal at some fixed lambda<1.

Scope

The source also names finite groups. For a fixed finite group the number of distinct irreducible frequencies is bounded; a growing group or repeated-observation convention must be specified. The unambiguous U(1) case is the admitted target.

P0068-A001 | Subspectral detection from a growing number of synchronization frequencies special-case or structural calculation

Argument or result

If L independent observations repeated the same rank-one signal, averaging would divide noise standard deviation by sqrt(L) and reduce the effective spectral threshold. In the actual model the vectors x^(k) differ with k, so averaging Y_k yields a sum of different rank-one projectors. It is not a repeated observation of xx*.

Check

The noise-average variance calculation is exact. The signal identity fails already for phases whose squares are not a common multiple of their first powers.

Remaining obstruction

Need an algorithm exploiting algebraic relations between distinct powers. Repetition-based variance reduction solves a different experiment.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0069  Subexponential detection at fixed synchronization frequencies

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2024 | OpenProblem8, PDF p.7

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.20539v1

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

In the same multifrequency U(1) or specified finite-group Gaussian synchronization model, fix L independently of n. For signal strength between its statistical threshold and1, is reliable detection possible in time exp(n^delta) for some delta<1?

Scope

The statistical threshold and group must refer to the fixed model. This is a time-accuracy question distinct from growing the observation model in OpenProblem7.

P0069-A001 | Subexponential detection at fixed synchronization frequencies special-case or structural calculation

Argument or result

Try enumerating an oracle seed of m=o(n) signal coordinates and use their edges to estimate a new coordinate in one channel. After multiplying by known seed phases, the sum has signal amplitude lambda m/n and noise standard deviation sqrt(m/n), so signal-to-noise ratio is lambda sqrt(m/n), which tends to zero.

Check

The calculation uses the displayed matrix normalization; using unnormalized Wigner noise gives the same ratio. An oracle seed is stronger than an enumerated guessed seed.

Remaining obstruction

This only defeats a one-step seed-and-average method. Iterative amplification or a different subexponential algorithm is not ruled out.

P0070  Kikuchi spectral thresholds as the polynomial degree increases

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2024 | Conjecture9, PDF p.8

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.20539v1

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For even tensor order r and fixed l>=r/2, index M by l-subsets of [n] and set M_IJ=lambda+Z_(I symmetric_difference J) when that difference has size r, and zero otherwise, with independent standard Gaussian Z_S. Does the normalized spectral-outlier threshold n^(r/4) lambda_critical(r,l) tend to zero as l tends to infinity after n tends to infinity?

Scope

The source suppresses the n-dependence of its threshold notation. Its literal inclusion of r=2 needs a separate check against matrix-PCA limits; no silent restriction to r>=4 is made and no r=2 counterexample is yet claimed.

P0070-A001 | Kikuchi spectral thresholds as the polynomial degree increases special-case or structural calculation

Argument or result

Each row has k=binomial(l,r/2)binomial(n-l,r/2) nonzero entries. For the normalized all-ones vector v, v*M(lambda)*v=lambda k+(k/binomial(n,r))sum_(|S|=r)Z_S. Thus its noise variance is k^2/binomial(n,r). Write M(0)=sum_S Z_S A_S; A_S^2 is the diagonal indicator of |I intersection S|=r/2, hence sum_S A_S^2=k I.

Check

Double-counting pairs (I,J) with symmetric difference S gives the Rayleigh coefficient. Squaring A_S toggles the same subset S twice and returns I exactly when that toggle preserves cardinality.

Remaining obstruction

A Gaussian matrix-series bound introduces sqrt(log binomial(n,l)) into the norm estimate. Removing that loss and controlling the actual outlier transition remain necessary; the r=2 scope caveat needs resolution.

P0071  Polynomial Glauber mixing throughout the SK high-temperature regime

Source: Randomstrasse101: Open Problems of 2024 | OpenProblem15, PDF p.12

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.20539v1

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For the Sherrington-Kirkpatrick Ising model with arbitrary external field, does single-site heat-bath Glauber dynamics mix in polynomial total-variation time for every fixed beta<1, with high probability over the Gaussian disorder?

Scope

Keep SK Hamiltonian normalization consistent. A July2026 primary paper proves results for sufficiently large external fields, and an August2026 preprint claims optimal mixing for beta<1/2 uniformly over fields; neither states the full beta<1 arbitrary-field conclusion.

Dated literature checks

[{"finding": "Large-field result, not arbitrary-field full regime", "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06813"}, {"finding": "Preprint claims beta<1/2 uniform fields; full beta<1 remainder not resolved by its statement", "url": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22159"}]

P0071-A001 | Polynomial Glauber mixing throughout the SK high-temperature regime special-case or structural calculation

Argument or result

At beta=0, spins are independent under the target product law. Couple two random-scan heat-bath chains using the same update coordinate and uniform draw. Once a coordinate has been updated it agrees forever. The probability some coordinate remains unupdated by t is at most n(1-1/n)^t<=n exp(-t/n), giving mixing time at most n log(n/epsilon) for every external field.

Check

The update probability of each spin may depend on its field, but at beta=0 it does not depend on neighboring spins, so agreement is preserved. This is a complete endpoint proof, with no novelty claim.

Remaining obstruction

For beta>0 a later update can destroy agreement through neighboring disagreements. The beta=0 coupon-collector argument does not control the whole replica-symmetric interval.

P0072  Geodesic selection by reinforced ants

Source: Aletti et al.: Open problems in stochastic reinforcement models; March2023 workshop, published2025 | Section2, main conjecture, PDF pp.1-2

https://www.matrix-inst.org.au/wp_Matrix2016/wp-content/uploads/2023/Aletti.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

On a finite graph with nest N and food F, ants take edge-weighted random walks until hitting F. Reinforce either a uniformly chosen shortest N-F path in the visited trace, or the loop erasure of the reversed walk. Are all edges whose weights grow linearly contained in shortest N-F paths of the original graph?

Scope

The two reinforcement algorithms form one conjecture family here. The source proves series-parallel and losange cases, not all graphs.

P0072-A001 | Geodesic selection by reinforced ants structural argument

Argument or result

On a finite tree with one food vertex, every N-F walk has the same simple path after loop erasure, and the shortest path in its trace is that path too. Each edge of that path therefore receives exactly one increment per ant; every other edge receives zero. The normalized weights converge to its indicator without a probabilistic limit theorem.

Check

Uniqueness of the simple path verifies both algorithms separately. This endpoint is included in known special cases, not a new solution.

Remaining obstruction

Cycles permit different simple paths and histories affect their weights. The deterministic tree reduction gives no control of their competition.

P0073  Deterministic limits for trace-reinforced ants

Source: Aletti et al.: Open problems in stochastic reinforcement models; March2023 workshop, published2025 | OpenProblems1-2, PDF p.2; underlying Kious-Mailler-Schapira Conjecture1.1

https://www.matrix-inst.org.au/wp_Matrix2016/wp-content/uploads/2023/Aletti.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

When each ant reinforces every edge it visited at least once, do all edge weights divided by the number of ants converge almost surely to deterministic constants on general finite graphs, and does an appropriate edgewise statement hold on Z^2?

Scope

The finite and Z^2 versions are grouped. The underlying paper excludes multiple edges incident to food; the brief workshop statement omits this load-bearing restriction. Its wording main conjecture is interpreted using the immediately preceding trace-process paragraph.

P0073-A001 | Deterministic limits for trace-reinforced ants structural argument

Argument or result

If two parallel edges connect N directly to F, an ant crosses exactly one and reinforces it. Starting at weights a,b gives the classical Polya urn: the first normalized weight has Beta(a,b) limit with variance ab/((a+b)^2(a+b+1))>0. Thus the unrestricted multigraph formulation is false, and the underlying no-parallel-food assumption is necessary.

Check

The transition probability and one-increment update coincide exactly with the urn. This is a scope counterexample, not a refutation under the original restriction.

Remaining obstruction

For simple finite graphs the nonlinear trace-hitting probabilities still require a uniqueness/attraction argument. On Z^2, changing conductances additionally complicate the guarantee that every ant reaches food.

P0074  Maximal degree and its location in the infinite-path fitness regime

Source: Aletti et al.: Open problems in stochastic reinforcement models; March2023 workshop, published2025 | OpenProblem6, PDF p.5

https://www.matrix-inst.org.au/wp_Matrix2016/wp-content/uploads/2023/Aletti.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For superlinear preferential attachment with iid positive fitness and attachment f(k,w)=w k^p or k^p+w, determine the growth of the maximum degree and the label attaining it in the regime where the limiting tree is locally finite and has one infinite ray.

Scope

Use a positive initial-degree convention; the displayed one-vertex degree-zero multiplicative formula otherwise has a zero denominator. The source model must be initialized consistently.

P0074-A001 | Maximal degree and its location in the infinite-path fitness regime structural argument

Argument or result

Let Delta_n be the maximum degree and I_n a maximizing label. If Delta_n tends to infinity and each fixed vertex has finite limiting degree, then I_n tends to infinity: for every fixed K, the maximum of the K finite limiting degrees is finite, and eventually Delta_n exceeds it.

Check

This is a pathwise implication and does not assume independence. It deliberately leaves divergence of Delta_n as an extra hypothesis.

Remaining obstruction

Neither the rate of Delta_n nor even its divergence follows from local finiteness alone. A deterministic infinite path has maximum degree2.

Question

Determine the scaling and limiting empirical degree distribution of the superlinear preferential-attachment tree with fitness described in Section3, including the infinite-path regime.

Scope

A degree-count measure and a fitness-degree joint measure are different objects; the admitted target is the empirical degree distribution explicitly named in the source.

P0075-A001 | Empirical degree scaling for superlinear attachment with fitness structural argument

Argument or result

For every n-vertex tree, the empirical degree law pi_n satisfies sum_k k pi_n(k)=2-2/n, hence pi_n([K,infinity))<=2/K. Thus the unscaled empirical laws are tight on the positive integers. Any weak subsequential limit is a probability law with mean at most2; its mean can be smaller because degrees are not uniformly integrable.

Check

The handshaking identity proves the first statement. A star has empirical law tending to delta_1 while its empirical mean tends to2, confirming the moment caveat.

Remaining obstruction

Tightness supplies no unique limit or model-specific tail scaling. Escape of the first moment must be tracked separately.

Scope

Sampling with or without replacement and sequential updating must be fixed; the source proposes a multigraph extension. One family is counted for all m, rather than parameter copies.

P0076-A001 | Multiple-attachment phase transition with superlinear fitness structural argument

Argument or result

After n arrivals with exactly m outgoing attachments each, total degree is 2m(n-1) up to the finite seed. Consequently no collection of vertices can each have degree fraction greater than a if it contains more than 2m/a vertices. This bounds the number of macroscopic hubs but permits several of them, unlike a single-star conclusion.

Check

The bound follows by summing nonnegative degrees; it remains valid with parallel edges and sequential updates.

Remaining obstruction

A tree embedding into independent genealogical birth processes is lost when a vertex has several parents. The degree budget does not identify the phase boundary.

Question

Let a new positive value be sampled from mu with probability beta, and otherwise copy a previous value with probability proportional to value times multiplicity. With ess sup mu=1 and mu({1})=0, find the fluctuation limit of sum_(i<=n)(X_i-lambda_star), where lambda_star is the Section4 law-of-large-numbers constant.

Scope

The source equation beta integral x/(lambda_star-(1-beta)x) dmu=1 defines lambda_star when possible; otherwise lambda_star=1-beta. A universal square-root normalization is not assumed.

P0077-A001 | Fluctuations of fitness-reinforced sequence sums structural argument

Argument or result

Writing S_n=sum X_i and Q_n=sum X_i^2 gives E[X_(n+1)|F_n]=beta m_1+(1-beta)Q_n/S_n. Subtracting this predictable mean yields bounded martingale differences, but the centered sum also contains sum[beta m_1+(1-beta)Q_i/S_i-lambda_star].

Check

The conditional mean is obtained by summing x times its copying probability. Boundedness controls the martingale part without controlling the accumulated drift.

Remaining obstruction

A martingale CLT alone cannot settle the question: fluctuations of Q_n/S_n and the slowly relaxing drift may contribute at the same or larger scale.

Convergence against compactly supported test functions does not by itself identify total condensate mass; mass at zero or infinity must be separately controlled.

Argument or result

Let rho=1-beta integral (1-x)^(-1)dmu be the mass missing from the bulk limit. If the claimed rescaled measure is tight on (0,infinity) and carries all that missing mass, integrating its density forces c=rho/Gamma(alpha+1). Without those two additional controls this identification is invalid.

The integral of e^(-x)x^alpha equals Gamma(alpha+1); the deduction is explicitly conditional on no escaped mass.

The source provides only vague convergence away from the boundary. Finding a_n and proving boundary tightness are the load-bearing missing steps.

No single rate can be asserted for every unbounded mu without describing its tail; the question is a distribution-dependent classification.

P0079-A001 | Growth of sums with unbounded reinforcement fitness structural argument

Argument or result

Let K_n count innovation draws and let Y_j be their iid values. Every innovation appears at least once, so S_n>=sum_(j<=K_n)Y_j and S_n>=max_(j<=K_n)Y_j, while K_n/n tends to beta almost surely. If E_mu Y=infinity, the truncated strong law and then monotone convergence give S_n/n tending to infinity from this lower bound alone.

For a truncation L, divide the innovation sum by n and use the iid strong law; let L increase afterward.

Finite-mean unbounded mu requires amplification by reinforcement; innovation lower bounds alone remain linear and cannot give the requested rate.

Question

For a bounded-degree infinite graph, bounded positive vertex firing rates, and WARM exponent 0<alpha<1, establish convergence of every normalized edge count to the intended unique positive equilibrium.

Scope

The source literally says unique equilibrium while allowing nonnegative coordinates. Boundary equilibria make that literal uniqueness false; the meaningful positive-attractor formulation is recorded explicitly, not silently substituted.

P0080-A001 | Weak reinforcement on infinite WARM networks structural argument

Argument or result

On Z with all firing rates1, set edge values alternately2 and0. At each vertex the only positive incident edge is selected with probability1; its two endpoints supply total rate2, while zero edges get rate0. This is an equilibrium for every alpha>0. Shifting gives a second one, and the uniform edge value1 is a third. Thus uniqueness among all nonnegative equilibria is false.

Check

All denominators equal2^alpha, so the example has no undefined 0/0 term. It does not contradict convergence from strictly positive initial counts.

Remaining obstruction

The positive-equilibrium uniqueness and stochastic attraction problem on general infinite graphs remains; no finite total Lyapunov function is available.

For WARM on Z with alpha=1 and all vertex firing rates1, prove that each edge count divided by time converges almost surely to1.

Scope

Keep the stochastic count process distinct from its deterministic equilibrium equation.

P0081-A001 | Critical WARM on the integer line structural argument

Argument or result

For any a,b>0 with a+b=2, the alternating edge configuration a,b,a,b,... solves the equilibrium equation: an a-edge receives a/(a+b) from each endpoint, totaling a, and similarly for b. Thus the ODE has a continuum of positive equilibria on the same graph.

Check

Substitution in the local rate formula verifies every edge. This extends the source even-cycle observation but concerns deterministic equilibria only.

Remaining obstruction

Convergence to1 cannot follow merely from uniqueness of ODE equilibria, which is false. One must exploit stochastic dynamics or spatial structure to select the uniform equilibrium.

P0082  Sharp reinforcement threshold for WARM forests

Source: Aletti et al.: Open problems in stochastic reinforcement models; March2023 workshop, published2025 | OpenProblem12, PDF pp.10-11

https://www.matrix-inst.org.au/wp_Matrix2016/wp-content/uploads/2023/Aletti.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Improve the known sufficient threshold alpha>2 ensuring that every connected component of a stable finite-graph WARM equilibrium is a tree, allowing arbitrary positive firing rates.

Scope

The source identifies the uniform triangle threshold4/3 as a lower obstruction. A rederivation of it is not a new threshold theorem.

P0082-A001 | Sharp reinforcement threshold for WARM forests structural argument

Argument or result

At a positive equilibrium x, put s_v=sum_(e~v)x_e^alpha. The Hessian of the correctly rate-weighted Lyapunov function satisfies H[delta,delta]=(alpha-1)sum_e delta_e^2/x_e-alpha sum_v p_v/s_v^2 (sum_(e~v)x_e^(alpha-1)delta_e)^2. Along an even cycle choose alternating delta_e=+/-x_e^(1-alpha), zero elsewhere. Every vertex sum vanishes, giving a positive direction for alpha>1 and hence linear instability.

Check

The equilibrium identity x_e=sum_(v~e)p_v x_e^alpha/s_v reduces the diagonal term. Multiplication by the positive diagonal x preserves the Hessian inertia of the linearized gradient flow.

Remaining obstruction

This eliminates even cycles, not odd cycles or all odd unicyclic supports. It does not improve the universal forest threshold by itself.

P0083  Sharp reinforcement threshold for WARM whiskers

Source: Aletti et al.: Open problems in stochastic reinforcement models; March2023 workshop, published2025 | OpenProblem13, PDF pp.10-11

https://www.matrix-inst.org.au/wp_Matrix2016/wp-content/uploads/2023/Aletti.pdf

Question

For finite WARM graphs with uniform vertex firing rates, improve the sufficient threshold alpha>25 for every surviving stable component to have diameter at most3.

Scope

This is a geometry question beyond the forest conclusion; all alpha values are grouped as one threshold problem.

P0083-A001 | Sharp reinforcement threshold for WARM whiskers structural argument

Argument or result

Consider a support path of four edges with reflection-symmetric values a,b,b,a and unit vertex rates. The endpoint equation is a=1+a^alpha/(a^alpha+b^alpha), so 1<a<2. The neighboring inner-edge equation is b=b^alpha/(a^alpha+b^alpha)+1/2. Summing gives a+b=5/2. Thus any such equilibrium reduces to a scalar equation with b=5/2-a.

Check

The total rate of the five vertices is5, equal to2a+2b. The central vertex contributes1/2 to each equal inner edge.

Remaining obstruction

Existence and stability of this scalar branch must still be analyzed, and excluding this symmetric path alone would not exclude all larger asymmetric trees.

P0084-A001 | Finite surviving components in infinite superlinear WARM structural argument

Argument or result

If every edge had survival probability at most q, this would not imply domination by iid Bernoulli percolation. For example, activate all edges simultaneously with probability q and none otherwise. Every marginal is q, yet there is an infinite component with probability q for every q>0 on an infinite connected graph.

The example computes the marginals and connectivity exactly. It is a counterexample to a marginal-only proof method, not to WARM.

The existence and uniqueness parts are grouped. Tree examples do not directly embed as bounded-length lattice edges.

P0085-A001 | WARM percolation with vanishing rates on lattices structural argument

Argument or result

There is no bounded-length embedding of a b-ary tree, b>=2, into Z^d with distinct vertices: its depth-r ball contains order b^r vertices, whereas images lie in a lattice ball of radius Lr with only O(r^d) vertices. Therefore transferring the known regular-tree construction requires unbounded subdivisions or a different geometry.

This is the exponential-versus-polynomial volume comparison, independent of reinforcement details.

Unbounded subdivisions change the local competition and rates along paths. The volume obstruction explains why a direct bounded-distortion transfer fails but does not exclude lattice WARM percolation.

Scope

The source states positivity and the growth upper bound, without an explicit monotonicity hypothesis. Any use of monotonicity must be flagged.

P0086-A001 | Recurrence of sublinear vertex-reinforced walk on Z structural argument

Argument or result

For constant w the walk is simple symmetric random walk. Starting at1 and stopping at0 or m, the harmonic function h(k)=k/m gives probability1/m of hitting m before0. Letting m tend to infinity shows that the probability of avoiding0 forever is0; by the strong Markov property returns recur indefinitely.

Check

This is the standard gambler-ruin proof of the constant-weight case, not a new result. Constant weights satisfy the stated sublinear condition for any alpha in(0,1).

Remaining obstruction

For nonconstant reinforcement, position alone is not Markov and the gambler-ruin harmonic function no longer solves the conditional transition equation.

P0087  Forced network urn convergence and diffusion scaling

Source: Aletti et al.: Open problems in stochastic reinforcement models; March2023 workshop, published2025 | OpenProblem16, PDF pp.14-16

https://www.matrix-inst.org.au/wp_Matrix2016/wp-content/uploads/2023/Aletti.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For Z_(n+1)=(1-r-f)Z_n+r X_(n+1)+f p with E[X_(n+1)|F_n]=Q Z_n, Q=A^T irreducible row-stochastic and X binary coordinatewise, determine nonconvergence and the continuous-time limit when r tends to0 and f is asymptotic to c r^2.

Scope

Assume r,f>0 and r+f<=1. The source permits 0<=p<=1, including extreme vectors; those have a different convergence answer. The displayed conditional mean alone does not specify the cross-coordinate noise covariance.

P0087-A001 | Forced network urn convergence and diffusion scaling structural argument

Argument or result

A complete fixed-step classification follows. On any event Z_n->z, the update forces X_n to converge to a binary vector x and hence eventually equal it. The bounded martingale-difference strong law gives x=Qz. The update limit gives z=(r x+f p)/(r+f). A binary coordinate of Qz can equal0 or1 only when all its positive-weight predecessors have the same x-value and matching p-value. Irreducibility then forces x and p to be the all-zero or all-one vector. Conversely if p=0, the positive stationary vector pi gives E[pi.Z_n]=(1-f)^n E[pi.Z_0], and Markov plus Borel-Cantelli yields Z_n->0; p=1 follows by complementing.

Check

The proof needs no conditional independence. The strong law follows from summable variances of martingale increments divided by n, then Kronecker. The maximum principle can also be checked by propagating the set of zero coordinates backwards through irreducibility.

Remaining obstruction

This resolves the fixed-step convergence subquestion under the stated assumptions, but not the whole diffusion question. Fast synchronization, an initial layer, and the covariance specification must be treated before a full solution label.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0088-A001 | Matching local limits of Bernoulli and Poisson-outdegree attachment structural argument

Argument or result

Conditional on current degrees, the Bernoulli outgoing count has mean Lambda_n=sum_i p_ni. Its total variation distance to Poisson(Lambda_n) is at most sum_i p_ni^2 by the elementary independent Bernoulli-to-Poisson coupling. If max_i p_ni->0 and Lambda_n stays bounded, this error tends to0.

Check

Use sum p_i^2<=max p_i sum p_i. This verifies the count approximation only; the vertex targets retain degree-dependent marks.

Remaining obstruction

A matching outgoing count does not prove local graph convergence. Target choices, ancestor types, and dependencies across successive births must be coupled over an explored neighborhood.

With m=1 and equal attachment weights, the graph is a uniform recursive tree and its empirical degree law is tight by the tree degree identity. With m>=2 the degree budget still gives uniform-root degree tightness, since its mean is at most2m plus a vanishing seed term.

P0090-A001 | Multistate pivot-tree criterion for interpretation structural argument

Argument or result

For a finite-type pivot branching process with mean matrix M and spectral radius less than1, E[population at level n] tends to0, hence P(level n nonempty)<=E[size] tends to0. This proves extinction and explains the subcritical direction already covered by Theorem1.8.

Let each vertex state be its own independent Bernoulli(p) innovation, ignoring its children. The pivot tree is empty and the state is measurable from the marked tree, but for 0<p<1 it is independent of the unmarked tree and cannot be a measurable function of that tree.

P0093  Infinitely many fixed points of a finite-color automaton

Source: Random tree recursions: which fixed points correspond to tangible sets of trees? | Section6, PDF p.40, third paragraph

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1808.03019v2

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For a finite color set of size at least3 and a deterministic child-count automaton under the paper standing offspring assumptions, can the distributional map have infinitely many fixed points?

Scope

The one-child identity example is excluded by the genuine-branching assumption. Empty-color embeddings are not used to manufacture examples.

P0093-A001 | Infinitely many fixed points of a finite-color automaton structural argument

Argument or result

If the offspring distribution has finite support bounded by D, each coordinate of the distributional map is a polynomial of degree at most D in the color probabilities. Fixed points are therefore a compact real algebraic subset of the probability simplex. In contrast with one variable, polynomial equations may have curves of zeros, so analyticity alone cannot prove finiteness.

Check

For example, the algebraic system x-y=0 in a square has a whole line of zeros; this illustrates the logical gap without asserting that this system comes from an automaton.

Remaining obstruction

The nonnegative multinomial coefficients and deterministic count assignments constrain which algebraic sets can occur. Neither an admissible positive-dimensional example nor a general exclusion is obtained.

P0094  Prescribing rogue and interpretable automaton fixed points

Source: Random tree recursions: which fixed points correspond to tangible sets of trees? | Section6, PDF p.40, third paragraph

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1808.03019v2

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Given a finite prescribed configuration of two-state fixed points with rogue/interpretable labels, determine whether a deterministic tree automaton and offspring distribution can realize it, including the monotone-automaton restriction.

Scope

Endpoint and multiplicity constraints are part of realizability. The request is not permission to assign arbitrary labels that contradict the pivot criterion.

P0094-A001 | Prescribing rogue and interpretable automaton fixed points structural argument

Argument or result

For a differentiable increasing distributional map Psi, let g(p)=Psi(p)-p. At an isolated simple fixed point, crossing from g>0 to g<0 gives Psi prime<1, while the opposite crossing gives Psi prime>1. Consecutive simple interior crossings must alternate signs. Thus a prescribed list of simple attractive/repulsive crossings cannot have two consecutive crossings of the same orientation without an intervening zero.

Check

This is the intermediate value theorem and the derivative sign at a simple root; it makes no claim for tangencies or nondifferentiable maps.

Remaining obstruction

The derivative restriction is necessary, not sufficient for an automaton realization or rogue classification in degenerate cases. Constructing compatible multinomial maps remains open here.

P0095  Automaton-level conditions for a unique fixed point

Source: Random tree recursions: which fixed points correspond to tangible sets of trees? | Section6, PDF p.40, fourth paragraph

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1808.03019v2

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Find structural conditions on a tree automaton itself that force uniqueness of its distributional fixed point, complementing the source condition on a first-order interpretation.

Scope

The condition must involve the offspring law as appropriate; the same rule can undergo bifurcations when that law changes.

P0095-A001 | Automaton-level conditions for a unique fixed point structural argument

Argument or result

Suppose that for each offspring count D the rule depends on a fixed set of at most k(D) child positions, and E k(D)=c<1. Couple child colors optimally with disagreement probability dTV(mu,nu); the union bound over that fixed relevant set gives dTV(Psi(mu),Psi(nu))<=c dTV(mu,nu). Iteration is a contraction and yields a unique fixed point on the finite simplex.

Check

The relevant set must be fixed independently of the child configuration; a pointwise bound on pivotal children alone would not justify this global coupling. For symmetric count automata the crude k(D)=D criterion reduces to E D<1.

Remaining obstruction

For supercritical offspring the crude contraction constant exceeds1. A useful structural criterion must exploit much finer properties than the number of children.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0097  An explicit nontrivial FPP time constant and limit shape

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Questions1 and37, PDF pp.11 and141

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Question

Identify an explicit positive axial time constant for a nondegenerate iid nearest-neighbor passage-time law on Z^d, or explicitly determine a nontrivial compact limit shape.

Scope

The survey itself calls Question37 a rephrasing of Question1; only one problem is counted. A zero time constant from a supercritical zero-edge atom or the deterministic l1 ball is excluded.

P0097-A001 | An explicit nontrivial FPP time constant and limit shape structural argument

Argument or result

If every edge time is multiplied by c>0, every finite path cost and every passage time is multiplied by c, so the time constant is multiplied by c and the limit shape is dilated by1/c. Thus scaling a known degenerate example cannot produce the missing nontrivial shape.

Check

The equality holds configuration by configuration before taking any limit. Positive-time and nondegeneracy restrictions exclude the zero-time percolation examples.

Remaining obstruction

Homogeneity supplies a transformation law, not a computable nondegenerate base model. Integrable directed last-passage formulas apply to a different optimization problem.

P0098  Strict FPP comparison in higher dimensions at a large minimum atom

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question3, PDF p.13; Definition2.8 and Theorem2.12, pp.12-13

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Question

Extend strict comparison of axial time constants for strictly ordered passage-time laws in increasing-concave order to d>2 when the minimum support value r>0 has mass at least the oriented-percolation threshold.

Scope

More variable means integral phi dF <= integral phi dG for every increasing concave phi. The claim is axial; equality inside an oriented percolation cone cannot be ignored in statements about arbitrary directions.

P0098-A001 | Strict FPP comparison in higher dimensions at a large minimum atom structural argument

Argument or result

For the special coupling tau'_e=tau_e+delta with delta>0, every path from0 to ne1 has at least n edges. Its primed cost is at least its original cost plus delta n. Taking infima and limits gives mu'(e1)>=mu(e1)+delta.

Check

The bound does not depend on a minimizing path or an upper bound on its length. It proves an actual strict gap for this special stochastic ordering.

Remaining obstruction

General increasing-concave order, including mean-preserving spreads, has no pointwise additive coupling. The argument does not handle the remaining comparison class.

P0099  Strict convexity of iid FPP limit shapes

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question4, PDF p.21; Section2.8

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For continuous iid passage-time distributions satisfying the shape-theorem assumptions, prove that the deterministic limit shape is strictly convex.

Scope

This qualitative absence of boundary segments is distinct from a quantitative uniform-curvature estimate. Continuity means no atoms; finite moment and positive-speed assumptions must accompany a compact shape.

P0099-A001 | Strict convexity of iid FPP limit shapes structural argument

Argument or result

Finite geodesic uniqueness does not imply strict convexity of an asymptotic norm. For example, the strictly convex norms ||x||_p converge uniformly on compact sets to ||x||_infinity as p tends to infinity, and the latter unit ball has flat faces. Even stronger finite approximations can lose strictness in the limit.

Check

The inequality ||x||_infinity <= ||x||_p <= d^(1/p)||x||_infinity verifies the convergence directly.

Remaining obstruction

This is a counterexample to an inference about limits, not an iid FPP counterexample. A uniform quantitative gap in the triangle inequality is missing.

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Questions5,8,9, PDF pp.21 and24

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Rule out a cube as an iid FPP limit shape and, more strongly, rule out a boundary segment perpendicular to e1 at the axial boundary point; exhibit a nondegenerate law with no such axial segment.

Scope

These nested axial-flatness formulations are grouped. Convexity, coordinate-reflection symmetry, and a nonempty interior are known but do not settle them.

P0100-A001 | Excluding axial facets and cubical FPP shapes structural argument

Argument or result

The l_infinity unit ball satisfies every elementary symmetry and convexity condition of an FPP limit shape and has axial facets. The l1 unit ball also satisfies those conditions but has an axial corner. Thus the shape theorem's convexity and lattice symmetries permit both geometries.

Check

Both balls are compact with nonempty interior and invariant under coordinate permutations and sign changes. Their intersections with the supporting plane x1=1 distinguish the facet from the corner.

Remaining obstruction

One needs a property specific to iid optimization that excludes the cubical candidate; deterministic convex geometry alone supplies none.

P0101  Classification of flat FPP boundary pieces

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Questions6-7, PDF p.24; definition of M_p, p.22

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For planar iid FPP, show that the only flat boundary portions are the oriented-percolation-cone portions associated, after rescaling, with a positive minimum edge weight carrying a sufficiently large atom.

Scope

M_p in the source normalizes the minimum support value to1 and its atom to p>=oriented p_c. The two source questions concerning flats outside the cone and converse classification are grouped.

P0101-A001 | Classification of flat FPP boundary pieces structural argument

Argument or result

If tau_e>=1, then every path from0 to x costs at least ||x||_1, so the limit shape lies inside the l1 ball. An oriented path consisting entirely of unit-time edges attains this lower bound exactly. This explains the mechanism generating the known cone flats.

Check

The lower bound is pathwise. Along an oriented path, its length equals the l1 displacement, so equality is exact.

Remaining obstruction

The mechanism proves why these flats occur, not why no alternative mechanism creates a flat segment away from that cone.

P0102  Strong Busemann limits for subadditive arrays and FPP

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Questions10 and32, PDF pp.26 and113

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Find useful conditions ensuring almost-sure limits of X_(m,N)-X_(n,N), including FPP differences of passage times to receding hyperplanes.

Scope

The survey explicitly translates the FPP limit question into the general-array question. These are one family; a weak subsequential or averaged limit is not the requested pathwise limit.

P0102-A001 | Strong Busemann limits for subadditive arrays and FPP structural argument

Argument or result

Let S be simple symmetric random walk and X_(m,n)=|S_n-S_m|. This is an integrable stationary ergodic subadditive array. For N with S_N>=2, X_(0,N)-X_(1,N)=S_1; for S_N<=-2 it equals -S_1. Both events occur infinitely often almost surely, so this Busemann difference does not converge.

Check

Subadditivity is the triangle inequality; stationarity and ergodicity come from the iid increments. Recurrence in both directions proves the two subsequential values, which differ because S_1 is+1 or-1.

Remaining obstruction

Kingman's usual hypotheses are insufficient. FPP triangle bounds yield compact subsequences, but an additional uniqueness or directional structure is needed for a full limit.

P0103  Uniform curvature of continuous-law FPP shapes

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question11, PDF p.31

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Prove that every continuous iid passage-time law with a compact limit shape has a uniform constant C>0 such that 1-mu(z)>=C min(mu(z-z1),mu(z-z2))^2 for boundary points z1,z2 and z=(1-lambda)z1+lambda z2.

Scope

This is the source's geometric definition, avoiding ambiguity in printed curvature-exponent conventions. It is quantitatively stronger than strict convexity.

P0103-A001 | Uniform curvature of continuous-law FPP shapes structural argument

Argument or result

Strict convexity alone cannot imply this inequality. On the l_p unit ball for p>2, take z1=((1-t^p)^(1/p),t), z2=((1-t^p)^(1/p),-t), and their midpoint z. Then 1-||z||_p is asymptotic to t^p/p, whereas ||z-z_i||_p^2=t^2. Their ratio tends to0.

Check

The expansion (1-u)^(1/p)=1-u/p+o(u) gives the asserted ratio. The l_p ball is strictly convex for p>1.

Remaining obstruction

This is a norm counterexample to a proof strategy, not an FPP counterexample. Uniform lower curvature must be proved from the random model.

P0104  Subdiffusive and KPZ passage-time fluctuation exponents

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Questions13 and15, PDF pp.34 and40; Section3

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Under suitable nondegenerate iid passage-time assumptions, prove a fluctuation exponent strictly below1/2, identify the planar exponent as1/3, and establish a positive exponent in a suitable dimension.

Scope

These closely related exponent-range targets are grouped. Different definitions using variances or stronger concentration must be specified before claiming equivalence.

P0104-A001 | Subdiffusive and KPZ passage-time fluctuation exponents structural argument

Argument or result

A variance upper bound of order n/log n does not establish an exponent below1/2. Indeed log(n/log n)/(2log n) tends to1/2. Sums of floor(n/log n) independent mean-zero variance-one variables realize that variance order in an elementary model.

Check

The logarithm calculation is exact in the limit; the example has additive variances. It is not asserted to be FPP.

Remaining obstruction

Known sublinear-variance estimates leave a polynomial-exponent gap. An additional power saving, and a matching lower bound for identification, are required.

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question14, PDF p.34

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Determine whether the dimension-dependent passage-time fluctuation exponent chi(d) tends to zero as d tends to infinity.

Scope

Existence and definition of chi(d) are themselves part of the scope; decay of the time constant is not decay of its fluctuation exponent.

P0105-A001 | High-dimensional limit of the FPP fluctuation exponent structural argument

Argument or result

Embedding Z^d into Z^(d+1) with shared weights gives T_(d+1)<=T_d and hence mu_(d+1)<=mu_d. This ordering says nothing comparable about variance: a Bernoulli random variable is bounded above by the constant1 but has larger variance.

Check

The passage-time inequality follows because the higher-dimensional infimum has more admissible paths. The variance counterexample has values1/4 and0 at Bernoulli parameter1/2.

Remaining obstruction

A distributional comparison controlling centered fluctuations across dimensions is missing. Monotonicity of means cannot supply it.

P0106  Polynomial overlap of independent planar FPP geodesics

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question16, PDF p.42

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Show that two independent geodesics from0 to ne1, sampled in independent iid planar environments with the same law, have expected edge overlap at least n^a for some a>0.

Scope

Independent environments and a fixed measurable geodesic-selection rule are intended; two samples in one unique-geodesic environment would be identical.

P0106-A001 | Polynomial overlap of independent planar FPP geodesics structural argument

Argument or result

Let E_r be the 8r+4 edges leaving the square[-r,r]^2, for r=0,...,n-1, and p_e=P(e belongs to the selected geodesic). Every path to ne1 crosses E_r, so sum_(e in E_r)p_e>=1. Independence and Cauchy-Schwarz give E|Gamma intersect Gamma'|=sum_e p_e^2 >= sum_(r=0)^(n-1)1/(8r+4) >=(1/8)log(2n+1).

Check

The sets E_r are disjoint; corners contribute two outward edges and total8r+4 is exact. Tonelli justifies the infinite sum. Integrating1/(8x+4) gives the final lower bound.

Remaining obstruction

This establishes only a logarithmic overlap lower bound. Improving shell-level spreading to a power requires model-specific concentration of geodesic locations.

P0107  Critical Bernoulli FPP growth in three dimensions

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question19, PDF p.71

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For iid edge times0 with probability p_c(Z^3) and1 otherwise, determine whether E T(0,boundary B(n)) is comparable to log n.

Scope

The target is undirected edge FPP at the three-dimensional bond threshold; planar critical exponents cannot be transferred.

P0107-A001 | Critical Bernoulli FPP growth in three dimensions structural argument

Argument or result

Collapse every connected zero-edge cluster to a vertex and retain unit-cost edges between the resulting vertices. Passage time is precisely graph distance in this random quotient graph, counting the fewest positive edges needed to reach the target. In the original graph it is bounded above by the number of positive edges on a fixed n-step coordinate path.

Check

Every original path projects to a quotient path with the same number of unit-cost crossings, and every finite quotient path lifts through finite zero-edge connections. This proves equality even when cluster sizes are unbounded.

Remaining obstruction

The logarithmic estimate requires quantitative geometry of the cluster-adjacency quotient. The planar argument based on nested dual circuits has no direct three-dimensional counterpart.

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question20, PDF p.72

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Determine the largest power alpha for which standardized passage times constrained to Z times[-n^alpha,n^alpha]^(d-1) converge to a standard normal law.

Scope

The source defines gamma_F(d) by this threshold under a finite2+delta edge-time moment. A sufficient exponent from the source theorem is not an optimality result.

P0108-A001 | Sharp width threshold for the thin-cylinder FPP CLT structural argument

Argument or result

At width0 the graph is a line, and the passage time to ne1 is exactly the sum of n iid edge weights. For a nondegenerate finite-variance law the classical iid CLT gives normalization sqrt(n Var(tau)) and mean n E(tau).

Check

Any line path containing a backtracking excursion can have that excursion removed without increasing its nonnegative cost, leaving the unique coordinate segment.

Remaining obstruction

For growing width, competing paths introduce correlations and varying block sizes. The width0 reduction does not control how rapidly an approximately independent block decomposition deteriorates.

P0109  Existence of finite FPP geodesics without distributional assumptions

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question21, PDF p.73

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For every iid finite nonnegative passage-time distribution in d>=3, prove that the infimum over finite paths between any two vertices is attained.

Scope

Infinite passage-time edges are not included in this formulation. Non-iid weighted graphs can fail attainment and therefore cannot settle the iid question by analogy.

P0109-A001 | Existence of finite FPP geodesics without distributional assumptions structural argument

Argument or result

If all edge times are at least a>0, a fixed connecting path of cost M bounds any competing path length by M/a; only finitely many simple paths of that length start at a fixed vertex, so a minimum exists. Without a positive lower bound, a deterministic ladder can fail: give each vertical edge from height j-1 to j on both rails weight2^(-j), and the rung at height k weight3*2^(-k). A route across at height k costs2+2^(-k), with infimum2 unattained.

Check

For a finite ladder route attaining maximum height k, vertical cost is at least2(1-2^(-k)) and at least one rung has cost at least3*2^(-k); this gives the same strict lower bound. Embed the ladder into Z^d with all other edges cost100 to preserve failure.

Remaining obstruction

The ladder environment is deterministic and spatially inhomogeneous, so it is only a counterexample to a general compactness inference. The iid critical case requires additional probabilistic structure.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0110  An iid FPP law with proved KPZ exponent relation

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question22, PDF p.89; equation4.19

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Exhibit a nondegenerate iid undirected FPP law with well-defined fluctuation and wandering exponents satisfying chi=2xi-1.

Scope

Conditional proofs assuming suitably strong exponents exist do not exhibit a law for which those assumptions hold. Exactly solvable directed last-passage models are outside the target.

P0110-A001 | An iid FPP law with proved KPZ exponent relation structural argument

Argument or result

If a geodesic deviates transversely by n^xi and the norm has quadratic curvature, the deterministic excess norm over a displacement of length n has order n*(n^(xi-1))^2=n^(2xi-1). This yields the proposed balance only after assuming random fluctuations are of the same order.

Check

The power follows from norm homogeneity and the square of the relative transverse displacement. A curvature exponent kappa would instead give kappa*xi-(kappa-1).

Remaining obstruction

Both the required exponent existence and error control when replacing random passage times by the norm remain unproved for a concrete iid law here. The balance calculation is conditional, not a proof.

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Questions23-24, PDF p.91

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Question

Scope

The universal continuous-law question and its weaker example target are grouped. Distinct rays mean sharing at most finitely many vertices, not merely having different first edges.

P0111-A001 | Infinitely many planar FPP geodesic rays for continuous laws structural argument

Argument or result

Finite geodesic existence and finite lattice degree yield at least one ray: among geodesics to points tending to infinity, choose an infinite subsequence with a common first edge, then a common second edge, and continue. Every finite segment of the diagonal limit is minimizing.

Check

At each stage finitely many choices ensure an infinite subsequence. Path lengths tend to infinity, and any shortcut of a limiting finite segment would contradict minimization in all sufficiently long selected paths.

Remaining obstruction

This compactness proof gives one ray, not many eventually disjoint rays. Endpoint sequences in different directions could still share the same limiting tail without additional shape information.

P0112-A001 | Full-sequence convergence of FPP geodesics to a ray structural argument

Argument or result

The space of infinite rooted paths in a locally finite graph has a compact product topology after adjoining finite-path endpoints. Thus long finite geodesics admit subsequential limits. But the abstract sequence of paths alternating between two disjoint infinite branches has two subsequential limits and no full limit.

On a rooted binary tree, alternating the all-left and all-right depth-n paths supplies an explicit sequence illustrating the failure of compactness to imply uniqueness.

This is the stronger almost-sure formulation printed in the survey. The known BKS result that the probability of midpoint membership tends to zero is not by itself this statement.

P0113-A001 | Almost-sure escape from a fixed midpoint in FPP structural argument

Argument or result

If a fixed vertex v belongs to infinitely many such geodesics, both endpoint distances from v tend to infinity. A diagonal subsequence of the two oriented halves produces a doubly infinite geodesic through v. Therefore absence of all bigeodesics would imply the target.

Check

Finite degree supplies the diagonal subsequence and every limiting finite segment is a minimizing segment of a selected geodesic. Conversely, probabilities tending to0 cannot justify a Borel-Cantelli conclusion: independent events with probability1/n occur infinitely often.
