Neither a summable midpoint bound nor unconditional absence of bigeodesics is obtained. Later midpoint papers must be compared against this exact almost-sure quantifier.

Question

P0116-A001 | Growth of the infinite-descendant part of the FPP geodesic tree structural argument

Argument or result

In a locally finite rooted tree, an edge has infinitely many descendants exactly when it lies on an infinite ray, by the finite-branching compactness lemma. If the tree has infinitely many ends, then for every k one can choose k rays with disjoint tails outside some fixed ball; each tail crosses every sufficiently large circle. Consequently f(r)>=k eventually.

The forward implication is Konig's lemma applied below the edge; the reverse is immediate. A finite selection of distinct ends separates beyond finitely many branch vertices.

This proves divergence conditional on infinitely many ends, without a rate. It supplies no unconditional ray multiplicity and no bound on where k rays separate.

P0118  Noncircular low-dimensional Eden limit shape

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question33, PDF p.130; model definition pp.128-129

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Prove that the Eden model's limit shape is not a Euclidean ball in the dimensions left open by existing high-dimensional results, in particular dimension2.

Scope

The source defines an Eden version choosing a boundary vertex uniformly. Edge-exponential infection generally chooses vertices at rates proportional to infected neighbors; these versions must not be identified without checking the time change.

P0118-A001 | Noncircular low-dimensional Eden limit shape structural argument

Argument or result

With independent rate-one clocks on currently available boundary vertices, the next added vertex is uniform and the holding time is exponential with rate equal to their number. In edge-clock growth, a vertex with k infected neighbors has k active edge clocks and hence total rate k. A boundary vertex with two infected neighbors is selected twice as often as one with a single neighbor in that version.

Check

The minimum of m independent rate-one exponentials has rate m and a uniform minimizing index. Summing k independent clock hazards gives rate k.

Remaining obstruction

This identifies the correct finite-state generator and prevents a model substitution, but the angular growth rate does not close as a function of the current radius. No anisotropic limit-shape inequality is obtained.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0119  Noncoexistence of unequal-speed Richardson infections

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Questions34-35, PDF pp.134-135

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Show that two-type Richardson growth on Z^d, d>=2, from distinct finite seeds cannot coexist indefinitely at unequal infection speeds; establish the proposed monotonicity in relative speed if that route is valid.

Scope

The source assumes an ordering of the two parameters before asking monotonicity. Speed versus mean-passage-time conventions must be fixed. The universal noncoexistence and monotonicity route are grouped.

P0119-A001 | Noncoexistence of unequal-speed Richardson infections structural argument

Argument or result

A common-clock monotone comparison of one infection's absolute size does not imply monotonicity of coexistence, because the latter requires both species to remain infinite. On Z with seeds at0 and1, every site at most0 can only be reached first through the left seed and every site at least1 through the right seed; with positive finite passage times both species survive for every speed ratio.

Check

The seeds block any nearest-neighbor path across the interface once colors are permanent. Both outward rays are infinite, independent of relative rates.

Remaining obstruction

The one-dimensional example shows the essential role of bypass geometry and is excluded from the intended d>=2 conclusion. A coupling controlling the joint survival event in higher dimensions remains missing.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0120  Strict interface speedup in half-plane inhomogeneous FPP

Source: Auffinger, Damron and Hanson: 50 years of first-passage percolation | Question38, PDF p.142

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1511.03262

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Characterize the pairs of iid half-plane edge laws F_minus,F_plus for which vertical growth along their interface is strictly faster than in either homogeneous model.

Scope

Greater speed means a smaller directional time constant. The exact convention for edges on the interface must be fixed; finite detours in the constant-law check below do not affect its conclusion.

P0120-A001 | Strict interface speedup in half-plane inhomogeneous FPP structural argument

Argument or result

For constant times a on one half-plane and b on the other, let c=min(a,b)>0. Every path with vertical displacement n costs at least cn. A path taking a bounded horizontal detour into the cheaper half-plane, moving n steps vertically, then returning costs cn+O(1). Thus the interface time constant is exactly c, so there is no strict speedup beyond both homogeneous models.

Check

The lower bound uses path length at least n; the explicit path gives a matching upper bound after division by n.

Remaining obstruction

Random switching between regions may exploit complementary favorable segments. The deterministic comparison cannot characterize when that advantage is strictly positive.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0121  Uniform Lipschitz height models and their interfaces

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problems2.2-2.3, PDF pp.15-16

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Establish Gaussian-free-field scaling and SLE4 interface limits for the source's uniform odd-integer triangular-lattice heights, and for its uniform real-valued Lipschitz heights with the appropriate boundary amplitude.

Scope

The two genuinely different height ensembles are conservatively grouped as one universality family. Integer heights have nearest-neighbor difference0 or2 and boundary values plus/minus1; real heights have difference at most1 and tuned boundary values plus/minus lambda.

P0121-A001 | Uniform Lipschitz height models and their interfaces structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

Conditioning an interior height on all other heights gives a uniform law on its feasible interval, intersected with the odd integers in the discrete model. The interval endpoints are the maximum of neighbor heights minus the slope bound and the minimum plus the bound. Its conditional mean is the midpoint of these extrema, not generally the arithmetic mean of neighboring heights.

Check

For real slope bound1 and six neighboring heights0,0,0,0,0,1, the feasible interval is[0,1], so the conditional mean is1/2 while the neighbor average is1/6. These neighbor values satisfy their mutual triangular-edge constraints.

Remaining obstruction

The discrete Gaussian harmonic-observable proof therefore does not transfer by replacing a Gaussian field with a uniform Lipschitz field. One must first control the effective large-scale field and then the discontinuous operation of extracting its zero interface.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0122  SLE4 from spectral truncations of the Gaussian free field

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem2.4, PDF p.16

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Project a planar Dirichlet Gaussian free field onto Laplace eigenmodes with eigenvalue magnitude at most R, add the harmonic extension of suitably chosen two-arc boundary values, and prove that the connecting zero interface converges to SLE4 as R tends to infinity.

Scope

The cutoff is spectral and nonlocal. Distributional convergence of the field itself is already built into the construction and is not the requested interface convergence.

P0122-A001 | SLE4 from spectral truncations of the Gaussian free field structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

Even uniform convergence of smooth functions does not make zero sets continuous. On a fixed square, h_n(x,y)=1/n has no zero set, whereas its uniform limit0 vanishes everywhere. With changing signs, n^(-1)sin(nx) also converges uniformly to0 but has many macroscopic vertical zero components.

Check

The sup norms are at most1/n. For the sinusoidal example the zero lines are x=k*pi/n wherever they lie in the square; their number diverges.

Remaining obstruction

These examples do not satisfy the prescribed random-field law or boundary data; they invalidate only the proposed continuous-mapping argument. Uniform control of near-zero topology and a characterization of the selected interface are missing.

P0123  High-temperature triangular Ising interfaces converge to SLE6

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem2.5, PDF p.17, high-temperature portion

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For every fixed inverse temperature strictly between0 and the triangular Ising critical inverse temperature, prove convergence of the two-boundary-arc spin interface to chordal SLE6.

Scope

The critical-temperature SLE3 component has been resolved in later literature and is not admitted here. The independent-spin beta=0 endpoint is also known. Fixed positive high-temperature correlations are the target.

P0123-A001 | High-temperature triangular Ising interfaces converge to SLE6 structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

The high-temperature Gibbs law is not a small total-variation perturbation of independent spins uniformly over a growing domain. For n independent two-spin blocks with coupling beta>0, the average within-block product concentrates at tanh(beta), whereas at beta=0 it concentrates at0. Choosing a threshold halfway between these means distinguishes the two product laws with probability tending to1.

Check

Each block product is plus/minus1 with mean tanh(beta); its variance is at most1. Chebyshev applied to n blocks proves asymptotic separation without a central limit approximation.

Remaining obstruction

Thus finite-range or exponentially decaying local dependence does not justify global total-variation replacement. A renormalization or observable argument controlling crossing geometry is still needed for the actual triangular model.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0124  Critical planar random-cluster interfaces for general cluster weight

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problems2.6 and2.11, PDF pp.17-18

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For the square-lattice FK model at p=sqrt(q)/(1+sqrt(q)), q in(0,4), establish the predicted chordal SLE limit with kappa=4*pi/arccos(-sqrt(q)/2).

Scope

The q=2 FK-Ising case is already resolved and excluded from the unresolved scope. Square bond percolation is q=1 and is grouped here instead of being counted again as Problem2.11.

P0124-A001 | Critical planar random-cluster interfaces for general cluster weight structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

Given all edges except e, the FK conditional probability that e is open is p if its endpoints are already connected, and p/[p+q(1-p)] otherwise. At the proposed self-dual p these become sqrt(q)/(1+sqrt(q)) and1/(1+sqrt(q)). They straddle1/2 for q>1.

Check

Opening e changes its Bernoulli odds by p/(1-p); if it merges components it also changes the cluster-count weight by1/q. Normalizing the two possible weights gives the displayed conditional probabilities.

Remaining obstruction

Self-duality supplies symmetry but not an independent percolation exploration law. The dependence on long-range connectivity prevents a direct reuse of the q=1 or q=2 observable argument.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0125  Conformal limits of the critical and dense O(n) loop model

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem2.7, PDF p.17

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For the hexagonal O(n) loop model, establish the predicted SLE limits of a boundary-to-boundary strand at the critical edge weight and in the dense phase.

Scope

The source's whole parameter family is one problem. Previously admitted self-avoiding walk at n=0 is cross-referenced, and known Ising cases at n=1 are not new open items. The remaining interacting-loop ensembles form this entry.

P0125-A001 | Conformal limits of the critical and dense O(n) loop model structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

Deleting all closed loops from a configuration does not give a self-avoiding walk with weights depending only on its length. The marginal weight of a strand gamma is proportional to x^|gamma| times the partition function of loops compatible with gamma. Two equal-length strands may leave different complementary regions and therefore different loop partition functions.

Check

Summing the joint weight x^(|gamma|+total loop length)*n^(number of loops) over compatible loop families factors out x^|gamma| and leaves exactly the stated complementary partition function.

Remaining obstruction

The environmental partition-function factor is the missing ingredient in a reduction to self-avoiding walk. Its conformal asymptotics have not been derived here.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0126  Planar uniform lattice-tree diameter and scaling limit

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem2.8, PDF p.18

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Determine the expected Euclidean diameter growth and the rescaled random-set limit of the uniform n-vertex lattice tree containing the origin, in particular on the planar square lattice.

Scope

A uniform embedded n-vertex tree is not a uniform spanning tree of a fixed region or a critical Galton-Watson tree. High-dimensional spread-out results do not settle the planar model.

P0126-A001 | Planar uniform lattice-tree diameter and scaling limit structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

Every such planar tree has Euclidean diameter at most n-1. If its diameter is D, all its vertices lie in an axis-aligned square of side at most2D around any chosen vertex, containing at most(2D+1)^2 lattice points. Hence D is at least(sqrt(n)-1)/2, deterministically.

Check

The upper bound follows from connectedness and at most n-1 edges on any simple tree path. The lower bound counts the containing square and applies to every connected n-vertex subset, independently of the measure.

Remaining obstruction

The interval between square-root and linear growth is too wide to identify the exponent. Expected-diameter normalization also gives no tightness of the full tree geometry by itself.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0127  Efficient approximately uniform sampling of lattice trees

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem2.9, PDF p.18

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Give a provably efficient algorithm to sample approximately uniformly from the n-vertex lattice trees containing the origin, or prove an appropriate computational obstruction.

Scope

An ergodic chain with uniform stationary distribution is not automatically a polynomial-time sampler. The meaning of approximation must specify, for example, total variation error.

P0127-A001 | Efficient approximately uniform sampling of lattice trees structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

All eligible trees lie in the radius n-1 lattice ball. Enumerating all subsets of its edges, retaining exactly the connected acyclic n-vertex subsets containing0, and choosing a uniformly random retained index gives an exact finite sampler. On Z^2 the ambient edge count is O(n^2), so the naive running time is at most exponential in n^2.

Check

Every tree vertex is reached by at most n-1 unit steps from0, proving the finite containment. Acyclicity plus connectedness and n vertices characterizes the required objects, with each edge set counted once.

Remaining obstruction

This supplies existence and exactness but fails efficiency. The later Fredes-Marckert survey constructs uniform-stationary chains; their existence alone does not provide a polynomial mixing bound.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0128  Cardy conformal invariance for Voronoi percolation

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem2.12, PDF p.19

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Prove the analogue of Smirnov's crossing-probability theorem for critical planar Voronoi percolation, with the corresponding conformally invariant interface limit.

Scope

Annealed Poisson geometry and independent fair cell colors are intended. Invariance under changing a background metric, known earlier, is a different statement from Cardy's formula.

P0128-A001 | Cardy conformal invariance for Voronoi percolation structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

A planar conformal map f sends a homogeneous Poisson process of intensity lambda to an inhomogeneous process whose intensity at w is lambda/|f'(f^(-1)(w))|^2. Thus exact rotation and translation symmetry of the original process do not produce exact invariance under arbitrary conformal maps.

Check

The expected image count in a Borel set A equals lambda times the area of f^(-1)(A); the change-of-variables formula gives the stated Jacobian. Disjoint-set independence is preserved, so this identifies the transformed process.

Remaining obstruction

One would need to prove that a smoothly varying local intensity does not affect the limiting critical crossing law. The Jacobian computation exposes that missing universality step without proving it.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0129  Cardy conformal invariance for Boolean continuum percolation

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem2.13, PDF p.19

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Establish Smirnov-type conformally invariant critical crossing limits for the planar Poisson Boolean model of equal-radius disks.

Scope

Varying the disk radius at intensity1 and varying intensity at radius1 are related by a Euclidean scaling and are one problem, not two corpus entries.

P0129-A001 | Cardy conformal invariance for Boolean continuum percolation structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

Scaling space by1/r maps intensity1 centers with disk radius r to radius-one disks whose centers have intensity r^2. Therefore all Euclidean-scale behavior depends on the dimensionless product lambda*r^2 for general intensity lambda. Under a nonaffine conformal map, however, each disk is deformed and its local size depends on position.

Check

An area A in scaled coordinates corresponds to original area r^2 A, yielding mean center count r^2 A. This proves the exact parameter equivalence.

Remaining obstruction

The scalar scaling relation does not control spatially inhomogeneous radii and noncircular images under conformal maps. Proving their irrelevance at criticality remains the obstruction.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0130  Sharp planar one-arm probability beyond its exponent

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem3.1, PDF p.20, one-arm remainder

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Improve the critical triangular-percolation one-arm probability R^(-5/48+o(1)) to sharp multiplicative asymptotics, at least bounds by constant multiples of R^(-5/48).

Scope

Du-Gao-Li-Zhuang resolve sharp half-plane and planar polychromatic multi-arm asymptotics; those resolved portions of the 2006 question are excluded. Their abstract does not include the planar one-arm event.

P0130-A001 | Sharp planar one-arm probability beyond its exponent structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

Quasi-multiplicative information and a logarithmic exponent need not imply a constant asymptotic amplitude. For alpha>0 and0<a<alpha, define f(R)=R^(-alpha)*exp(a*sin(log R)), R>=1. Then f is decreasing, has logarithmic exponent-alpha, and obeys exp(-3a)<=f(RS)/(f(R)f(S))<=exp(3a), but R^alpha f(R) has no limit.

Check

The derivative of log f with respect to log R is-alpha+a*cos(log R)<0. The quasi-multiplicative bound follows from three sine terms each bounded by1; subsequences with sine plus/minus1 give different amplitudes.

Remaining obstruction

This is a scalar obstruction to extracting an asymptotic constant from coarse multiplicativity. It is not a percolation counterexample, and even constant-factor bounds for the specified probability are not proved by it.

P0131  Exact critical percolation chemical-distance exponent

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem3.3, PDF p.20

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Determine the exponent alpha for the expected length of the shortest left-right critical percolation crossing of a square of side R, conditional on a crossing existing.

Scope

The Euclidean connection event, the number of edges in a shortest open path, and its conditional expectation are distinct quantities. The source requests the exponent itself, not merely improved upper or lower bounds.

P0131-A001 | Exact critical percolation chemical-distance exponent structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

A shortest crossing may be chosen self-avoiding by deleting loops. Its length is at least a constant times R and at most the number of sites in the square, which is O(R^2). Thus any exponent defined by the stated expectation must lie in[1,2].

Check

The lower bound is net horizontal displacement divided by the maximum horizontal displacement per edge. The upper bound counts sites on a self-avoiding path, so it holds on every configuration with a crossing.

Remaining obstruction

These geometric bounds do not quantify the critical detours around closed clusters. Interface dimensions are not automatically dimensions of shortest open crossings.

P0132  Measurable loop erasure of planar Brownian motion

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem6.1, PDF p.24; Berestycki-Sauzedde2026, Section7 first question

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

In the joint scaling limit of a planar random walk and its loop erasure, determine whether the SLE2 path is a measurable function of the Brownian path.

Scope

The 2026 loop-addition theorem establishes the coupling and its chronological Brownian law; the authors explicitly leave inverse measurability open. Existence of a coupling does not imply a deterministic factor.

P0132-A001 | Measurable loop erasure of planar Brownian motion structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

For random elements(G,W) in standard Borel spaces, G is measurable from W exactly when the regular conditional distribution Law(G|W) is a point mass almost surely. An arbitrary measurable choice of a point in each fiber is insufficient: if G is standard normal and W=|G|, choosing the positive preimage changes the joint law, whose conditional signs remain fair.

Check

The forward implication follows by conditioning G=g(W). For the reverse, a probability kernel supported on Dirac measures identifies a measurable atom through a countable separating family. In the normal example symmetry gives conditional sign probability1/2 for W>0.

Remaining obstruction

No degeneration of the conditional SLE law given the complete Brownian chronology has been shown. The new forward map does not provide this missing conditional-law argument.

P0133  Torus spanning-tree dual homotopy distribution

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem6.2, PDF p.24

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Determine the limiting distribution of the homotopy classes of the three edge-simple noncontractible closed paths in the dual complement of a uniform spanning tree on a fine toroidal square grid.

Scope

The question concerns the joint winding information, not only the number of dual cycles. The geometry of the torus is an input. Later line-bundle determinant methods require a separate exact-scope comparison.

P0133-A001 | Torus spanning-tree dual homotopy distribution structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

On a toroidal N-vertex square grid, E=2N and there are N faces. Removing the N-1 primal tree edges leaves N+1 dual edges. The dual complement is connected, so its first Betti number is(N+1)-N+1=2. This explains two independent topological cycles but does not itself specify the triple of simple-loop homotopy classes.

Check

Euler's formula on the torus gives V-E+F=0. Dual connectivity follows because a separating primal-dual cut would correspond to a cycle contained in the primal tree, which is impossible. The cycle-rank formula is E-V+1 for a connected finite graph.

Remaining obstruction

Rank2 determines neither winding slopes nor their probabilities. A scalar Laplacian determinant counts all spanning trees without resolving this joint topological information.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0134  A nontrivial exact Hausdorff gauge for SLE

Source: Schramm: Conformally invariant scaling limits, overview and collection of problems, 2006v2 | Problem7.1, PDF p.25

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0602151

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Find a nontrivial Hausdorff gauge measure for SLE_kappa, kappa<8, which is sigma-finite and preferably finite on compact subarcs, as intended by the source's natural-parametrization question.

Scope

Literally, the zero measure is sigma-finite; the source context requires a useful nonzero measure. Rezaei proves that the critical power gauge r^(1+kappa/8) gives zero Hausdorff measure. Minkowski content is not automatically a Hausdorff measure.

P0134-A001 | A nontrivial exact Hausdorff gauge for SLE structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

Hausdorff dimension alone cannot select the exact gauge. For any set of dimension d, a power exponent s>d gives zero Hausdorff measure, while s<d gives infinite measure; at s=d either zero, finite positive, or infinite behavior can occur. Multiplying r^d by a slowly varying factor can change the critical measure while leaving the dimension unchanged.

Check

The two off-critical conclusions are the defining threshold property of Hausdorff dimension. At the critical exponent, a smooth interval has finite positive H^1, while a countable union of disjoint intervals with divergent total length has infinite H^1; critical zero examples exist among dimension-one Cantor sets.

Remaining obstruction

One needs fine local occupation estimates, not just dimension or existence of Minkowski content. The predicted logarithmic correction has not been proved here.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0135  Almost-sure fibers of chronological loop addition

Source: Berestycki and Sauzedde: Brownian paths as loop-decorated SLEs,2026 | Section7, Almost sure(non-)injectivity

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.04673

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Determine whether the chronological attachment map from a typical SLE2 path and the intersected Brownian loops to the resulting Brownian path has a unique preimage almost surely, or instead infinitely many.

Scope

Global noninjectivity is already noted by the authors. The question concerns typical fibers in the specified support. It is related to, but stronger than, recovering only the SLE path measurably.

P0135-A001 | Almost-sure fibers of chronological loop addition structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

Global noninjectivity cannot settle almost-sure invertibility. The map f:R->R with f(0)=f(1)=0 and f(x)=x otherwise is noninjective, yet it is injective off the two-point set and therefore injective on a full-measure set for a continuous input law. Conversely, x->|x| has two typical preimages for a symmetric continuous law.

Check

A finite set has probability0 under an atomless law. For the absolute-value map, every y>0 has two preimages of equal conditional probability under a standard normal input.

Remaining obstruction

One must exhibit an ambiguity mechanism occurring on a positive-probability or full-probability family of loop configurations. A deterministic exceptional pair or a measurable selection theorem does not do this.

The source explains that its planar deterministic continuity hypothesis fails in dimension3. A probabilistic continuity statement at typical configurations could still suffice.

P0136-A001 | Chronological Brownian loop addition in three dimensions structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

A fixed planar circle intersecting a line in R^3 becomes disjoint from that line after an arbitrarily small translation in the perpendicular direction. For example, gamma is the x-axis segment[-2,2] and ell is the unit circle in the xy-plane; ell+(0,0,epsilon) avoids gamma for every epsilon nonzero while converging uniformly to ell.

Every point of the translated loop has z-coordinate epsilon and every point of gamma has z-coordinate0. At epsilon0 the circle meets gamma at(plus/minus1,0,0).

Remaining obstruction

Attaching or omitting a loop of fixed diameter changes the resulting path macroscopically. Typical random intersections need a stability argument beyond deterministic uniform closeness; the example does not refute such an almost-sure statement.

P0137  A loop-erased representation of a unit Brownian loop

Source: Berestycki and Sauzedde: Brownian paths as loop-decorated SLEs,2026 | Section7, SLE2 loop analogue

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.04673

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Describe a unit Brownian loop as an SLE2-type loop decorated by an independent Brownian loop soup, with a precise law and chronology.

Scope

A closed loop has no distinguished endpoint ordering. Rooting conventions, duration conditioning, and the law of the proposed SLE loop must be fixed together.

P0137-A001 | A loop-erased representation of a unit Brownian loop structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

Even in a finite closed walk, rooting before chronological loop erasure can discard all information: the standard loop erasure of a finite walk ending at its starting vertex is the single starting vertex. When the terminal return is processed, it erases the whole currently retained path.

Check

The standard stack algorithm retains distinct vertices and, upon revisiting a vertex already on the stack, removes everything after its previous occurrence. The final return to the first vertex therefore leaves only that vertex.

Remaining obstruction

A nontrivial loop analogue cannot simply apply the open-path loop-erasure algorithm to an arbitrarily rooted closed Brownian path. A cyclic or marked-edge construction and its resulting sampling bias remain to be established.

P0138  Law of loop-decorated SLE beyond kappa2

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2602.04673

Question

Identify the chronological path law obtained by decorating chordal SLE_kappa with Brownian loops at restriction intensity lambda=(8-3*kappa)*(6-kappa)/(2*kappa), including its proposed interpretation as partially erased Brownian motion.

Scope

Only parameters giving a nonnegative soup intensity and an applicable attachment construction are meaningful. The interval2<=kappa<=8/3 interpolates the Brownian kappa2 endpoint and undecorated SLE8/3 endpoint.

P0138-A001 | Law of loop-decorated SLE beyond kappa2 structural reduction or obstruction

Argument or result

At kappa2 the displayed intensity is1; at kappa8/3 it is0. Agreement of the filled hull with a restriction law cannot identify the ordered path: a circle traversed clockwise or counterclockwise has the same trace and hull, and arbitrary monotone time changes also preserve the hull.

Check

Substitution gives lambda2=(8-6)*(6-2)/4=1 and lambda8/3=0. The two circle trajectories differ as parametrized maps while having identical ranges.

Remaining obstruction

Restriction formulas control avoidance of sets, not chronological finite-dimensional distributions or quadratic variation. A path-level stochastic characterization is missing.

The precise topology must include all necessary time and iterated-integral information. Continuity in the source's existing path topology does not imply continuity of rough-path lifts.

Argument or result

Concatenate m positively oriented circles of radius m^(-1/2), each based at0, over a unit time interval. Their sup norm tends to0 and, for p>2, their first-level p-variation is at most C_p*m^(1/p-1/2), which also tends to0. But their total signed area is m*pi*m^(-1)=pi.

Each circle contributes O(r^p) to the p-variation power, and splitting a partition at returns to0 changes the bound by only a p-dependent factor. Areas add over closed concatenated loops and equal pi*r^2 per circle.

Remaining obstruction

This proves that first-level convergence alone cannot control the second iterated integral. It does not rule out continuity with properly enhanced input loops or the source's additional cusp restrictions.

Two-sided logarithmic bounds are weaker than convergence to constants. The positive-delta regime is the finite-variance degree regime; logarithmic-logarithmic formulas from negative delta do not apply.

Argument or result

For every connected graph, the distance D(U,V) between two uniform vertices is bounded by its diameter, so if the two normalized quantities converge in probability to constants a,b then necessarily a<=b. Strict inequality does not follow: a complete graph has typical distance and diameter both1 with probability tending to1.

If a>b, choose epsilon<(a-b)/2; simultaneous convergence would force D(U,V)>diameter with probability tending to1, a contradiction. The complete-graph calculation checks the strictness issue separately.

This gives only the necessary weak ordering. The preferential-attachment age correlations and extreme peripheral vertices must be quantified to identify constants and show a strict gap.

Argument or result

If integer-valued X_n is centered by real c_n, every centered value lies in Z-c_n. Along a subsequence with fractional parts c_n tending to r, any weak limit is supported on Z-r. Two subsequences whose fractional parts have different limits can therefore have incompatible translated-lattice limits.

Check

For a closed set staying a positive distance from Z-r, its probability is eventually0; the portmanteau theorem puts any weak limit on Z-r. The deterministic example X_n=floor(c_n) gives centered value minus the fractional part.

Scope

The rooted shortest-path tree is a random recursive tree, for which substantial height refinements already exist. The all-pairs fluctuation target is retained; known rooted results are not new solutions.

P0143-A001 | Second-order extremal hopcount in exponential complete-graph FPP structural reduction

Argument or result

For a random recursive tree, F_n(z)=E sum_v z^(depth(v)) equals product_(j=1)^(n-1)(1+z/j). For z>1, P(height>=k)<=z^(-k)F_n(z)<=exp(z H_(n-1)-k log z). Taking k=a log n,z=a gives an upper exponent a-a log a. A union bound over n roots adds1, explaining alpha_star log(alpha_star)-alpha_star=1.

Check

When vertex n is added, its parent is uniform among n-1 vertices, so the expected generating function is multiplied by1+z/(n-1). The bound log(1+z/j)<=z/j yields the display.

Remaining obstruction

This recovers a first-order upper bound only. It loses dependence between extreme paths and the logarithmic corrections needed for a fluctuation law.

Question

Describe the fluctuations of the minimal total weight between two typical vertices of an infinite-variance configuration model when the associated weighted branching process is conservative.

Scope

The source explicitly says the question concerns random fluctuations beyond the leading quantile-sum scale. Later leading-order weighted-distance results must not be mistaken for a complete centered limiting law.

P0144-A001 | Conservative weighted-distance fluctuations in heavy-tailed configuration models structural reduction

Argument or result

For nonnegative weights, a path sum is at least its maximum edge, so the FPP distance dominates the minimax path value. The latter is exactly the smallest level t at which the two vertices are connected using edges of weight at most t. This follows by comparing the same path family under the two objectives.

Check

A connection at level t supplies a path with every edge at most t, and any path with maximum edge at most t supplies such a connection; taking infima proves the minimax/percolation identity.

Remaining obstruction

The lower bound tracks the largest obstruction but discards accumulated small costs. In the conservative regime infinitely many layers can contribute, so the minimax comparison does not determine centered sum fluctuations.

Question

Determine the scaling and possible central limit laws for the number of edges on a minimum-weight path between typical vertices of an infinite-variance configuration model.

Scope

The target is hopcount, not total weight or unweighted distance. Separate edge-weight laws may lead to different behaviors but are one classification problem.

P0145-A001 | Hopcount universality in infinite-variance weighted configuration models structural reduction

Argument or result

If all weights lie in[a,b] with0<a<=b, the hopcount H of a minimizing path and graph distance d satisfy d<=H<=(b/a)d: a shortest unweighted path costs at most bd, whereas the chosen path costs at least aH. Monotone transformations do not preserve hopcount: paths with weights(1,4) and(1.8,1.8,1.8) reverse their cost ordering when weights are squared.

Check

The original costs are5 and5.4; the squared costs are17 and9.72. Both paths can be realized as the two branches of a finite cycle, so the comparison is exact.

Remaining obstruction

A bounded-factor comparison gives no fluctuation law, and it disappears for weights approaching0. The exploration process must jointly control accumulated cost and generation depth.

Question

Scope

The source assumes a convergent degree law with a uniformly bounded2+epsilon moment and positive mass at the minimum degree. If the graph is disconnected, distances must be restricted to the relevant giant component; infinite distances are not silently included.

P0147-A001 | Fluctuations of weighted flooding and diameter on configuration models structural reduction

Argument or result

On any fixed graph, the minimum incident exponential edge weight at a degree-d vertex is Exp(d). For a collection of m pairwise nonadjacent degree-d vertices these minima use disjoint edge sets and are independent. Hence P(d max X_v-log m<=x)=(1-exp(-x)/m)^m tends to exp(-exp(-x)).

Check

The minimum of d independent rate-one exponentials has tail exp(-dt). Nonadjacent vertices cannot share an incident edge. Multiplying their distribution functions proves the extreme-value limit.

Remaining obstruction

This identifies one endpoint-delay component of the extremes. The bulk connecting distances and their dependence on the extreme endpoints are not controlled, so no full flooding or diameter limit is claimed.

Question

Extend weighted flooding-time and diameter asymptotics from exponential weights to general iid nonnegative edge-weight laws on the configuration model.

Scope

Both the lower tail used by fast paths and the upper tail of the minimum weight leaving a peripheral vertex can matter. This is one law-classification problem.

P0148-A001 | General edge-weight laws for random-graph flooding and diameter structural reduction

Argument or result

At a degree-d vertex the minimum incident weight X has tail P(X>t)=(1-F(t))^d. Over m nonadjacent such vertices, P(max X_v<=t)=[1-(1-F(t))^d]^m. For weights E^s this gives max X_v asymptotic to(log m/d)^s in probability.

Check

For E^s, P(X>t)=exp(-d*t^(1/s)); setting t=((1 plus/minus epsilon)log m/d)^s and using the displayed maximum distribution gives matching upper and lower probability bounds.

Remaining obstruction

Endpoint delays alone can change scale, but the fastest route through the bulk is a different optimization. Matching these two mechanisms for general F is still missing.

Question

Determine the first-order extreme hopcount from one root and over all pairs for general configuration-model degree laws, and extend beyond exponential edge weights.

Scope

The known regular-graph constants use a branching-generation large-deviation calculation. Replacing the degree by its mean is not justified for extremes.

P0149-A001 | Extreme hopcounts for general random-graph degree and weight laws structural reduction

Argument or result

On a graph with maximum degree Delta, there are at most n*Delta*(Delta-1)^(k-1) oriented simple k-edge paths. With iid weights Y, the chance any such path has total weight at most t is at most this count times exp(a*t)*(E exp(-aY))^k for every a>0.

Check

After the first edge there are at most Delta-1 nonbacktracking choices. Independence along a simple path and exponential Markov inequality bound its lower-tail cost. The union bound needs no independence between different paths.

Remaining obstruction

The bound is too crude when a few hubs make Delta large and does not automatically constrain minimizing paths with unknown total cost. Degree-biased branching and extreme endpoint selection require sharper control.

Question

Scope

The printed problem heading says finite third moment, but its referenced theorem and surrounding text concern infinite third moment, tau in(3,4). Bhamidi et al2020 improve the global lower-mass bound under slightly stronger assumptions; the minimal-assumption remainder requires exact comparison.

P0150-A001 | Minimal assumptions for heavy-tailed critical metric convergence structural reduction

Argument or result

Let X_n=[0,1] with the ordinary metric and probability measure(1-1/n)delta_0+(1/n)Lebesgue. Every fixed finite iid sample from this measure lies at0 with probability tending to1, so its distance matrices converge to those of a point. Yet the spaces all have diameter1 and cannot converge in Gromov-Hausdorff distance to a point.

Check

The probability that k sampled points all select the delta_0 component is(1-1/n)^k. The Gromov-Hausdorff distance from a compact space to a point is half its diameter. Each X_n still has full-support measure.

Remaining obstruction

This is a precise failure of weak measured convergence to control thin appendages. A uniform lower bound on masses of fixed-radius balls is the missing type of estimate.

Local weak convergence alone cannot determine macroscopic cluster sizes. A single cycle on n vertices and a disjoint union of cycles whose lengths are about sqrt(n) have the same local weak limit, the two-sided infinite path, but their largest components have orders n and sqrt(n).

For every fixed radius r, once each cycle length exceeds2r+1 all rooted radius-r neighborhoods are identical. Their total component sizes remain explicitly different.

P0152  Kernel criteria for critical inhomogeneous random-graph universality

Source: Van der Hofstad: Stochastic processes on random graphs, Saint-Flour2017 | Open Problem4.5-4.6, PDF pp.222-223

https://www.win.tue.nl/~rhofstad/SaintFlour_SPoRG_fin_Saint_Flour.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Characterize critical component universality for general inhomogeneous random-graph kernels, including the proposed role of the positive principal eigenfunction belonging to L3.

Scope

The general question and the eigenfunction criterion are grouped. Rank-one and finite-type theorems do not cover every irreducible unbounded kernel.

P0152-A001 | Kernel criteria for critical inhomogeneous random-graph universality structural reduction

Argument or result

L3 integrability of the principal eigenfunction does not itself give a spectral gap. Partition[0,1] into blocks of masses p_i tending to0, choose epsilon_i>0 tending to0, and let m=sum p_i epsilon_i. On blocks i,j set kappa=(1-epsilon_i)1{i=j}/p_i+epsilon_i epsilon_j/m. This symmetric positive kernel has row integral1, so its principal eigenfunction is the constant1 in L3. For the centered indicator of block i, the Rayleigh quotient tends to1 as epsilon_i tends to0.

Check

The row integral is1-epsilon_i+epsilon_i=1. The Dirichlet energy of the block indicator is at most p_i epsilon_i, while its centered L2 norm squared is p_i(1-p_i). Thus the spectral gap is at most epsilon_i/(1-p_i), tending to0. Positivity of the kernel makes the constant eigenfunction unique up to scale.

Remaining obstruction

The example is an obstruction to using an unstated spectral-gap consequence of the conjectured criterion. It is not asserted to refute random-graph universality; the component scaling for this explicit kernel remains to be analyzed.

Question

Scope

Time normalization, the giant component, and initial distribution must match the source. The suggestion that the threshold vanishes for infinite second moment is a question, not an established consequence of ordinary percolation.

P0153-A001 | Vacant-set percolation threshold on general configuration models structural reduction

Argument or result

For simple random walk on a finite connected graph, stationary mass at v is d_v/sum_w d_w. This controls expected occupation counts in stationarity, but it does not identify the probability of ever visiting v: repeated returns can account for many occupation times. In particular P(visit v by time t)<=E[number of visits by time t], with no matching lower bound from this mean alone.

Check

The stationary law follows from detailed balance pi_u/d_u=pi_v/d_v on each edge. The displayed visit bound is Markov's inequality for the nonnegative integer occupation count.

Remaining obstruction

Degree-biased occupation cannot be substituted for independent vertex deletion. One needs escape probabilities and joint avoidance estimates to determine the critical remaining branching structure.

Random-regular vacant-set metric limits are already established. The general degree law and possible suppression of high-degree hubs form the unresolved extension.

P0154-A001 | Metric scaling of critical random-walk vacant components structural reduction

Argument or result

A high degree need not imply large escape capacity. Attach K leaves to a vertex v that has just one additional edge leading into a fixed transient infinite tree. Every walk step from v into a leaf returns to v with probability1; only the remaining edge can contribute to escape. Thus d_v*P_v(no return after time0) is at most1, uniformly in K.

Check

Condition on the first step from v. Each leaf contributes0 to the sum of neighbor escape probabilities, and the one transient branch contributes at most1. This is the usual unit-conductance singleton capacity calculation.

Remaining obstruction

The example is not a configuration-model limit theorem. It shows why deleting hubs based only on degree can fail: escape geometry, not stationary occupation alone, controls avoidance.

Argument or result

For a fixed finite graph with distinct edge weights, applying any strictly increasing function to every weight leaves the minimum spanning tree unchanged. Question

P0158-A001 | Ordered-phase susceptibility exponent on random trees and graphs structural reduction

Argument or result

At zero cavity field, the expected generation-k contribution to susceptibility is proportional to nu^(k-1)*(tanh beta)^k, yielding a geometric series only when nu*tanh beta<1. Above criticality that same bound diverges, so it cannot prove the desired finite susceptibility asymptotic in the plus state.

The ratio of consecutive generation terms is nu*tanh beta, which equals1 at criticality and exceeds1 on the ordered side. Nonzero random cavity fields supply extra attenuation factors in the exact source formula.

A sharp lower bound on that attenuation, averaged along the size-biased spine and uniformly near criticality, is missing. Analytically continuing the high-temperature geometric sum would give an invalid negative denominator.

P0159  Quenched non-Gaussian critical spin limits on heterogeneous random graphs

Source: Van der Hofstad: Stochastic processes on random graphs, Saint-Flour2017 | Open Problem5.2, PDF p.305

https://www.win.tue.nl/~rhofstad/SaintFlour_SPoRG_fin_Saint_Flour.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Extend the critical non-classical total-spin limit theorem from annealed generalized random graphs to quenched configuration models or quenched generalized random graphs.

Scope

Quenched means fixing the sampled graph before taking its spin law. Matching critical exponents or annealed pressure does not establish a quenched distributional limit.

P0159-A001 | Quenched non-Gaussian critical spin limits on heterogeneous random graphs structural reduction

Argument or result

For environment G and partition functions Z_G(h), the averaged quenched moment generating function is E[Z_G(h)/Z_G(0)], whereas the annealed one is E Z_G(h)/E Z_G(0). These are generally different. If two equally likely environments have(Z0,Zh)=(1,1) and(2,2*cosh h), the expressions are(1+cosh h)/2 and(1+2*cosh h)/3.

Check

The two finite Boltzmann systems can be realized by one zero-spin state versus two states with spins plus/minus1. The formulas then follow directly from normalization. This is a normalization example, not a graph-model counterexample.

Remaining obstruction

The annealed integral identity weights graph environments by their partition functions. Removing this tilt while preserving critical fluctuations is the unresolved step.

P0160  Fixed-temperature metastability for general configuration models

Source: Van der Hofstad: Stochastic processes on random graphs, Saint-Flour2017 | Open Problem5.4, PDF p.313

https://www.win.tue.nl/~rhofstad/SaintFlour_SPoRG_fin_Saint_Flour.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Prove metastable behavior of low-temperature ferromagnetic Ising dynamics on configuration models as n tends to infinity with inverse temperature fixed and large.

Scope

The source contrasts this with taking temperature to0 at fixed n. Later random-regular metastability results are known; the full general configuration-model extension is retained.

P0160-A001 | Fixed-temperature metastability for general configuration models structural reduction

Argument or result

A deterministic expansion condition gives an energy barrier. If every vertex set S of size at most n/2 has at least h|S| boundary edges, and the Hamiltonian is-J sum_edges sigma_u sigma_v-B sum_v sigma_v, then changing S from minus to plus costs2J|boundary S|-2B|S|, at least2(Jh-B)|S|. Every single-spin path from all minus to all plus crosses a set of size floor(n/2).

Check

Each boundary edge changes its interaction contribution by2J and each flipped spin changes its field contribution by-2B. The number of plus spins changes by1 per step, forcing the intermediate set size.

Remaining obstruction

A positive energy barrier is not by itself a fixed-temperature exit-time theorem because exponentially many configurations contribute entropy. One must compare beta times the barrier with entropy and control the metastable basin, with expansion estimates valid for the sampled graph.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0160-A002 | Energy plus entropy conductance bound

Argument or result

Let G have odd n and edge expansion |boundary S|>=h|S| for all |S|<=n/2. Use H(sigma)=-J sum_edges sigma_u sigma_v-B sum_v sigma_v with J>0,B>=0,Jh>B, and Gibbs law pi proportional to exp(-beta H). Consider any reversible single-spin continuous-time chain with each individual spin-flip rate at most1. Put A={number of plus spins< n/2}. Global spin reversal maps A bijectively onto its complement and does not decrease Gibbs weight, so pi(A)<=1/2. An A-to-complement jump must start with(n-1)/2 plus spins, whose energy exceeds the all-minus energy by at least(Jh-B)(n-1). There are at most2^n such states and total exit rate at most n per state. Since pi(A) is at least the all-minus weight, the conductance Phi=Q(A,A-complement)/pi(A) is at most n*2^n*exp[-beta(Jh-B)(n-1)]. Starting with pi conditioned on A, the time-s law is bounded above by pi/pi(A), by Markov-kernel positivity and stationarity. Consequently the expected number of A-to-complement jumps by time t is at most t*Phi. The probability of still being in A at time t is at least1-t*Phi, giving total variation distance at least1/2-t*Phi. Convexity of total variation implies the same lower bound for the worst starting state. Therefore t_mix(1/4)>=1/(4*Phi), at least exp[beta(Jh-B)(n-1)]/(4*n*2^n). This is exponential in n when beta(Jh-B)>log2. The calculation accounts explicitly for entropy. It is a deterministic expansion-based mixing lower bound; it does not prove a metastable exit-time distribution, or expansion for every configuration-model degree law.

Check

Boundary-layer counting and stationary domination give separate energy and time estimates.

Remaining obstruction

No general random-graph expansion theorem or metastable exit-time distribution is proved.

P0161  Potts free energy for general configuration-model degrees

Source: Van der Hofstad: Stochastic processes on random graphs, Saint-Flour2017 | Open Problem5.5, PDF p.314

https://www.win.tue.nl/~rhofstad/SaintFlour_SPoRG_fin_Saint_Flour.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Extend the ferromagnetic Potts Bethe free-energy and phase-diagram results from regular locally tree-like graphs to general configuration-model degree laws.

Scope

Can and van der Hofstad2503.17636 establish additional regular-graph and parameter-region results. A lower Bethe bound is not equality throughout the general heterogeneous phase diagram.

P0161-A001 | Potts free energy for general configuration-model degrees structural reduction

Argument or result

For a finite forest at zero field, Z=q^(number of components)*(exp(beta)+q-1)^(number of edges), by successively summing leaf colors. Applying that formula to a d-regular locally tree-like graph as if it were a forest predicts pressure log q+(d/2)log[(exp(beta)+q-1)/q]. For d>2 and large beta this is below beta*d/2, while the q monochromatic states alone imply pressure at least beta*d/2+log(q)/n.

Check

Leaf elimination gives one factor exp(beta)+q-1 per edge and q for each remaining component root. In a connected graph the monochromatic states have weight exp(beta*|E|), so their contribution is an exact lower bound.

Remaining obstruction

Local tree neighborhoods do not erase the linear number of global cycle constraints. Correct Bethe fixed-point selection, beyond the symmetric forest expression, remains essential.

P0162  Fourier entropy versus total influence

Source: O Donnell: Open problems in analysis of Boolean functions | PDF p.5, Fourier Entropy-Influence Conjecture

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1204.6447

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Bound the Shannon entropy of the squared Fourier coefficients of every Boolean function by a universal constant times its total influence.

Scope

The weaker min-entropy formulation is grouped here. Gonzalez,MacManus,Pereyra2606.00246 still calls the full statement a conjecture and proves additional classes.

P0162-A001 | Fourier entropy versus total influence exact calculation or structural obstruction

Argument or result

Discarding the Boolean constraint destroys the claim: the probability measure uniform on the n singleton subsets has entropy log2(n) but mean subset size1. It cannot be the squared spectrum of a Boolean function when n>=2.

Check

If all Fourier mass were on singletons, f=sum_i a_i*x_i with a_i=plus/minus1/sqrt(n). In f^2 the coefficient of x_i*x_j is2*a_i*a_j, nonzero for distinct i,j, whereas Boolean f requires f^2=1. This independently exhibits the violated Fourier convolution identity.

Remaining obstruction

Entropy versus mean cardinality alone is insufficient. Any proof must use the nonlinear identities imposed by f^2=1; no dimension-free inequality exploiting all of them is obtained.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1204.6447

Question

For a size-s DNF, concentrate all but epsilon of its squared Fourier mass on at most s raised to O(log(1/epsilon)) coefficients.

Scope

The weaker polynomial-in-s formulation is grouped. Lecomte-Tan2109.04525 improve read-dependent bounds, not the full assertion.

P0163-A001 | Polynomial Fourier concentration for DNF formulas exact calculation or structural obstruction

Argument or result

Let C_j be the indicator of clause j, and encode the DNF by f=1-2*product_j(1-C_j). Each consistent conjunction has Fourier l1 norm1, and any product of conjunction indicators is either another conjunction or zero. Inclusion-exclusion gives L=sum_S |fhat(S)|<=2^(s+1)-1. Keeping coefficients of magnitude at least epsilon/L leaves squared tail at most epsilon and uses at most L^2/epsilon coefficients.

Check

For discarded coefficients, sum a_S^2 <=(epsilon/L)*sum |a_S|<=epsilon. For retained coefficients, their number times epsilon/L is at most L. Thus the explicit exponential bound is valid.

Remaining obstruction

This handles every DNF but is exponentially larger than the requested polynomial bound. The expansion ignores cancellations among overlapping clauses.

P0164  Gaussian lower bound for noise stability of unbiased thresholds

Source: Kamath,Kumar,Manurangsi: When Majority Fails,2026 | Section1.4, Conjecture1; O-Donnell2012 p.5 weaker consequences

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.06590

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For an unbiased linear threshold f and rho in(0,1), prove Stab_rho(f)>=(2/pi)*arcsin(rho).

Scope

This is the authors refined conjecture, not the false finite-majority comparison. The degree-one Fourier lower bound2/pi and small-noise consequence are grouped as parts of this family.

P0164-A001 | Gaussian lower bound for noise stability of unbiased thresholds exact calculation or structural obstruction

Argument or result

For f=sign(S), S=sum_i w_i X_i with sum w_i^2=1 and no zero ties, Holder gives E|S|>=(E S^2)^(3/2)/(E S^4)^(1/2)>=1/sqrt(3), since E S^4=3-2*sum w_i^4<=3. Cauchy-Schwarz applied to E[fS]=sum_i w_i*fhat(i) yields sum_i fhat(i)^2>=1/3. Therefore Stab_rho(f)>=rho/3.

Check

Interpolate L2 between L1 and L4: 1=||S||2 <=||S||1^(1/3)||S||4^(2/3). Fourier noise stability is sum_S rho^|S|*fhat(S)^2, with nonnegative terms for rho>0. This verifies the bound without assuming regular weights.

Remaining obstruction

The elementary bound is weaker even than the established robust Khintchine bounds and far below the proposed arcsine curve. A central limit argument gives the arcsine law only when all normalized weights vanish; it does not compare arbitrary nonregular thresholds.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1204.6447

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

When r independent noisy copies of a common uniform bit string use the same odd Boolean function, is the agreement probability maximized by majority on some odd subset of coordinates?

Scope

The number of players and the noise parameter are one optimization problem. This differs from two-party erasure distillation and from minimizing threshold stability.

P0165-A001 | Optimal odd functions for many-player correlation distillation exact calculation or structural obstruction

Argument or result

Write rho=1-2*epsilon and m(x)=T_rho f(x). Conditional independence gives agreement probability E[((1+m)/2)^r+((1-m)/2)^r]. For r=2 this is(1+E m^2)/2, and for r=3 it is(1+3E m^2)/4. Since odd f is unbiased, Parseval gives E m^2=sum_nonemptyS rho^(2|S|)*fhat(S)^2<=rho^2. Dictators attain both bounds.

Check

Expand the two binomials; odd powers cancel. A dictator has m=rho*x_1 and equality in the Fourier bound. For r=4 the expression becomes(1+6E m^2+E m^4)/8, displaying a genuinely additional moment.

Remaining obstruction

The exact r=2,3 calculation does not optimize the higher moments jointly for r>=4. No argument shows that a subset-majority maximizes all those combinations.

Question

Scope

Bhattacharya2402.13952 and its March2026 revision concern random restrictions. That does not settle every original polynomial.

Argument or result

For d=1, write f=a0+sum_i a_i*x_i. Evaluating at coordinate signs that align with a_i and at their opposites gives |a0|+sum_i|a_i|<=1. Hence Var(f)=sum_i a_i^2<=max_i|a_i|, so max_i Inf_i(f)=max_i a_i^2>=Var(f)^2.

Check

The two extreme cube values are a0 plus/minus sum|a_i|. L2 influence for an affine polynomial is exactly a_i^2. The proof is dimension-free and uses the full range constraint.

In higher degree, cancellation among monomials prevents this direct l1-coefficient bound. The argument gives no polynomial-in-d control of the largest influence.

Question

Scope

The proposed centered-ball optimizer is not the general optimization question. Heilman1511.00382 gives negative results for ball/complement maximizers in dimension at least2; this does not identify every optimum.

P0167-A001 | Minimum correlation of symmetric Gaussian sets exact calculation or structural obstruction

Argument or result

For fixed A, put h=T_rho 1_A. The objective is integral_B h dgamma. Its minimum over sets of measure nu is attained by a lower-level set of h, with a suitable fraction of a level set if necessary. Since A is symmetric, h is even, so B can also be chosen symmetric.

Check

If B selects positive measure at a higher value of h while omitting equal measure at a lower value, exchanging the two decreases the integral. Applying the threshold at the nu-quantile proves the characterization. Gaussian measure is nonatomic, including after passing to antipodal pairs.

Remaining obstruction

This eliminates one set but leaves a nonlinear optimization over A. Symmetry alone does not make its smoothed indicator radial or force a ball.

Represent a correlated pair as(G1,rho*G1+sqrt(1-rho^2)*G2). Rotational symmetry in the plane gives sign-disagreement angle arccos(rho)/pi. The product of the two coordinate agreement events supplies the displayed four-cell benchmark.

This computes a non-simplex competitor exactly but does not order it against every partition. At rho=0 every equal-volume partition has stability1/q, so independence is not a uniqueness argument.

The stronger and weaker proposed constants are grouped. Jha1611.01029 and Wang2020/002 discuss partial cases and reformulations; neither title is treated as a complete resolution.

Under independent input bits with common mean t, F(t)=E_t f is a polynomial of degree at most d bounded by1 in absolute value for t in[-1,1]. Its derivative at0 is sum_i fhat(i). A proof through one-variable biased expectations must exploit additional constraints inherited from a Boolean function. Chebyshev is an obstruction to the relaxed method, not a Boolean counterexample.

P0170  Optimal limited independence for Gaussian polynomial thresholds

Source: O Donnell: Open problems in analysis of Boolean functions | PDF p.9, k-wise Independence for PTFs

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1204.6447

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Determine the smallest k(d,epsilon) for which every k-wise independent standard-Gaussian vector fools every degree-d polynomial threshold to error epsilon.

Scope

The joint distribution of every k coordinates must be product Gaussian. Matching finitely many mixed moments alone is a weaker property and is not substituted for this assumption.

P0170-A001 | Optimal limited independence for Gaussian polynomial thresholds exact calculation or structural obstruction

Argument or result

Take n=k+1 signs uniformly distributed subject to product_i sign_i=1, independent of n independent absolute standard Gaussians R_i. Then X_i=sign_i*R_i has k-wise independent standard-Gaussian coordinates. Yet product_i X_i>0 almost surely, while the same product for fully independent Gaussians is positive with probability1/2.

Check

Every assignment of any k signs has exactly one extension satisfying the parity constraint, so its probability is2^-k. Independent magnitudes then yield independent Gaussian marginals on that subset. The distinguishing polynomial has degree k+1.

Remaining obstruction

For epsilon<1/2 this proves the necessary bound k>=d. It is weaker than known quantitative lower bounds and supplies no sharp dependence on epsilon.

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For each fixed error delta>0, determine whether every distribution whose nonconstant parity biases are at most s^(-C_delta) fools every size-s DNF to error delta.

Scope

The2025 Generalised Linial-Nisan counterexample controls marginals of bounded-size coordinate sets; its theorem alone does not assert small bias for every parity. It is not treated as a resolution of this different source statement.

P0171-A001 | Polynomial small bias sufficient to fool DNF events exact calculation or structural obstruction

Argument or result

For a single conjunction C of k literals, its Fourier coefficients have magnitude2^-k on subsets of those k coordinates. An epsilon-biased distribution therefore changes E C by at most epsilon*(1-2^-k). Inclusion-exclusion for a union of s clauses gives the valid but crude error at most(2^s-1)*epsilon, since each nonempty intersection of consistent clauses is another conjunction.

Check

The constant coefficient contributes no expectation difference, while all other coefficients are paired with parity biases of magnitude at most epsilon. Expanding the union into its2^s-1 nonempty clause intersections proves the stated global bound without assuming independence of clauses.

Remaining obstruction

To obtain polynomial rather than exponential bias requirements, one needs a substantially more economical approximation or cancellation argument. Small errors for individual clause probabilities do not by themselves control their union.

Question

Scope

The false finite-majority comparator is excluded. This is the2026 authors refined conjecture. Requiring the optimizing second output to be unbiased is a distinct constraint; the source formulation here uses the absolute conditional expectation.

P0172-A001 | Sharp erasure-distillation bound from the Gaussian limit exact calculation or structural obstruction

Argument or result

Identify the conditional expectation with the multilinear extension f(Z), where each Z_i is0 with probability1-p and otherwise a fair sign. Orthogonality gives E f(Z)^2=sum_S p^|S|*fhat(S)^2<=p for unbiased f. Thus Cauchy-Schwarz yields E|f(Z)|<=sqrt(p). A parity on m coordinates instead has exact value p^m.

Check

If S and T differ, the expectation of Z_S Z_T vanishes because a differing coordinate has mean0. For S=T it equals p^|S|. Parseval and the absent constant coefficient give the bound. A parity survives only when all m relevant coordinates are retained.

The L2 estimate loses the proposed arcsine constant. Conditional distributions of the smoothed Boolean function, beyond variance alone, must be controlled.

Question

Scope

The exact symmetric-maximizer conjecture is false, with Chapman2108.02288 giving later counterexamples. The weaker asymptotic bounds explicitly posed in the source are grouped here.

P0173-A001 | Asymptotic influence bounds for polynomial thresholds exact calculation or structural obstruction

Argument or result

The desired C*d*sqrt(n) bound holds when the polynomial is a product of d affine linear forms with no cube zeros. Indeed its sign is a product of d threshold functions. If that product changes on a coordinate flip, at least one factor changes, so its influence is at most the sum of the factor influences. For a threshold g, unateness gives Inf_i(g)=|ghat(i)| and hence I(g)<=sqrt(n)*sqrt(sum_i ghat(i)^2)<=sqrt(n).

Check

For a monotone coordinate of a Boolean function, its discrete derivative is either0 or1 after the conventional half-difference, so its expectation equals its flip probability. Cauchy-Schwarz and Parseval then give the threshold bound. A union bound gives the product estimate.

Remaining obstruction

A general multivariate polynomial need not factor into real affine forms. The argument supplies a genuine subclass result, not a reduction for arbitrary polynomial thresholds.

P0174  Sharp support-diameter threshold for free Brownian unimodality

Source: Hasebe,Ueda: Large time unimodality for classical and free Brownian motions | Problem 3.3, arXiv PDF p.6; journal p.359

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1710.08240

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: candidate_complete_proof

Question

Find the optimal universal C such that every compactly supported law mu becomes unimodal after free convolution with the variance-t semicircle law for all t >= C times the square of its support diameter.

Scope

The source prints 2<=C<=4, but its own symmetric-Bernoulli threshold t=4 and diameter 2 imply only C>=1. The candidate proof derives both bounds independently. Manuscript header 2021 differs from the arXiv revision date 2018.

P0174-A001 | Centered fourth-moment diameter inequality and reweighted Cauchy-profile critical points

Argument or result

Candidate sharp answer C=1. For any centered law Q of support diameter D, with a=E X^2 and b=E X^4, a<=D^2/4 and b+3a^2<=D^2*a. Reweighting mu by ((x-u)^2+R^2)^(-2) at a stationary Cauchy profile gives y=R^2<=3a when the second derivative is nonnegative, and 1/F=(b+2ay+y^2)/(a+y)<=D^2. Equality forces symmetric endpoint Bernoulli and a strict fourth-order maximum. Thus no local minimum has F<=1/D^2; each level 1/t for t>=D^2 has at most two roots. Biane parametrization and the source level-set lemma imply unimodality. Symmetric Bernoulli gives sharpness.

Check

Second analytic route differentiates v_t directly at critical points. For symmetric Bernoulli on {-c,c}, v_t second derivative at 0 equals (4*c^2-t)/(t*sqrt(t-c^2)), so t<4*c^2=D^2 gives a positive density local minimum. The centered moment inequality follows independently from E[(X-Xprime)^4]<=D^2*E[(X-Xprime)^2]. These are same-author checks, not independent review.

Remaining obstruction

No mathematical gap currently identified in the full written candidate. Later-literature novelty is unverified and there is no independent proof review. Check the reweighting normalization, Jensen direction, equality case, and exact source density assumptions before promotion.

Question

Scope

The older AIM free-analysis question is the same family and is not counted again. A semicircle factor and some freely infinitely divisible subclasses are established cases.

P0175-A001 | Symmetric unimodality under free addition structural calculation

Argument or result

The classical proof by mixing symmetric unimodal components does not transfer by linearity: free convolution is not affine even on symmetric unimodal laws. Let mu=(delta_0+S(0,2))/2 and nu=S(0,1). The sixth moment of mu free-added to nu is67, whereas the mixture of the two component free convolutions has sixth moment70.

For mu, m2=1,m4=4,m6=20, so its free cumulants are k2=1,k4=2,k6=3. Adding nu changes k2 to2 only, giving m6=3+6*2*2+5*2^3=67. The affine mixture would be(S(0,1)+S(0,3))/2, with m6=(5+135)/2=70. Both mixture components and mu are symmetric unimodal.

Remaining obstruction

This disproves a proposed proof mechanism, not the unimodality conjecture. The actual free convolution in this example is covered by the known semicircle-factor theorem.

P0176-A001 | Existence of nontrivial freely strongly unimodal laws structural calculation

Argument or result

An explicit infinite-variance candidate also fails: the standard Cauchy law. Let nu=(1/20)*delta_0+(19/20)*Uniform[0,1000], a unimodal law with mode0. Cauchy free convolution equals classical convolution, whose density derivative, multiplied by pi, is -x/[10*(1+x^2)^2]+19/20000*[1/(1+x^2)-1/(1+(x-1000)^2)]. Its signs at0,1,250,1000 are respectively positive,negative,positive,negative, so the density has at least two peaks.

Check

At1 the negative term is-1/40 and the positive term is less than19/40000. At250, writing A=62501 and B=562501, the derivative is -25/A^2+475/(A*B), positive because19*A>B. At1000 both displayed contributions are negative. The initial law has a single atom at its mode and a constant density to its right.

Remaining obstruction

This gives a concrete Cauchy counterexample, consistent with the known strong-unimodality criterion, but does not exclude every infinite-variance law.

P0177  Location of Brownian modes at large time

Source: Hasebe-Ueda: Large time unimodality, published version | Problem 4.6

https://alea.math.cnrs.fr/articles/v15/15-15.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Estimate the mode of classical Brownian motion started from a law with a finite exponential quadratic moment, and determine analogous estimates for free Brownian motion.

Scope

The source gives a growing interval of radius sqrt(t)/2 once unimodality holds. Classical and free versions of this mode-location request are grouped.

P0177-A001 | Location of Brownian modes at large time structural calculation

Argument or result

For the classical mode, let m be the initial mean and mu_k=E[(X-m)^k]. The source exponential-moment assumption yields the asymptotic expansion mode(t)=m-mu_3/(2*t)+(mu_5-6*mu_2*mu_3)/(8*t^2)+O(t^-3). In particular the mode tends to the initial mean, and the leading displacement has sign opposite the third central moment.

Check

Set s=1/t and H(h,s)=E[(X-m-h)*exp(-s*(X-m-h)^2/2)]. The exponential quadratic moment makes H analytic near(0,0), with H_h=-1 there. The implicit function theorem gives h=a*s+b*s^2+O(s^3). Expanding H gives a=-mu_3/2 and b=mu_5/8-3*mu_2*mu_3/4. The associated density stationary point is a strict local maximum since H_h remains negative; the source large-time unimodality identifies it with the unique global mode.

Remaining obstruction

This is an author-checked asymptotic partial answer, with no novelty claim. It does not provide a uniform explicit finite-time error constant or solve the free-Brownian half.

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For a classical or free strictly alpha-stable process, alpha in(0,2), does a finite absolute(2+alpha)-moment of the initial law ensure eventual unimodality under the corresponding convolution?

Scope

Use the published wording: classical convolution with classical stable laws, free convolution with free stable laws. The archived preprint wording differs. All stability indices are one source conjecture; the Cauchy case is already established.

P0178-A001 | Critical moment condition for eventual stable-process unimodality structural calculation

Argument or result

A separated-atom counterexample of the source type cannot retain the critical moment while making its usual tail mechanism arbitrarily strong. If mu=sum_k w_k*delta_(a_k) and sum_k w_k*|a_k|^(2+alpha)<infinity, then w_k*|a_k|^(2+alpha) tends to0. Thus it cannot diverge along a subsequence, which is the condition used to make an isolated atom dominate the far-tail derivative of the main stable density.

Check

Every term of a convergent nonnegative series tends to0. In the Cauchy case alpha=1, the derivative of the bulk kernel at a_k has order |a_k|^-3, while the local atom contributes order w_k at any fixed smoothing time; their ratio is of order w_k*|a_k|^3. This matches the critical exponent in the exact known construction.

Remaining obstruction

Failure of that counterexample mechanism is not a uniform derivative estimate for arbitrary initial laws. Asymmetric stable laws and the free cases still require global control.

Scope

Surface groups are already covered by the source. The later2602.11905 free-product theorem concerns a different relation and does not settle this hyperbolic3-manifold group.
