Argument or result

Independent-permutation moment formulas cannot be inserted unchanged. Taking determinants in the relation forces sign(U)=sign(V). More fundamentally, the relator U^2*V^2*U^-1*V^-1 is identically I, so its normalized trace is1, whereas the corresponding nontrivial reduced free-group word has trace0 in the free regular representation.

The determinant equation is1=sign(V)*sign(U). The relator identity follows by multiplying U^2*V^2=VU on the right by U^-1*V^-1. Removing the constant vector leaves the identity on the remaining space, so the trace discrepancy persists.

The correct nonfree group trace respects the relator, but no asymptotic enumeration or outlier estimate for uniform constrained pairs is obtained.

P0181  Fixed-degree random Cayley graphs with optimal spectral gap

Source: The strong convergence phenomenon,2025 survey | Section 6.2, pp.65-66

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.00346

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Scope

Different field and rank regimes are grouped; growing-number-of-generator theorems do not answer the fixed-degree question.

P0181-A001 | Fixed-degree random Cayley graphs with optimal spectral gap structural calculation

Argument or result

Abelian groups cannot supply even a uniform fixed-degree gap in a basic test case. For arbitrary r generators a_i of Z/pZ, divide the r-dimensional phase cube into M^r boxes with M^r<p. Two of the p points k*(a_1,...,a_r)/p fall in one box. Their nonzero difference k gives a nontrivial character for which all generator phases have distance at most2*pi/M from zero. Hence its adjacency eigenvalue is at least2r-4*pi^2*r/M^2.

Check

Use cos(x)>=1-x^2/2 in the eigenvalue2*sum_i cos(2*pi*k*a_i/p). Taking M tending to infinity at order p^(1/r) forces the gap to0, regardless of how the generators were chosen.

Remaining obstruction

The argument identifies a structural obstruction to abelian candidates but supplies no optimal bound for the nonabelian special-linear families.

P0182  Strong convergence of high-dimensional representations of a fixed compact group

Source: The strong convergence phenomenon,2025 survey | Section 6.3, p.66

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.00346

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For independent Haar elements of a fixed compact semisimple group, do their growing irreducible representations converge strongly to free Haar generators, starting with symmetric powers of SU(2)?

Scope

The source already establishes weak convergence in broad generality. The SU(2) test case and broader fixed-group question are one family.

P0182-A001 | Strong convergence of high-dimensional representations of a fixed compact group structural calculation

Argument or result

The one-generator SU(2) case has an elementary complete answer. A Haar element has eigenvalues exp(plus/minus i*theta), with theta/pi irrational almost surely. Its N-th symmetric power has eigenvalues exp(i*(N-2k)*theta),0<=k<=N. These become dense on the unit circle, so every Laurent-polynomial norm converges to the supremum on the circle, the regular Z norm.

Check

Irrational rotation makes the first N+1 multiples of2*theta asymptotically dense; multiplying the set by exp(i*N*theta) preserves its maximal circular gap. Uniform continuity then proves norm convergence for each Laurent polynomial. Geometric sums give convergence of every nonzero normalized trace moment to0.

Remaining obstruction

For two or more independently sampled group elements, their representation eigenbases interact; the scalar circle-density argument no longer controls joint polynomial norms.

Weak freeness plus the usual adjacency norm is insufficient in general. Start with any strongly convergent r-unitary model and append a one-dimensional block in which every generator equals z=exp(i*theta), with cos(theta)=sqrt(2r-1)/r. This block does not change limiting traces and its adjacency eigenvalue is exactly2*sqrt(2r-1). Yet the phase-twisted adjacency polynomial has value2r on that block, above its free norm2*sqrt(2r-1).

Normalized trace weight of the added block tends to0. Multiplying each free Haar generator by a fixed phase preserves the free law and norm. Thus both asserted facts hold while a different linear polynomial has a persistent outlier.

Remaining obstruction

This is an obstruction to inferring strong convergence from one spectral-gap theorem; the constructed scalar blocks are not the actual LPS representation, so the source conjecture is not refuted.

P0184  Strong convergence of modular-group actions on finite projective lines

Source: The strong convergence phenomenon,2025 survey | Section 6.4, p.67

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.00346

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does the PSL_2(Z) action on P^1(F_q), restricted to constants-orthogonal vectors, converge strongly to the regular group representation for every fixed tuple of integer matrices?

Scope

The diffusion-operator formulation and arbitrary-polynomial formulation are equivalent in the source and are counted once. Random homomorphisms from C2*C3 are a different model.

P0184-A001 | Strong convergence of modular-group actions on finite projective lines structural calculation

Argument or result

Weak convergence follows directly from fixed points. For a fixed nonidentity matrix X in PSL_2(Z), its fixed points on P^1(F_q) solve a projective quadratic equation. Except for finitely many primes where X becomes scalar, there are at most two fixed points. The normalized trace in the q-dimensional constants-orthogonal representation is therefore(fix(X)-1)/q, which tends to0.

Check

For X represented by entries a,b,c,d, finite fixed points satisfy c*z^2+(d-a)*z-b=0, with the point at infinity handled projectively. A nonzero quadratic on the projective line has at most two zeros. Applying this to each word yields the regular trace moments. Faithfulness of the regular trace then gives the lower polynomial-norm bound by even moments.

Remaining obstruction

Moment convergence gives no matching upper bound on operator norms; sparse exceptional eigenvalues remain the exact obstruction.

Question

Can permutation models satisfy the free polynomial norm upper bound with no error for every polynomial of degree at most q(N) tending to infinity, or for each fixed polynomial along a chosen model?

Scope

The per-polynomial weaker question and simultaneous-degree version are grouped. The source excludes a bound simultaneously for every degree at fixed dimension.

P0185-A001 | Ramanujan inequalities for growing families of matrix polynomials structural calculation

Argument or result

The one-generator amenable case satisfies the exact inequality at every degree: take an N-cycle permutation and remove its constant eigenvector. Its remaining eigenvalues lie on the unit circle, so the norm of any Laurent polynomial is at most its supremum on that circle, exactly the norm in the regular representation of Z.

Check

A normal finite matrix is unitarily diagonalizable, and Laurent-polynomial norms are maxima over its eigenvalues. This proves the bound without an asymptotic error or probability qualifier.

Remaining obstruction

For r>=2 the free reduced group algebra is noncommutative and has no finite-dimensional faithful representation. The circle argument gives no construction for any diverging degree window in that setting.

P0186-A001 | Largest coefficient dimension compatible with strong convergence structural calculation

Argument or result

Expected outlier counts can fail as a method even when the extreme eigenvalue converges in probability. Let E_N have probability p_N tending to0, and take X_N=1_(E_N)*I_(D_N). Then its norm tends to0 in probability, but the expected number of eigenvalues above1/2 equals D_N*p_N. With D_N=exp(N) and p_N=exp(-N/2), that count diverges.

Check

The event norm(X_N)>1/2 is exactly E_N, whereas the count on that event is exactly D_N. This isolates multiplicity rather than a failure of norm convergence.

Remaining obstruction

The example does not refute strong convergence in the requested models. It explains why a trace-statistic proof needs a different mechanism once coefficient dimension creates large multiplicities.

P0187-A001 | Optimal edge-fluctuation scales for arbitrary random-matrix polynomials structural calculation

Argument or result

The nondegeneracy qualifier is necessary even for a one-variable quadratic polynomial. For a standard GUE matrix G_N let P(x)=20-(x-2)^2. With probability tending to1 its spectrum lies in a fixed neighborhood of[-2,2] where P is positive. Then norm(P(G_N))=20-min_i(lambda_i-2)^2. Since the largest GUE eigenvalue is2+O_p(N^-2/3), one has0<=20-norm(P(G_N))<=O_p(N^-4/3).

The upper bound uses the largest eigenvalue as one candidate in the minimum. Spectral mapping and positivity give the norm identity. Consequently N^(2/3)*(norm(P(G_N))-20) tends to0, rather than a nondegenerate Tracy-Widom variable.

The example is deliberately degenerate and does not contradict the stated research direction. A correct general theorem must identify regularity of the polynomial edge before asserting the universal scale.

P0188  Joint strong convergence for permutation and deterministic matrices

Source: The strong convergence phenomenon,2025 survey | Section 6.8, p.69

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.00346

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Scope

Removing the common constant vector of permutation matrices requires an explicit convention for deterministic matrices that do not preserve its orthogonal complement.

P0188-A001 | Joint strong convergence for permutation and deterministic matrices structural calculation

Argument or result

Naively compressing arbitrary deterministic matrices creates persistent defects. Let e be the normalized all-ones vector, P=I-ee*, and B a balanced diagonal matrix with entries plus/minus1. B strongly converges to the symmetric Bernoulli law. But on e-perp, PBP has the zero eigenvector Be, while all its other eigenvalues are plus/minus1. Thus norm(I_(e-perp)-(PBP)^2)=1, whereas the corresponding Bernoulli-limit polynomial is zero.

Check

Because B^2=I and e*Be=0, PBP(Be)=PB(e)=Pe=0. More generally (PBP)^2-PB^2P=-PBee*BP, whose norm here is1. Its normalized trace tends to0 but its operator norm does not.

Remaining obstruction

This refutes the naive unchanged-deterministic-limit formulation after compression, not every possible counterpart requested by the survey. Appropriate invariant subspaces or additional deterministic hypotheses remain to be formulated and proved.

P0189  Brown-measure convergence from general strong convergence

Source: The strong convergence phenomenon,2025 survey | Section 6.9, pp.69-70

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.00346

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: candidate_complete_counterexample

Question

Does strong convergence of a family of random matrices imply convergence of empirical complex-eigenvalue measures of every noncommutative polynomial to its limiting Brown measure?

Scope

This is the literal general statement. It does not impose independent entries, independence of the matrix family, or a free limiting family. The narrower Ginibre-with-adjoints question is recorded as a remaining subclass, not counted again.

P0189-A001 | Brown-measure convergence from general strong convergence structural calculation

Argument or result

Complete candidate counterexample: rational clock/shift unitary pairs U_j,V_j approach an irrational rotation pair u,v strongly, while U_j^q=I and V_j^q=-I force det(zI-U_j-V_j)=z^q. Hence every finite sum is nilpotent with empirical measure delta_0. The established Brown measure of u+v is Haar measure on the unit circle. Simultaneous Haar conjugation makes the finite family random without changing any of these statements.

Check

The strong-convergence proof maps the simple irrational rotation algebra into the product of matrix algebras modulo norm-null sequences; injectivity gives every subsequential limsup polynomial norm and therefore the full limit. Normalized word traces are checked directly. The determinant calculation independently checks nilpotency. Full proof and all source dependencies are in the proof packet.

Remaining obstruction

No gap presently identified in the literal-general-scope construction, but there is no independent review and no novelty claim. It does not resolve independent Ginibre polynomials with adjoints; the June2026 holomorphic-Ginibre theorem is compatible with this example.

Hermitization exposes a missing hypothesis. Weak singular-value convergence and bounded largest singular value do not control the logarithmic potential: nu_N=(1-1/N)*delta_1+(1/N)*delta_(exp(-N)) converges weakly to delta_1 and has bounded support, but integral log(s) dnu_N=-1 rather than0. Thus a comparison based only on bounded polynomial spectral statistics cannot justify passage through the logarithm.

P0191  Least singular values at the polynomial constant term

Source: Yi Han: Brown measure convergence for the spectrum of polynomials in Ginibre matrices | Question 1.6

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.01664

Question

Extend the polynomial least-singular-value bound for a nonzero holomorphic polynomial of independent complex Ginibre matrices to the shift equal to its constant term.

Scope

The source theorem excludes that single shift. Qualitative invertibility and a quantitative polynomial lower-tail estimate are kept distinct.

P0191-A001 | Least singular values at the polynomial constant term structural calculation

Argument or result

For a monomial P=X_(i1)...X_(id), the missing shift is0 and the estimate reduces to the individual matrices. Since smin(P)>=product_k smin(X_(ik)), the event smin(P)<=epsilon implies at least one factor has smin<=epsilon^(1/d). The standard complex-Ginibre bound then gives P(smin(P)<=epsilon)<=C*d*N^2*epsilon^(2/d). Repeated factors require no independence in this union bound.

For normalized complex Ginibre, N*smin has the usual bounded quadratic lower tail. Multiply the deterministic singular-value inequalities successively, and apply the union bound over the d factor positions.

Remaining obstruction

Sums of monomials can cancel, so this argument gives no least-singular-value estimate for an arbitrary noncommutative polynomial at its constant term.

P0192  Brown-measure universality beyond complex entry densities

Source: Yi Han: Brown measure convergence for the spectrum of polynomials in Ginibre matrices | Question 1.7 and first part of Question 1.8

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.01664

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Extend Brown-measure convergence for holomorphic noncommutative polynomials to real Gaussian or discrete entry laws such as Bernoulli, allowing complex polynomial coefficients.

Scope

Real-valued and discrete entry versions are grouped as one source universality family. The source bounded-density-on-C theorem does not cover either automatically.

P0192-A001 | Brown-measure universality beyond complex entry densities structural calculation

Argument or result

Every one-matrix holomorphic polynomial is already reducible to the circular law. If X_N has a circular-law limit and P is a fixed complex polynomial, spectral mapping gives mu_(P(X_N))=P_pushforward(mu_(X_N)) exactly, including algebraic multiplicities. The continuous mapping theorem therefore yields the pushforward of the circular law. The Brown measure of P(c) is the same pushforward, by factorizing P(z)-lambda and using multiplicativity of the Fuglede-Kadison determinant.

Check

For each test function h bounded and continuous, h composed with P is bounded and continuous, so weak convergence passes through P. At the logarithmic-potential level, P(c)-lambda factors into commuting(c-root_j) terms, and determinant multiplicativity gives the same transformed Brown measure. Existing real-Gaussian and Bernoulli circular laws supply the initial one-matrix convergence.

Remaining obstruction

For several noncommuting input matrices there is no joint eigenvalue spectral mapping. Arithmetic and structural dependence in least-singular-value estimates remain unresolved here.

P0193  Eventual log-unimodality from arbitrary compact positive initial laws

Source: Hasebe-Ueda-Wang: Log-unimodality for free positive multiplicative Brownian motion | Problem 4.7, PDF p.11; Lemma 4.1, p.8

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.13848

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: candidate_complete_counterexample

Question

If nu is supported on a compact interval [alpha,beta] with 0<alpha<beta, must sigma_t free-multiplied by nu be log-unimodal for all sufficiently large times?

Scope

This asks whether the support-ratio restriction in Theorem 4.4 can be removed. Log-unimodality is unimodality after taking logarithms. The known positive theorem assumes a multiplicatively symmetric log-unimodal initial law; the question here allows non-log-unimodal initial laws.

Dated literature checks

Focused primary-source searches2026-09-06 found no verified later resolution. The arXiv page has one2020version and a2022journal reference. Author publication pages and Auer2026 concern related results, not a verified resolution here. Search absence is not current-open evidence.

P0193-A001 | Four exact crossings of the multiplicative Cauchy-profile criterion

Argument or result

Candidate negative answer: nu=(delta_(1/10)+delta_10)/2 yields failure of log-unimodality for every t>=5*pi. Set a=log10 and K_R(s)=1/[2*(cosh(s)-cosR)], F_R(u)=(K_R(u-a)+K_R(u+a))/2. Choose the unique R in[pi/2,pi) such that R/sinR=t/10. Lemma4.1 then asks whether F_R(u)=1/10 has at most two roots. But F_R(0)<=10/101<1/10, F_R(+-a)>=1/8>1/10, and F_R tends to0 at both infinities. Continuity gives four distinct roots, contradicting the necessary condition for log-unimodality.

Check

Same-author second algebra route in r: at r=1 the integral is10/(101-20*cosR)<=10/101; at r=10 and1/10 the resonant atom alone contributes1/[4*(1-cosR)]>=1/8; endpoint limits are0. The four roots lie in(0,1/10),(1/10,1),(1,10),(10,infinity). Monotonicity of R/sinR follows from(sinR-R*cosR)prime=R*sinR>0.

Remaining obstruction

No gap currently identified in the full written argument. Later-literature novelty and independent review remain unverified. Check the exact necessity direction in Lemma4.1, the time normalization, and whether the published question changed from the archived version before promotion.

P0194  Polynomial mixing with plus Ising boundary conditions

Source: Levin-Peres: Markov Chains and Mixing Times, second edition | Chapter26 Question1, PDFp375

https://pages.uoregon.edu/dlevin/MARKOV/mcmt2e.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Is Glauber mixing on the n-by-n square with plus boundary polynomial in n at every fixed positive temperature?

Scope

The2026 phase-ordering paper still uses a quasi-polynomial plus-boundary estimate. Its biased random initialization theorem is not a worst-start plus-boundary polynomial bound. Critical and high-temperature established cases are not counted separately.

P0194-A001 | Polynomial mixing with plus Ising boundary conditions explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

The direct disagreement coupling proves the high-temperature part. Write N=n^2 and c=4*tanh(beta). A single disagreement vanishes if its site is updated, while at most four neighboring updates create disagreement with probability at most tanh(beta). Thus the expected Hamming distance contracts by1-(1-c)/N when c<1. Iteration and the coupling inequality give tmix(1/4)<=N*log(4*N)/(1-c), up to integer rounding.

Check

Changing one neighboring spin changes the heat-bath plus probability by at most tanh(beta), since the conditional probability is(1+tanh(h))/2. A path coupling along single-spin differences extends the bound to arbitrary initial pairs. Boundary sites have at most four variable neighbors, so the estimate remains valid with fixed plus boundary.

Remaining obstruction

For4*tanh(beta)>=1 the coefficient ceases to contract. This is a failure of the local metric argument, not a lower bound showing slow mixing; the low-temperature interface regime remains unresolved here.

P0195  Monotonicity of the ferromagnetic Ising spectral gap

Source: Levin-Peres: Markov Chains and Mixing Times, second edition | Chapter26 Question2, PDFp375

https://pages.uoregon.edu/dlevin/MARKOV/mcmt2e.pdf

Question

Does increasing any ferromagnetic interaction decrease the Glauber spectral gap? What stronger time-dependent total-variation monotonicity holds?

Scope

Temperature, edge addition and individual-coupling versions are grouped. The source has zero field in its general formula. Cycle and mean-field special cases are established in the cited literature.

P0195-A001 | Monotonicity of the ferromagnetic Ising spectral gap explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

The two-spin calculation supports the conjecture exactly. With interaction J>=0, let c=tanh(J) and update either spin with probability1/2. The eigenfunctions1, sigma1+sigma2, sigma1-sigma2, sigma1*sigma2-c have eigenvalues1,(1+c)/2,(1-c)/2,0. Hence the spectral gap is(1-tanh(J))/2, strictly decreasing for finite J.

Check

Conditionally E[sigma1|sigma2]=c*sigma2. Applying the update kernel to the four displayed basis functions gives the eigenvalues directly. The stationary mean of sigma1*sigma2 is c, so the last function is centered.

Remaining obstruction

For a general graph, increasing J changes both the reversible measure and the Dirichlet form. Pointwise comparison of transition probabilities alone does not order the Rayleigh quotient because its variance denominator also changes.

P0196  Sharp comparison of systematic and random Ising updates

Source: Levin-Peres: Markov Chains and Mixing Times, second edition | Chapter26 Question3, PDFpp375-376

https://pages.uoregon.edu/dlevin/MARKOV/mcmt2e.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For ferromagnetic Ising dynamics, compare the number of updates in a systematic sweep order with random-scan mixing, with universal upper constant and lower logarithmic loss as posed in the source.

Scope

The two inequalities are one comparison problem. General Gibbs-system polynomial comparison is established by Gaitonde-Mossel2025; their counterexamples for arbitrary target laws do not by themselves refute the ferromagnetic Ising scope.

P0196-A001 | Sharp comparison of systematic and random Ising updates explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

At infinite temperature one deterministic sweep gives an exact independent fair-spin sample. Random scan has mixing time asymptotic to(N/2)*log N. The upper bound follows from Fourier eigenvalues1-|S|/N: chi-square distance is sum_(k>=1) binom(N,k)*(1-k/N)^(2t) <=(1+exp(-2t/N))^N-1. For the lower bound, magnetization from all plus has mean N*(1-1/N)^t and variance at most N, whereas stationary magnetization has variance N. Thus at t=(N/2)*(log N-C) the means are separated by order exp(C/2)*sqrt(N).

Check

For distinct spins, E[sigma_i*sigma_j]=(1-2/N)^t <=(1-1/N)^(2t), so their covariance is nonpositive and the variance bound follows. Chebyshev separates the two magnetization laws as C grows. This independently recovers the factor log N between random updates and one sweep.

Remaining obstruction

The product case shows why the logarithmic allowance is necessary. Interacting conditional-expectation projections fail to commute, so their product cannot be replaced by the power of their average.

Time counts individual random-site updates. This concerns a relation of orders, distinct from the separate question of whether there is cutoff.

P0197-A001 | Relation between Ising relaxation and mixing on transitive graphs explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

At zero interaction the heat-bath operator has eigenvalues1-k/N with multiplicity binom(N,k), so trel=N and tmix is asymptotic to(N/2)*log N. This exhibits the proposed relation, and also shows that the logarithm comes from many coordinate modes rather than a single slow eigenfunction. A gap estimate alone gives only tmix<=trel*log(4/pi_min); at beta0, pi_min=2^-N, which overestimates by a factor of order N/log N.

Check

The largest nonconstant eigenvalue is1-1/N. Summing the full spectral multiplicities yields the sharper chi-square expression(1+exp(-2t/N))^N-1 as an upper bound; retaining only the gap loses the binomial structure.

Remaining obstruction

Transitivity of vertices does not itself identify the multiplicities or nonlinear slow observables of the interacting spin configuration chain. A comparison preserving this spectral structure is missing.

Salez2025 repeats this as a current challenge. Optimal Ramanujan expansion is an established special case. This is not the random-Cayley spectral-gap question P0181.

P0198-A001 | Cutoff on bounded-degree transitive expanders explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

The assumptions already imply mixing of order log N and the usual product condition. From any vertex, after t steps the support lies in a ball of at most(Delta+1)^t vertices, so total variation is at least1-(Delta+1)^t/N. Thus tmix(1/4)>=log(3*N/4)/log(Delta+1). A uniform positive lazy spectral gap gamma gives tmix(1/4)<=gamma^-1*log(4*N), while trel<=gamma^-1.

Check

The lower bound tests the reachable set against uniform stationarity; transitivity makes the graph regular. The upper bound is the standard reversible L2 contraction. Their ratio is bounded and trel/tmix tends to0.

Remaining obstruction

These bounds establish only a bounded transition-time ratio, not a ratio tending to1. They provide no concentration of the relevant entropic or hitting-time clock, which is the unresolved step.

P0199  Cutoff for cyclic-to-random transpositions

Source: Levin-Peres: Markov Chains and Mixing Times, second edition | Chapter26 Question6(a), PDFp376

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does the shuffle that swaps the cyclically chosen position with a uniform random position have cutoff?

Scope

This is a time-inhomogeneous deck chain or a chain sampled by full sweeps. It differs from the cyclic adjacent-transposition shuffle, whose cutoff was proved by Nam-Nestoridi.

P0199-A001 | Cutoff for cyclic-to-random transpositions explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

The one-card marginal admits an exact update that may identify candidate slow modes. If position i is chosen deterministically and the tracked-card probabilities are p_j, then p_i becomes1/N, while p_j becomes(1-1/N)*p_j+p_i/N for j different from i. Thus a centered vector z has new z_i=0 and new z_j=(1-1/N)*z_j+z_i/N. A sweep is the explicit product of these N linear maps.

Check

If the card is at i it moves uniformly; if it is at j different from i, it stays unless the random partner is j. Summing the two cases gives the formula, which preserves the uniform distribution. Adding a deterministic phase as a state coordinate would give a periodic phase marginal and cannot justify ordinary mixing of the augmented chain.

Remaining obstruction

The one-card product can supply lower-bound observables, but does not control correlations of all cards or produce a matching sharp upper bound for the full permutation.

A coupon-collector strong-stationary-time argument fails even after every label has been updated. For three cards initially123, update labels1,2,3 once each. P0201  Optimal Glauber mixing near the coloring degree threshold

Source: Levin-Peres: Markov Chains and Mixing Times, second edition | Chapter26 Question7, PDFp376

https://pages.uoregon.edu/dlevin/MARKOV/mcmt2e.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does proper-coloring Glauber dynamics mix in order N*log N whenever the color count exceeds Delta+2 on graphs of maximum degree Delta?

Scope

The book asks strictly more than Delta+2. Nearby formulations at Delta+2 are grouped. Later2026 results for high-girth graphs do not settle unrestricted graphs; vertex coloring is different from edge coloring.

P0201-A001 | Optimal Glauber mixing near the coloring degree threshold explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

The elementary Hamming path coupling stops at q>2*Delta. For configurations differing at one vertex, updating that vertex removes the disagreement. Updating one of its at most Delta neighbors creates disagreement with probability at most1/(q-Delta), by maximally coupling the two uniform available-color sets. Therefore E[H_next]<=1-1/N+Delta/[N*(q-Delta)], which contracts precisely when q>2*Delta.

Check

The two available-color sets differ by at most one exchanged color, each has at least q-Delta elements, and their uniform laws have total variation at most1/(q-Delta). Iterating the path coupling yields an N*log N bound with constant proportional to(q-Delta)/(q-2*Delta).

Remaining obstruction

The denominator degenerates before the source threshold. Exploiting local color availability or multi-vertex moves is necessary; the failed bound is not a counterexample to rapid mixing.

The2018 spectral-embedding paper still states this as an open question. Relaxation bounds of this order do not automatically prove the mixing bound.

P0202-A001 | Diameter-squared upper bound for transitive mixing explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

The cycle illustrates the missing spectral information. On the N-cycle, lazy eigenvalues are1-sin^2(pi*k/N). Since sin(pi*k/N)>=2*k/N for k<=N/2, the chi-square bound from a point is at most2*sum_(k>=1) exp(-8*t*k^2/N^2). At t=C*N^2 this sum is as small as desired for a universal C, proving the proposed diameter-squared order in this case without a log N penalty.

Check

Use symmetry k versus N-k and(1-x)^(2t)<=exp(-2tx). The Gaussian series converges and tends to0 as C increases. The cycle diameter is floor(N/2) and its degree is2.

Remaining obstruction

For a general transitive graph, the gap alone bounds all N-1 modes by the same exponential and introduces log N. A uniform bound on low-frequency multiplicities or spectral mass strong enough to remove that factor is missing.

Question

Determine the mixing order for the walk on GL_N(2) that adds one uniformly chosen distinct row to another.

Scope

The same question on the Peres author page is counted once. The book phrase saying order2^N nodes is a typo; the group size is of order2^(N^2). The asymptotic question concerns N tending to infinity.

P0203-A001 | Mixing exponent of uniform binary row additions explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

The entropy/support lower bound has an exact leading constant. After t steps at most[N*(N-1)]^t states can be reached. Since |GL_N(2)|=2^(N^2)*product_(j=1)^N(1-2^-j), and the product tends to a positive constant, total variation is at least1-[N*(N-1)]^t/|GL_N(2)|. Thus tmix(1/4)>=(N^2*log2-O(1))/log[N*(N-1)], asymptotically at least(log2/2)*N^2/log N.

Check

The group-size formula chooses successive linearly independent columns: product_(k=0)^(N-1)(2^N-2^k). The infinite product is positive because sum2^-j converges; log(1-x) is bounded below by-2x for0<=x<=1/2 after treating the first factor separately.

Remaining obstruction

Counting update words supplies no upper bound because many words cancel or represent the same matrix. The known O(N^3) upper bound leaves a logarithmic and polynomial gap.

P0204  Mixing on large monotone subsets of the hypercube

Source: Yuval Peres: related open Markov-chain problems | Related open problems, Question1

https://www.yuval-peres-books.com/markov-chains-and-mixing-times/

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For an increasing subset V of the binary N-cube with at least half its vertices, is lazy induced-subgraph random-walk mixing always O(N*log N)?

Scope

The author page gives an O(N^3) theorem of Ding-Mossel and conjectures N*log N. This is the induced graph walk, whose stationary law is degree weighted, not automatically uniform.

P0204-A001 | Mixing on large monotone subsets of the hypercube explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

Every such induced graph is connected and has diameter at most2*N: from any x in V one may flip zero coordinates upwards to the all-one vertex while remaining in V. Every vertex has degree at least the number of its zero coordinates, but the degrees need not be constant. For V equal to the cube with only the all-zero vertex removed, a unit-vector vertex has degree N-1 while a vertex of Hamming weight at least2 has degree N (for N>=3). Thus uniform stationarity is already false in an almost-full monotone set.

Check

For an undirected graph, detailed balance holds with pi(x)=deg(x)/sum_y deg(y). On an edge between a unit vector and a weight-two vertex, a uniform law would have unequal transition flow1/[2*(N-1)] versus1/(2*N). The up-path connectivity argument is deterministic and uses only monotonicity.

Remaining obstruction

Applying full-cube product Fourier estimates without correcting the stationary law is invalid. Diameter and volume alone give much weaker general bounds; the monotone geometry must control bottlenecks quantitatively.

P0205  Metropolis relaxation for geometric-time Cayley targets

Source: Aldous open problems: Metropolis on Cayley graphs | Questions(i)-(iii), first posted March2009

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/cayley.html

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: partial_counterexample_author_checked

Question

Control the relaxation time of Metropolis random walk targeting the distribution of a Cayley walk at an independent geometric time; in particular, test monotonic decrease in the geometric parameter.

Scope

The source defines p in[0,1] but writes tau(infinity) in its proposed bounds; that reference parameter is undefined and is retained as a source ambiguity. The monotonicity question itself is precise. Its negative answer below is a partial answer to the grouped request for general bounds.

P0205-A001 | Metropolis relaxation for geometric-time Cayley targets explicit structural calculation

Argument or result

Monotone decrease is false already on the two-element Cayley group, with relaxation defined by1/(1-lambda_2). For the nonlazy base walk that flips states, mu_p=(1/(2-p),(1-p)/(2-p)), and its Metropolis matrix is[[p,1-p],[1,0]]. Its second eigenvalue is p-1, hence tau(p)=1/(2-p), which is strictly increasing. The same conclusion survives a lazy base walk: the base matrix has all entries1/2, mu_p=((1+p)/2,(1-p)/2), the Metropolis off-diagonal rates are(1-p)/(2*(1+p)) and1/2, and tau(p)=1+p.

Check

For the flip walk, sum the geometric probabilities over even versus odd times. For the lazy base, X_0 is the identity and X_k is uniform for every k>=1. The nontrivial eigenvalue of a two-state stochastic matrix is1 minus the sum of its off-diagonal probabilities, giving both formulas directly. If laziness is imposed after forming the nonlazy Metropolis kernel, tau becomes2/(2-p), still increasing.

Remaining obstruction

The literal monotonicity direction is refuted under the standard spectral-gap relaxation convention, but this does not answer the other universal-bound questions or resolve the source undefined tau(infinity). If relaxation meant inverse absolute gap for a nonlazy chain, that convention differs; the lazy example has nonnegative spectrum and removes that ambiguity. Prior-art status remains unverified.

Source: Aldous-Fill: Reversible Markov Chains and Random Walks on Graphs, 2002 recompiled2014 | Open Problem7.27, PDFp259; definitionp254

Question

On arc-transitive n-vertex graphs other than n-cycles, are maximal commute time O(n log n), relaxation time O(n), and the maximal-commute to twice stationary-average-hitting ratio asymptotic to1?

Scope

The three linked bounds are one question. The source definition has a variable-assignment typo; the intended standard ordered-edge transitivity is used, as in the surrounding tori examples. Wreath graphs themselves are established prior art.

Dated literature checks

Focused primary searches2026-09-06 found source question and related established results, but no verified later disposition. Absence from search is not proof of current openness or novelty.

P0208-A001 | Extremal arc-transitive walk parameters away from cycles explicit counterexample

Argument or result

For odd m>=5 the established wreath graph C_m[edgeless K2] is simple, connected,4-regular and arc-transitive on n=2m vertices. Its base projection is cycle walk. Exact hitting means are j*(m-j)+m between different fibers and2m between twins. Thus tau_star=2*floor(m^2/4)+2m, tau0=(m^2-1)/6+m, and tau2=1/(1-cos(2*pi/m)). The first two scale quadratically in n, and tau_star/(2*tau0) tends to3/2, refuting all three proposed bounds.

Check

The invariant fiber-constant and fiber-antisymmetric subspaces give the full transition spectrum: cycle eigenvalues plus m zeros. Its fundamental trace agrees with the direct stationary hitting-time sum. Neighbor hitting equals2m-1=n-1 as required by the source lemma.

Remaining obstruction

Verify prior disposition of this1994 question: the graph construction is classical. The source typo and rate-one convention are explicit. No mathematical gap found by the author, but independent review and novelty remain pending.

P0209  Uniform stationary normal approximation from gap and bounded observable

Source: Aldous-Fill: Reversible Markov Chains and Random Walks on Graphs, 2002 recompiled2014 | Open Problem4.30, PDFp137; continuous ratesp26

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/RWG/book.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: candidate_complete_counterexample

Question

For stationary finite reversible continuous-time chains and Epi g=0,Epi g^2=1, does a universal normal-approximation error bound psi(||g||infinity,t/tau2) tend to0 at fixed first argument?

Scope

The source uses asymptotic variance normalization, permits arbitrary continuous transition rates, and starts in stationarity. The counterexample fixes ||g||infinity exactly2 and also survives uniformizing rates to at most1.

Dated literature checks

Focused primary searches2026-09-06 found source question and related established results, but no verified later disposition. Absence from search is not proof of current openness or novelty.

P0209-A001 | Uniform stationary normal approximation from gap and bounded observable explicit counterexample

Argument or result

Let epsilon=s^-2,R=s^4, Y have rates0to1=epsilon,1to0=1-epsilon, and independently let fair sign Z flip at R/2. Gap=1. Set A=2*epsilon+sqrt((1-epsilon)*(1-4*epsilon)),B=(2-A)/(1-epsilon),g=A*Z+B*(Y-epsilon). Then mean0,variance1,supnorm2 exactly and sigma^2=2*A^2/R+2*B^2*epsilon*(1-epsilon). By time s the chance ever Y=1 is at most epsilon*(s+1); off that event the slow integral is negligible after sigma*sqrt(s) normalization. The fast normalized variance is at most A^2/[R*B^2*epsilon*(1-epsilon)] tending0. Thus the normalized integral tends in probability to0, with normal Kolmogorov distance liminf at least1/2, although s/tau2 tends to infinity.

Check

The product generator has eigenvalues0,-1,-R,-(R+1). Exact covariance integration verifies sigma and confirms finite-time variance divided by sigma^2*s tends to1 despite distributional collapse: rare events destroy uniform integrability. Scaling Q to Q/R and time to R*s leaves the standardized law and time/gap ratio unchanged, and bounds outgoing rates by1.

Remaining obstruction

No gap identified in the written quantifier and normalization checks. Current literature and independent review remain unverified; no original full-solution promotion.

P0210  Martingales detectable by computationally practical stopping rules

Source: Aldous open problems: Martingale for practical purposes | Problem and polynomial-constraint formulation

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/fields.html

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Give a useful probabilistic definition of martingale for practical purposes, or a tractable class defined by polynomially many stopping-time constraints.

Scope

The two formulations are grouped as one conceptual question. A complexity model and input representation are not specified by the source; no financial claim or backtest is involved.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0210-A001 | Martingales detectable by computationally practical stopping rules structural calculation

Argument or result

Any prescribed polynomial-size family of stopping tests can miss bounded conditional drift on an exponentially large history space. Reveal m fair bits by time m, set X_t=0 through time m and X_(m+1)=f(Y). For predetermined stopping rules T_j, each optional-mean constraint is the linear equation E[f(Y)*1_{T_j=m+1}]=0. If the number of tests plus the mean-zero condition is less than2^m, a nonzero f exists in their common nullspace. Rescale it to supnorm1. Then the process passes every supplied test but E[X_(m+1)|F_m]=f(Y) is nonzero.

Check

The witness T*=m+1 on{f(Y)>0} and m otherwise is a stopping time and has E X_T*=E f_+>0. Predetermined test rules are functions of the revealed-bit history; their final-time continuation indicators do not depend on the unknown terminal value. The construction does not claim f or T* is computationally efficient.

Remaining obstruction

A polynomial number of linear tests alone cannot reproduce the full martingale property. Selecting a useful computational subclass requires an explicit representation, complexity bound and error tolerance; the nullspace construction does not supply that theory.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

Question

Determine the order of the relaxation time for the reversible polymer coagulation-fragmentation chain, especially fragmentation rate beta_n comparable to1/n.

Scope

Clusters merge at rate2/n per pair; each internal link splits at rate beta. Fixed-beta and small-beta regimes are grouped. The source proves bounds between asymptotic1/sqrt(beta^2+4beta) and1/beta.

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0211-A001 | Relaxation of linear-polymer coagulation and fragmentation structural calculation

Argument or result

The cluster-count coordinate K has exact rates K->K+1 at beta*(n-K) and K->K-1 at K*(K-1)/n. Its deterministic equilibrium density mu solves mu^2=beta*(1-mu), and linearizing the density drift gives restoring rate beta+2*mu=sqrt(beta^2+4*beta). At beta=b/n this is order n^-1/2. The source edge coupling has discrepancy killing rate at least beta+(K-1)/n, so it also suggests this scale once K is near sqrt(b*n).

Check

For n=3 the partition states3,21,111 have rates3->21=2beta,21->3=2/3,21->111=beta,111->21=2. Direct characteristic-polynomial calculation gives gap[3beta+8/3-sqrt(beta^2+16/9)]/2. Thus finite-n small-beta behavior cannot simply be substituted for the joint n,beta_n limit.

Remaining obstruction

The required upgrade is a uniform exponential bound on the coupling survival functional E exp[-integral(beta+(K_u-1)/n)du], including atypically small K. Replacing K by its stationary mean inside the exponential has the wrong Jensen direction and is not a proof of the desired upper bound.

P0212  Low-density genealogical limit of branching coalescing walks

Source: Aldous open problems: Low-density coalescing branching random walk | Problem A,2012; updateMarch2018

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/cbrw.html

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Prove the proposed low-density, time-rescaled lineage limit for transient branching-coalescing walk and the constrained-Ising analogue, after discarding short-lived branching jitter.

Scope

The two explicitly linked microscopic models are grouped; the undefined dynamic-graph research direction is not a second admitted problem. Spatial positions are forgotten but splitting and merging histories remain.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0212-A001 | Low-density genealogical limit of branching coalescing walks structural calculation

Argument or result

Immediate parent-daughter returns renormalize the branching clock. For two independent simple walks started at neighboring sites in Z^3, their relative walk is a time change of simple walk. The probability they never meet is q=1-G(e,0)/G(0,0)=1/G(0,0), using G(0,0)=1+G(e,0) by the first-step identity and lattice symmetry. Thus a rare branch at rate beta produces a macroscopically surviving split at leading rate beta*q, rather than beta.

Check

The equality of the two Green-function expressions follows from translation and reflection symmetry of all six neighbors. It concerns an isolated pair; the chance of interference by other particles has not been set to zero without justification.

Remaining obstruction

A thinning heuristic suggests time scaling1/(beta*q), but a proof needs control of third-particle encounters, dependent return episodes and joint Palm genealogies. Stationary balance of split and merge counts does not imply independent Poisson clocks.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0213  Constant-factor online scheduling with subadditive batch durations

Source: Aldous open problems: Bacon and eggs batch scheduling | Problem,postedMarch2009

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/bacon.html

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Find an explicit online batch-scheduling algorithm with a universal ratio of long-run weighted waiting cost to the optimal online cost, over monotone strictly subadditive service-time functions.

Scope

Arrivals form a rate1 Poisson process with uniform types. The source assumes finite optimal cost. A finite deterministic queue example below is only an obstruction to a proposed greedy step, not a steady-state counterexample.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0213-A001 | Constant-factor online scheduling with subadditive batch durations structural calculation

Argument or result

Greedy batching can be arbitrarily poor for a fixed two-job queue when holding costs differ. Let S(a)=1,S(b)=L,S({a,b})=L+epsilon with0<epsilon<1 and L>1. Give a holding rate M and b rate1. Processing both immediately costs(M+1)*(L+epsilon). Processing a first and then b costs M+1+L. For M much larger than L their ratio approaches L+epsilon, although service times are strictly monotone and subadditive.

Check

The individual completion times are L+epsilon for both in the batch, and1,L+1 in the sequential schedule. This compares exactly the same initial jobs without incoming work. For the actual stationary model, Campbell reasoning also gives the elementary lower bound W_*>=integral c(a)*S({a}) da, since each task must spend at least its singleton service time in its completing batch.

Remaining obstruction

Embedding the bad queue event in a stable Poisson-arrival system while controlling its frequency and other waiting costs remains necessary. The two-job calculation does not disprove existence of a different constant-factor algorithm.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0214  Fluctuations of shortest routes in Poisson proximity networks

Source: Aldous open problems: Shortest routes in random proximity networks | Main question;2019 simulation study discussed

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/random_proximity.html

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Determine rigorous orders of route-length variance and transversal deviation for shortest paths in random proximity networks on planar Poisson points.

Scope

Variance and transversal exponents are grouped as the source fluctuation family. A law of large numbers is established and is not counted as a solution.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0214-A001 | Fluctuations of shortest routes in Poisson proximity networks structural calculation

Argument or result

A deterministic route of length D connecting points at distance r and reaching perpendicular deviation T must satisfy D>=sqrt(r^2+4*T^2). To see this, split at a point of maximal deviation and minimize the sum of its distances to the two endpoints over the longitudinal coordinate; the minimum occurs above the midpoint. Hence T<=sqrt(D^2-r^2)/2.

Check

When D/r tends to a stretch constant mu>1, the bound gives only T/r<=sqrt(mu^2-1)/2 asymptotically. It does not force sublinear wandering. This explicitly explains why the existing route-length shape theorem does not already identify a transversal exponent.

Remaining obstruction

A KPZ-type prediction needs estimates for centered route excess and correlations of competing near-geodesics. The deterministic Euclidean inequality leaves an order-r allowance whenever the limiting stretch exceeds1.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0215  Growth exponent for common subcladograms of random trees

Source: Aldous open problems: Largest common random substructures | Original2003 Section2 Example3 PDFp5; author updates

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/substructures.html

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Establish existence and values of the power-law growth exponents for the largest common induced leaf-labeled subtree of two independent uniform cladograms or coalescent trees.

Scope

The two source models form one admitted family. The2024 Budzinski-Senizergues paper improves the uniform-model upper exponent below1/2; that established bound is not a new solution or proof of a limiting exponent.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0215-A001 | Growth exponent for common subcladograms of random trees structural calculation

Argument or result

For the uniform model, sampling consistency gives an exact first-moment count: the expected number of agreeing k-subsets is binom(n,k)/(2k-3)!!. Thus P(C_n>=k) is at most this ratio. Stirling gives log of the ratio=k*[log n-2log k+2-log2]+o(k) when k is of order sqrt n. Consequently the elementary bound stops at order sqrt n, with threshold constant e/sqrt2.

Check

The agreement probability is the collision probability of two uniform induced k-cladograms, exactly1/(2k-3)!!. Existence of a common larger subset implies existence of one of size k. The coalescent model has a nonuniform induced law, so its collision probability must instead be sum_T p_T^2; the uniform formula is not transferred to it.

Remaining obstruction

First moments overcount many strongly overlapping agreeing subsets. They cannot reach the stronger known exponent bound or prove convergence of log E C_n/log n. The source centroid recursion constructs particular matches and is not automatically optimal.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0216  Limit constant for common random two-dimensional partial orders

Source: Aldous open problems: Largest common random substructures | Original2003 Section2 Example4 PDFpp5-6

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/substructures.html

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Prove that the mean size of the largest common induced suborder of two independent uniform two-dimensional partial orders is asymptotic to c*n^(1/3), and determine c.

Scope

The source already gives order n^(1/3) upper and lower arguments. The calculation here makes a constant in its grid lower construction explicit; it is a rederivation of an established method, not resolution of the limit question.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0216-A001 | Limit constant for common random two-dimensional partial orders structural calculation

Argument or result

Partition each square into m-by-m cells, m=floor(n^(1/3)). Keep a label when its two points occupy the same cell, and retain it only if no other kept label shares that row or column. The retained labels have identical comparisons in both posets. For a fixed label the match probability is1/m^2; conditional on its cell, another label is a conflicting match with probability(2m-1)/m^4. Therefore the expected retained number is n/m^2*[1-(2m-1)/m^4]^(n-1), asymptotic to exp(-2)*n^(1/3).

Check

Two retained labels have distinct row and column indices, so each of their coordinate orderings is determined by cell indices in both independent samples. Independence over labels gives the exact no-conflict probability. The bound is liminf E C_n/n^(1/3)>=exp(-2).

Remaining obstruction

The isolated-cell matching discards many usable labels and supplies no matching upper constant. Optimizing a local grid construction does not establish existence of the global optimal limit.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0217  Percolation of merging planar empires

Source: Aldous open problems: Percolation and empires | Two PROBLEM paragraphs;2009 and2017 updates

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/empires.html

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Characterize merging-rate conditions for finite-time infinite empires, in particular whether rate1 per adjacent pair percolates.

Scope

General rate criteria and the distinguished constant-rate case are grouped. The source already proves some sufficient conditions in its2010 paper; the main constant-rate case is not inferred from bond percolation.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0217-A001 | Percolation of merging planar empires structural calculation

Argument or result

The natural area-moment Lyapunov calculation works for strongly suppressed rates. In a finite periodic partition, F=sum_A area(A)^2 changes by2ab when areas a,b merge. If r(A,B)<=1/N(A,B), where N is at least each endpoint region adjacency degree, then the generator obeys L F<=sum_edges(a^2+b^2)/N(A,B)<=F. Hence E F_t<=e^t F_0. Under an infinite-volume limit preserving this bound and translation invariance, the mean area at a typical point stays finite, excluding an infinite empire.

Check

Use2ab<=a^2+b^2 and sum over neighbors of each region of1/N<=1. This independently explains the source suppressed-rate example. For rate1 the neighbor sum becomes the region degree, which has no such uniform control.

Remaining obstruction

For rate1, knowing each original boundary survives with probability exp(-t) does not give product bounds for contours: multiple segments separating the same two current empires share one merger clock. The missing joint survival estimate prevents the naive Peierls argument.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0218  Growth exponents in the spatial city-formation model

Source: Aldous: A spatial model of city growth and formation | Section3 equations4-9 and Section3.1; author open page

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1209.5120

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Prove the proposed balanced/unbalanced growth regimes and city-count exponents in the Aldous-Huang spatial immigration model.

Scope

All parameter regimes, including alpha1, are grouped. A city of size N has influence c0*N^alpha*r^-beta; an arrival founds a city with probability1/(1+maximum influence). The source heuristic is not a theorem.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0218-A001 | Growth exponents in the spatial city-formation model structural calculation

Argument or result

A rigorous one-sided bound supports the balanced exponent when0<alpha<1 and beta>2alpha. Write d=beta/2-alpha>0. A union bound over influence disks gives area{I(y,t)>u}<=pi*c0^(2/beta)*u^(-2/beta)*M_t^(1-2alpha/beta)*t^(2alpha/beta), using concavity of the population power. Thus the conditional founding probability is at least1/[2*(1+K*t^alpha*M_t^d)], K=(2pi)^(beta/2)*c0. Put theta=(1-alpha)/(1+d) and m=floor(kappa*t^theta). Until M reaches m, each of the last floor(t/2) arrivals has founding chance at least p=1/[2*(1+K*t^alpha*m^d)]. Choose kappa small so this Bernoulli count has mean at least2m. A stopped coupling and Chernoff then give P(M_t<m)<=exp(-c*m) for large t.

Check

The disk estimate is valid even at the square boundary because clipping only reduces area. Sum_i N_i^(2alpha/beta)<=M^(1-2alpha/beta)*t^(2alpha/beta). On the event M_t<m the threshold has never been reached, so domination by independent Bernoulli(p) increments throughout the half-interval is legitimate. The exponent identity theta*(1+d)=1-alpha makes the mean comparable to m.

Remaining obstruction

This proves a lower city-count growth bound in the balanced regime, not the matching upper bound, comparable populations, unbalanced regime, or alpha1 logarithmic law. The upper bound requires control of spatial clustering and influence overlap that the union bound does not supply.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0219  High-density oriented percolation flow asymptotics

Source: Aldous open problems: Discrete Hammersley process and oriented percolation flows | Conjecture,formulated2004

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/hammersley_flow.html

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For the oriented square-lattice maximal-flow constant v(p), prove1-v(p) is asymptotic to sqrt(2*(1-p)) as p tends to1.

Scope

The p-down-to-critical regime is not counted as an invented second problem. The author heuristic contains two normalization errors, recorded explicitly below; correcting them still supports the displayed conjecture.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0219-A001 | High-density oriented percolation flow asymptotics structural calculation

Argument or result

For a continuum Poisson process of intensity delta in[0,n]^2, rescaling coordinates by sqrt(delta) gives intensity1 in[0,n*sqrt(delta)]^2. Hence its minimum increasing-chain cover has the law U*(n*sqrt(delta),1), not U*(n/sqrt(delta),1). By the poset chain-cover/antichain relation it equals the longest decreasing subsequence, whose leading Poissonized Hammersley asymptotic is2m for a unit-intensity m-square. With delta=2*(1-p), dividing2*n*sqrt(delta) by2n recovers sqrt(2*(1-p)).

Check

The expected number of Poisson points, delta*n^2, is invariant under the corrected scaling and would fail under the printed inverse scaling. Reflection converts decreasing to increasing subsequences, preserving the known leading constant2. These are checks of the continuum surrogate only.

Remaining obstruction

One still needs a uniform comparison of the lattice closed-edge path-cover problem with the continuum antichain problem as n tends to infinity and p tends to1. The two corrected constants do not justify interchanging those limits.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0220  Explicit stationary law of the leftmost Hammersley drift-jump particles

Source: Aldous open problems: One-dimensional drift-jump particle process | Problem,postedFebruary2018;1993notes Section4.1

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/left_Hamm.html

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Give a useful explicit joint stationary law for the ordered particles which drift at speed x and jump left at rate1 Poisson space-time points.

Scope

The1993 notes already contain a patience-sorting representation and small-k calculations. The1999 paper Section5.2 uses the stationary process for limit theorems. Those established representations are not counted as newly solved open work.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0220-A001 | Explicit stationary law of the leftmost Hammersley drift-jump particles structural calculation

Argument or result

The established rank representation can be expanded into a convergent combinatorial density series. Let B=(1=b1<...<bk) be the limiting first-row labels of the insertion tableau, and let q_B be its probability. The first b=bk labels form a uniform permutation, so q_B=(1/b!)*sum over partitions lambda of b with first row length k of f^lambda times the number of standard tableaux of shape lambda with first row B. Independent unit-rate Poisson spacings then give density exp(-xk)*sum_B q_B*product_(i=2..k)[(x_i-x_(i-1))^(b_i-b_(i-1)-1)/(b_i-b_(i-1)-1)!].

Check

Restriction of an insertion tableau to labels at most b is the tableau of the corresponding restricted subword. The last specified first-row entry b pins the first k labels, irrespective of later larger labels. Conditional on B, the Poisson arrival times have independent gamma increments, so every term is a normalized conditional density and nonnegative summation is valid. For k1 this gives Exp(1); known Poissonized LIS formulas give P(X2>x)=exp(-x)*I0(2sqrt(x)) and E X2=e.

Remaining obstruction

This repackages a known representation into explicit but rapidly growing finite-tableau coefficients and an infinite sum. It does not establish a new tractable closed joint law or its novelty, and should not be promoted as a full solution to the source request.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0221  Limit of the optimal online random spanning-tree cost

Source: Aldous-Angel-Berestycki: Online random weight minimal spanning trees, draft2008 | Conjecture PDFp2;Theorem3 PDFp4; author open page

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/Beres2008.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Prove existence and determine the limiting optimal mean cost of an online spanning tree of the complete randomly weighted graph.

Scope

Combinatorial random-order and Poisson offer models are grouped, but their finite-n costs differ. The draft explicitly lacks a complete asymptotic equivalence proof. Numerical estimates near1.9 are not certified limits.

Dated literature checks

Author-maintained page and relevant linked primary sources checked2026-09-06. Focused later-literature searches did not establish a complete resolution; current status remains unverified.

P0221-A001 | Limit of the optimal online random spanning-tree cost structural calculation

Argument or result

The Poisson dynamic-programming recursion has exact small-state solutions. For two current components of sizes x,y, F=(xy/2)*F^2 and the positive solution is2/(xy). For three singleton vertices each merged state has cost1, giving F=(3/2)*(F-1)^2. The admissible root exceeding the child cost is(4+sqrt7)/3; the other root is below1 and violates strict monotonicity.

Check

The first recursion follows by integrating the accepted-offer surplus over weights below the continuation-cost difference. For n2 the Poisson cost is2 while the combinatorial model must buy its sole Uniform(0,1) edge and has cost1/2, demonstrating why finite-n model identification is invalid.

Remaining obstruction

The recursion depends on the entire component-size partition and on n. Standard coagulation hydrodynamic limits assume a prescribed kernel, whereas these acceptance kernels are themselves defined by unknown value functions. A compactness and uniqueness argument for their joint limit is missing.

P0222  Near-unit stretch versus length in spatial networks

Source: Aldous-Lando: The stretch-length tradeoff in geometric networks | Abstract and dense-network limit question; author open page

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1404.2653

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Establish existence and value of the exponent governing minimum network length per unit area as the allowed all-pairs stretch s decreases to1.

Scope

The Poisson average-case problem is the probability target; the source worst-case analogue is grouped as a comparison. This is different from the mean-route-length constraint for SIRSNs.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0222-A001 | Near-unit stretch versus length in spatial networks structural attempt

Argument or result

Every path of stretch at most1+epsilon between endpoints at distance r stays in an ellipse whose maximum transverse half-width is(r/2)*sqrt(2epsilon+epsilon^2). Indeed a point of deviation T forces the total path length to be at least sqrt(r^2+4T^2). Thus as epsilon decreases, each local pair needs a usable road within a tube of angular width order sqrt(epsilon). This is an explicit geometric constraint for a possible direction-packing lower bound.

Check

The ellipse inequality follows by minimizing the sum of distances from a path point to its two endpoints over the longitudinal coordinate. It is shared with the deterministic lemma in P0214, but the sourced optimization problem here has a different target.

Remaining obstruction

Counting constrained pairs is insufficient because one road segment can serve many pairs. A stochastic-geometric bound on this reuse, or a matching construction with controlled length, is needed to obtain a sharp exponent.

P0223  Sharp relaxation exponent for local tree rotations and triangulation flips

Source: Aldous: Mixing times for branch rotation and triangulation walk | PROBLEM,PDFp2; historyp3

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/206-RWG/RWGpapers/clad-mix.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Prove an order n^(3/2) relaxation-time upper bound for the branch-rotation cladogram chain and the related convex-polygon triangulation flip chain.

Scope

The two chains are conservatively grouped as the source rotation family. The2026 Alev-Frishberg-Sarantis-Tetali theorem improves the triangulation bound to n^2 times polylogarithms and explicitly leaves the conjectured exponent unresolved.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0223-A001 | Sharp relaxation exponent for local tree rotations and triangulation flips structural attempt

Argument or result

A canonical-path construction that first rotates every tree to one fixed comb has exponential congestion, even if each route is short. Let N be the number of states and use uniform stationary mass1/N. Routing the all-pairs demand pi(x)*pi(y) through that comb sends total order1 flow through a vertex whose total stationary transition capacity is1/N. Some incident edge therefore has load-to-capacity ratio at least order N. For triangulations N is a Catalan number of order4^n/n^(3/2).

Check

The total capacity through the chosen vertex is sum_y pi(v)P(v,y)=1/N. Pairs not starting or ending there have total demand(1-1/N)^2 and must cross it in the proposed construction. Summing their edge loads gives the claimed bottleneck independently of path length.

Remaining obstruction

A proof needs routes or transport flows distributed over many intermediate shapes. The comb normal form is useful for irreducibility but cannot furnish the conjectured polynomial relaxation bound.

P0224  Feasible length and stretch statistics of scale-invariant networks

Source: Aldous: Scale-invariant random spatial networks | Open Problems29 and30(i),PDFp48

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1204.0817

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Describe the feasible triples(Delta,ell,p(1)) for SIRSNs and obtain sharp bounds for the minimum sampled-network length ell at fixed mean route stretch Delta.

Scope

The feasible-region question and its lower-boundary function are one optimization family. Source30(ii), attainment, is separately admitted because it concerns closure and existence.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0224-A001 | Feasible length and stretch statistics of scale-invariant networks structural attempt

Argument or result

The feasible set is convex under the source axioms. Choose one of two entire SIRSN laws once using an independent Bernoulli variable, and use that law for all routes. Every realization retains compatibility and all required distributional invariances. Each of Delta,ell,p(1) is an expectation or monotone limit of expectations, hence is the same convex combination of the corresponding statistics. Taking near-minimizers shows ell_*(lambda*Delta1+(1-lambda)*Delta2)<=lambda*ell_*(Delta1)+(1-lambda)*ell_*(Delta2).

Check

The random choice is global, so routes from the two networks are never spliced together. No ergodicity assumption appears in the cited definition; imposing one would invalidate this mixture argument. Finite expected statistics remain finite for a two-law mixture.

Remaining obstruction

Convexity does not locate the feasible region or give its sharp boundary. It also does not guarantee an extremal law exists; a convex set need not be closed.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0225  Attainment of the optimal SIRSN length-stretch tradeoff

Source: Aldous: Scale-invariant random spatial networks | Open Problem30(ii),PDFp48

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1204.0817

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Does a SIRSN attain the infimum sampled-network length at a prescribed mean route stretch, and what structure can an optimizer have?

Scope

This is the existence part of the source optimization question, not a separately counted parameter choice.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0225-A001 | Attainment of the optimal SIRSN length-stretch tradeoff structural attempt

Argument or result

Uniform expected length in every compact set supplies tightness for the unlabelled length measures: Markov bounds on an exhausting sequence of compact sets control local masses, and bounded-mass sets of Radon measures are vaguely relatively compact. However the prescribed feasible-route class is not closed under even uniform convergence with bounded lengths. Inscribed polygonal approximations to a semicircle are finite unions of straight segments with uniformly bounded length, while their limit is a smooth arc and is not a jagged route made of straight segments.

Check

For the semicircle, no nontrivial subarc is a line segment; countably many straight segments lying in the arc can therefore cover only countably many points. The source itself discusses a possible extension from jagged to rectifiable routes.

Remaining obstruction

A compactness proof for length measures alone loses route labels, compatibility and the allowed-curve condition. One must choose a richer topology and prove closure or justify a relaxed model before claiming attainment.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0226  Local finiteness of scale-invariant network traffic measures

Source: Aldous: Scale-invariant random spatial networks | Open Problem31,PDFp49

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1204.0817

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For source-destination displacement density|z|^-beta with2<beta<4, prove suitable local finiteness of induced route traffic on the major-road set E(infinity,1).

Scope

The target is traffic restricted to major roads. Finiteness of the expected traffic on the full dense route network is a different, stronger statement and fails by the calculation below.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0226-A001 | Local finiteness of scale-invariant network traffic measures structural attempt

Argument or result

For a bounded area A, translation invariance and Tonelli give expected full-network traffic through A equal to area(A)*integral_R2 |z|^-beta*E len R(0,z) dz. Scale invariance makes E len R(0,z)=Delta*|z|, yielding area(A)*2*pi*Delta*integral_0^infinity r^(2-beta) dr. This integral diverges for every beta: at infinity for beta<=3, at zero for beta>=3.

Check

Translate each route by its source and integrate the length of its intersection with A over all source locations. Each infinitesimal route segment contributes area(A). This mass-transport identity uses nonnegative integrands, so it remains valid when the integral is infinite.

Remaining obstruction

The divergent full-network expectation neither disproves almost-sure local finiteness nor answers the restricted-major-road question. A proof must exploit the major-road restriction and separately control long routes and short routes which take large excursions.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0227  Implications among SIRSN finiteness and geodesic properties

Source: Aldous: Scale-invariant random spatial networks | Open Problem32(i)-(iv),PDFp49;definitionspp10-11,44-45

All four linked implications are grouped as one source family. Equation16 is finite ell;20 is finite p(1);49 is pairwise ray coalescence;50 is uniform small-neighborhood-to-far-target coalescence;51 is route coincidence away from nearby endpoints.

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0227-A001 | Implications among SIRSN finiteness and geodesic properties structural attempt

Argument or result

The available finite-intensity estimate does not yield the first implication: p(lambda,1)<=intensity S(lambda)=ell*sqrt(lambda), which diverges with the sampling density. For the third implication, finite coalescence radii at each finite sample do not bound their increasing supremum; a concrete scalar analogue is R_k=k, finite at every k and divergent in the limit. For the second, stationary families of parallel rays show that uniqueness of a ray at each origin does not alone imply coalescence. For the fourth, continuity at each fixed destination gives no uniform estimate as the destination recedes to infinity.

Check

The scaling identity for sampled intensity follows by rescaling a rate1 Poisson process by lambda^-1/2. The parallel-ray construction is deliberately only a counterexample to the isolated logical inference; it is not claimed to satisfy the full connected-route SIRSN axioms.

Remaining obstruction

Actual counterexamples must preserve compatibility, all scale invariances and finite required moments. Alternatively a proof needs a uniform estimate absent from the displayed bounds. No member of the four-part family is settled here.

P0228  Uniqueness of the unbounded major-road component

Source: Aldous: Scale-invariant random spatial networks | Open Problem33,PDFp49

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1204.0817

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Must the major-road set E(infinity,1) of every SIRSN have only one unbounded connected component almost surely?
