Disconnected finite major-road pieces are allowed by definition. The question concerns unbounded components only.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0228-A001 | Uniqueness of the unbounded major-road component structural attempt

Argument or result

A precise sufficient reduction is available: if every unbounded major-road component contains a tail of a singly infinite geodesic and all such geodesics coalesce, then uniqueness follows. Two purported unbounded components would contain two tails, whose common eventual tail lies in both, forcing the components to coincide.

Check

The conclusion uses connected-component maximality, not merely that the full network is connected. Finite local total length does not by itself supply the needed geodesic tail in every unbounded component, because the route union is not assumed to be a locally finite combinatorial graph.

Remaining obstruction

Neither the universal ray-coalescence property nor the component-tail property is proved from the source axioms. A Burton-Keane style argument would require a valid local-modification principle, which arbitrary SIRSN laws need not have.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0229  Integrable supremum of routes to a bounded region

Source: Aldous: Scale-invariant random spatial networks | Open Problem34,PDFp50

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1204.0817

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Under what additional assumptions does the expected supremum of route lengths from0 to an infinite independent uniform sample in the unit disk remain finite?

Scope

Pointwise E len R(0,x)=Delta*|x| does not control a random supremum. The sufficient increment condition below is stronger than the base axioms and is not claimed to follow from them.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0229-A001 | Integrable supremum of routes to a bounded region structural attempt

Argument or result

Let L(x)=len R(0,x). Suppose on a containing square E|L(x)-L(y)|^p<=C*|x-y|^p for a p>2, with a jointly measurable version and finite mean at one anchor. On the dyadic grid of mesh2^-k there are order2^(2k) parent-child or neighbor pairs. The expected maximum increment is at most Cprime*2^(-k*(1-2/p)). This is summable, so telescoping along grid ancestors constructs a continuous modification with finite expected supremum.

Check

Use max_j |Z_j|<=(sum_j |Z_j|^p)^(1/p) and Jensen, then sum the resulting geometric series. The increment bound implies stochastic continuity, identifying the modification at each deterministic point; Fubini identifies it at each independent uniform sample point, and countability handles the entire sampled sequence.

Remaining obstruction

The source only gives first moments of individual route lengths, not p-moment increments of L. Its compatibility condition does not make Euclidean route length an automatic metric with a triangle inequality, so a metric-chaining shortcut is unjustified.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0230  Asymptotic total length of finite SIRSN samples

Source: Aldous: Scale-invariant random spatial networks | Open Problem35,PDFp50

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1204.0817

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

For k uniform points in a square of area k, prove that the mean total length of their spanning route network is asymptotic to ell*k.

Scope

Length includes route pieces outside the sampling square. This global excursion contribution is the main source obstruction.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0230-A001 | Asymptotic total length of finite SIRSN samples structural attempt

Argument or result

A local truncation argument gives the lower bound liminf E length/k>=ell. Tile the bulk of the large square by unit cells. For fixed R and M, retain in each cell only the length, truncated at M, of routes between sampled endpoints within distance R of that cell. Its local binomial point configuration converges in total variation to the corresponding rate1 Poisson configuration. The retained length is bounded by M, so its expectation converges. Summing disjoint unit-cell contributions and then taking k to infinity gives the Poisson local truncated expectation as a lower bound. Let M and then R increase: monotone convergence recovers the full sampled-network intensity ell.

Check

Only a bounded endpoint region is used before taking k to infinity; its binomial count tends to a Poisson count and conditional positions have the same uniform law. Truncation avoids any unproved uniform-integrability step. Boundary cells are an asymptotically negligible fraction for fixed R.

Remaining obstruction

This proves no upper bound on total length. Rare paths can leave the sampling square by a large distance, and their exterior contribution is absent from the local lower-bound argument. Controlling those excursions remains necessary for equality.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0231  A zero-one law for Poisson-coloring frontier dimension

Source: Basdevant-Blanc-Curien-Singh: Fractal properties of the frontier in Poissonian coloring,2023 | Discussion before Open Question1.2,PDFp3

https://alea.impa.br/articles/v20/20-59.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Is the Hausdorff dimension of the limiting Poisson-coloring frontier almost surely deterministic?

Scope

The strict inequalities d-1<dimension<d are already proved in Theorem1.1. This entry concerns the explicitly remaining zero-one question, not that resolved conjecture.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0231-A001 | A zero-one law for Poisson-coloring frontier dimension structural attempt

Argument or result

The dimension event is not visibly a tail event of the independent arriving points. Changing an early point can change the color of infinitely many later descendants, so Kolmogorov zero-one cannot be invoked without a finite-modification invariance proof. The potential route is a regeneration principle producing, inside every surviving interface neighborhood, copies with the full essential supremum of the dimension law; countable-union stability of dimension would then force the maximal value.

Check

The mere facts that the frontier is closed and has dimension strictly between1 and2 do not imply determinism: a Bernoulli mixture of two fixed compact fractal curves with different dimensions satisfies those facts and has random dimension. This is a logical control, not a Poisson-coloring counterexample.

Remaining obstruction

Independent arrivals do not make recursively inherited colors independent across subregions. A regeneration or local-universality statement strong enough to remove early-history effects is missing.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0232  Continuous curves in the Poisson-coloring frontier

Source: Basdevant-Blanc-Curien-Singh: Fractal properties of the frontier in Poissonian coloring,2023 | Open Question1.2,PDFpp3-4

https://alea.impa.br/articles/v20/20-59.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Is the planar limiting frontier a countable union of continuous curves?

Scope

A curve means an image of a continuous map from[0,1]. It is not required to be rectifiable. Point and segment variants are grouped.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0232-A001 | Continuous curves in the Poisson-coloring frontier structural attempt

Argument or result

The established dimension lower bound already rules out a countable union of rectifiable curves: every rectifiable curve has Hausdorff dimension at most1, and the dimension of a countable union is the supremum of the component dimensions. Thus any affirmative construction must use nonrectifiable curves. Compact connectedness alone also cannot supply path connectedness, as shown by the topologist sine-curve closure.

Check

The sine-curve closure of{(x,sin(1/x)):0<x<=1} together with the limiting vertical segment is compact and connected but not path connected. This does not by itself refute a countable-curve covering; it specifically blocks the inference from connectedness to a single continuous parametrization.

Remaining obstruction

A scale-uniform crossing/tightness estimate is needed to construct continuous interface paths. The source lacks the correlation inequality used to establish such estimates in standard planar models.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0233  Absence of double points on Poisson-coloring interface curves

Source: Basdevant-Blanc-Curien-Singh: Fractal properties of the frontier in Poissonian coloring,2023 | Open Question1.3,PDFp4

https://alea.impa.br/articles/v20/20-59.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

If the limiting frontier is a union of curves, are its component curves almost surely simple?

Scope

This conditional question is distinct from existence of curves. The segment-model single-curve consequence is grouped.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0233-A001 | Absence of double points on Poisson-coloring interface curves structural attempt

Argument or result

A quantitative four-arm estimate would settle the proposed double-point obstruction. In a bounded region cover by order epsilon^-2 boxes. If the probability that a box supports four alternating color arms from scale epsilon to a fixed macroscopic radius is at most C*epsilon^(2+eta), a union bound makes the chance of any such box at most Cprime*epsilon^eta. Taking epsilon down to0 and then countably many macroscopic radii excludes points with four nontrivial alternating arms.

Check

A double point with four arms reaching a positive radius must lie in one of the grid boxes and induce a slightly enlarged box arm event. Changing fixed geometric constants does not alter the positive exponent margin.

Remaining obstruction

No four-arm exponent exceeding2 is established for this dependent coloring process. Excluding four-arm points would still have to be connected carefully to the exact parametrization and component statement, rather than silently equated with every form of self-touching.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0234  A safe margin around extended initial color seeds

Source: Basdevant-Blanc-Curien-Singh: Fractal properties of the frontier in Poissonian coloring,2023 | Open Question1.4,PDFp4

https://alea.impa.br/articles/v20/20-59.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

If the initial red seed is a segment or ball disjoint from the blue seed, does the limiting blue region almost surely avoid the entire initial red seed?

Scope

Pointwise protection of each fixed initial point is established in related arguments and does not imply simultaneous protection of a continuum. Disjoint initial colors are part of the meaningful scope.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0234-A001 | A safe margin around extended initial color seeds structural attempt

Argument or result

A sufficient uniform estimate can be stated precisely. Let the compact seed K have covering number at most C*delta^-d0. If every x in K satisfies P(dist(x,B_infinity)<=2delta)<=Cprime*delta^alpha uniformly, with alpha>d0, a delta-net and union bound give P(K intersects B_infinity)<=Cdouble*delta^(alpha-d0), hence zero. Compactness converts zero separation into intersection for the closed limiting blue set.

Check

Pointwise zero intersection probability is inadequate: for a uniformly random U in a segment, every fixed x differs from U almost surely, but the random singleton{U} intersects the segment surely. The net argument explicitly identifies the missing uniform rate.

Remaining obstruction

The available shielding results give positive protection probability or fixed-point statements, not a uniform small-distance exponent exceeding the dimension of the seed. The full safe-margin question remains unresolved here.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0235  A Feller topology for limits of reversible Markov chains

Source: Aldous: Limits for processes over general networks,2018 | Conjecture,slide5;author current compact page

https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~aldous/Research/OP/talk_towsner_2018.pdf

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Can the measurable limit process of finite reversible chains be given a natural complete separable state metric with the Feller property?

Scope

Towsner2015 already supplies the measure-theoretic existence and refined uniqueness results. This entry concerns only the remaining topological upgrade.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0235-A001 | A Feller topology for limits of reversible Markov chains structural attempt

Argument or result

The suggested unweighted heat-kernel metric can be infinite in a basic admissible example. For Brownian motion on the two-torus, the squared row-distance integrated against exp(-t)dt equals sum_(k in Z^2) |1-exp(2pi*i*k.h)|^2/[1+8pi^2*|k|^2]. At h=(1/2,0), every odd k1 has numerator4. Each dyadic annulus contains order N^2 such terms of order N^-2, so the series diverges. A repair is to use w(t)=exp(-t)/(1+G(2t)), where G is the integrated diagonal heat trace: Fubini gives finite weighted row norm for almost every state.

Check

Parseval computes the row norm exactly; integrating the nonnegative Fourier series is justified by Tonelli. For the repaired weight, the average row squared norm is integral w(t)*G(2t)dt<=1. This produces an almost-everywhere finite Hilbert-space pseudometric after identifying indistinguishable rows.

Remaining obstruction

Brownian motion already has a good usual topology, so the divergence refutes only the illustrative formula, not the existence conjecture. Completing a heat-kernel embedding can add states; proving valid transition kernels and the Feller property on that completion is still missing.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

P0236  Infinite mean route length at each large fixed Euclidean separation

Source: Aldous: Route Lengths in Invariant Spatial Tree Networks | Introduction conjecture,PDFp2;Section3.3

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2103.00669

Historical status: source_says_open | Current status: unverified | Result: attempted_unsolved

Question

Is there a universal r0 such that every invariant tree network on planar rate1 Poisson points has infinite mean within-tree distance at almost every fixed separation r>=r0?

Scope

Theorem2 proves the averaged statement for pairs at distance at most r. The stronger fixed-separation conjecture and the authors possible thin-annulus counterconstruction remain distinct from that theorem.

Dated literature checks

Primary source and available author updates read2026-09-06; focused later searches recorded in source registry. No current-open certification or novelty claim.

P0236-A001 | Infinite mean route length at each large fixed Euclidean separation structural attempt

Argument or result

Divergence of the averaged mean cannot alone imply pointwise infinite means. A scalar control is rho(s)=1+1/|s-a| for s different from a, with any finite value assigned at a. Then rho is finite at every s, but integral_0^r s*rho(s)ds is infinite for every r>a. Thus even all sufficiently large cumulative pair averages can diverge while no fixed-radius value is infinite.

Check

The planar radial averaging density is proportional to s, so a singularity at a positive a survives the weighting. This example is only a function-level obstruction; it is not asserted to arise from any invariant tree network.

Remaining obstruction

One needs regularity or geometric constraints on the actual Palm mean function rho, or a completed invariant-tree counterconstruction. The source thin-annulus idea still requires cycle removal and connectivity without destroying the desired finite conditional means.

Verification: author_checked_limited_argument | Independent review: none | Full question solved: False

Excluded and resolved source candidates

P0096  Four-edge-minimum lower bound for the FPP time constant

current status

resolved_in_literature

result status

literature_counterexample_rederived

statement

For iid nonnegative edge times on Z^2 with F(0)=0, is the axial time constant at least the expected minimum of four independent edge times?

statement caveat

E_4 is expectation of a minimum, not a fourth moment. The target is the universal survey formulation; the motivating simulation paper reports selected families.

resolution evidence

Daniel Ahlberg, Tertiles and the time constant, arXiv1911.09949 (2019;v2May2020), Journal of Applied Probability57(2),407-408,2020. The full two-page paper was read. Theorem1 gives mu<=2t_(2/3); final paragraph explicitly refutes Alm-Deijfen and surveyQuestion12. Published DOI10.1017/jpr.2020.9.

resolution url

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1911.09949

reason

Already resolved before this campaign; own construction is retained as a rederivation, excluded from1000 current-open target and original-solution counts.

Retained derivation or attempt

Take tau=eta+epsilon U with P(eta=0)=3/4 and U uniform[0,1]. For epsilon>0 this is atomless and bounded with F(0)=0. The four-edge minimum has expectation at least1/256. The eta-model time constant is0. For each n, passage times converge to their eta-model values and are bounded by2n; dominated convergence and mu=inf_n E T(0,ne1)/n imply mu_epsilon tends to0. All sufficiently small epsilon reverse the proposed inequality.

Status limit

No mathematical gap identified in the written candidate; later-literature and erratum search remain incomplete. No explicit numerical epsilon threshold or independent review is claimed.

Retained derivation or attempt

Ahlberg2019/2020 already refuted the question. Applying his mu<=2t_(2/3) to the independently drafted tau=eta+epsilon U family gives t_(2/3)=8epsilon/9, hence mu<=16epsilon/9. With epsilon=1/1024, mu<=1/576<1/256<=E minfour. This supplies an explicit atomless bounded counterexample using an established theorem.

Status limit

No current-open problem remains for this universal inequality. This is literature-established, not an original solution.

P0140  Random critical drift in the annealed thinned Levy process

current status

resolved_in_literature

result status

literature_result_rederived_with_scope_correction

statement

Explain directly how mixing the heavy-tailed degree weights yields the independent-increment iid-degree exploration limit.

statement caveat

The deterministic drift in the short2017 formulation must be replaced by the random critical shift Lambda_0. Dhara et al2019 equation2.22 and Remark11 already identify the corrected law indirectly.

later resolution

{"finding": "Corrected law is indirectly identified; direct marking proof supplied here is not counted as a new theorem", "locator": "Equation2.22 and Remark11, PDF pp.9-10", "url": "https://arxiv.org/pdf/1612.00650"}

Retained derivation or attempt

For PPP weights with intensity(tau-1)c*x^(-tau)dx and exponential marks of rate x/mu, the random drift Z/mu cancels the empirical quadratic compensator and leaves independent increments from a deterministic marked-Poisson intensity. With a deterministic drift, a mixed log-Laplace difference is positive for small a, so increments are dependent.

Status limit

No unproved theorem is claimed: the corrected law was already identified indirectly in2019. Exact source normalization and later availability of the direct proof are retained as scope/novelty limits.

Proof appendix: P0001_cactus_forests

Candidate and author-check labels inside this appendix govern its status. Mathematical source notation is preserved.

P0001: negative dependence for random forests

**Target remains unsolved here.** This note proves a special case and refutes an overly broad proof route. The special case is author checked; novelty is not claimed. No independent reviewer or proof assistant has checked this note.

The sourced target is Grimmett, *Selected problems in probability theory* (2022), Conjecture 4.2, PDF page 8: for a finite simple graph, uniformly choose an acyclic edge subset F. Are distinct edge indicators always negatively correlated? Tang and Zhang, arXiv:2603.10738v1 (March 2026), Conjecture 1.3, still states this general question. Their complete-graph results do not settle arbitrary graphs.

A special case with an exact formula

Let G be a finite cactus graph: each edge belongs to at most one simple cycle. Give every edge a finite positive weight w_e. Choose an acyclic edge set F with probability proportional to the product of its selected edge weights. This includes the uniform forest measure when every w_e=1.

**Proposition.** This measure is negatively associated: if A and B are disjoint edge sets and f and g are increasing real functions of the indicators in A and B respectively, then Cov(f,g) <= 0.

**Proof.** Begin with independent Bernoulli edge indicators X_e with p_e=w_e/(1+w_e). Conditioning on acyclicity gives exactly the desired measure, because the product probability of an edge set is a constant times its product weight.

The cactus cycles have disjoint edge sets. Acyclicity is therefore the intersection, over cycles C, of the events that not every edge of C is selected. These events depend on disjoint coordinate sets. Conditioning preserves independence between the cycle blocks and the remaining bridge edges.

Consider one cycle block. Write H for the event that every coordinate equals one and q=P(H)=product(p_e). For disjoint coordinate sets A and B in that block, set a=E f, b=E g, f_*=f(1_A), and g_*=g(1_B). Independence gives E(fg)=ab. On H, f=f_* and g=g_*. Consequently

E(f | H^c) = (a-q f_*)/(1-q),
    E(g | H^c) = (b-q g_*)/(1-q),
    E(fg | H^c) = (ab-q f_* g_*)/(1-q).

Subtracting the product of the first two equations from the third yields

Cov(f,g | H^c)
      = -q (f_*-a)(g_*-b)/(1-q)^2 <= 0.

The inequality follows from monotonicity: f_* >= a and g_* >= b. The denominator is positive because every p_e<1.

For completeness, independence products preserve negative association. Suppose X and Y are independent negatively associated blocks. Condition first on Y. The conditional covariance of f(X_A,Y_C) and g(X_B,Y_D) is nonpositive whenever A,B are disjoint and C,D are disjoint. The two conditional expectations are increasing functions of the disjoint Y-coordinate sets C,D; their covariance is also nonpositive. The total covariance identity proves the claim for (X,Y). Induct over the finitely many blocks, including independent bridge coordinates. This proves the proposition.

For two distinct edges e,f in the same cycle, the formula becomes

Cov(X_e,X_f | H^c)
      = -q (1-p_e)(1-p_f)/(1-q)^2.

For a uniform cycle with m edges this is

-2^(m-2)/(2^m-1)^2.

Edges in different blocks have covariance zero.

Second derivation and adversarial check

For the uniform cycle, count subsets directly. There are 2^m-1 forests, 2^(m-1)-1 containing a given edge, and 2^(m-2)-1 containing a specified pair. Thus the covariance numerator is

(2^(m-2)-1)(2^m-1) - (2^(m-1)-1)^2 = -2^(m-2),

which agrees with the conditional-product calculation. For a triangle it gives -2/49. For a tree, all edge subsets are forests, and independent Bernoulli(1/2) indicators give zero covariance. These checks test both the sign and the degenerate no-cycle case.

The strongest reason this does not solve the target is that general cycles share edges. Their exclusion events no longer factor into independent blocks. One cannot simply say that conditioning independent bits on a decreasing event preserves negative association.

Here is a minimal counterexample to that proposed general principle. Take three fair independent bits (X1,X2,X3), conditioned on neither X1 X2 nor X2 X3 equaling one. The five allowed states are

000, 100, 010, 001, 101.

Then E X1=E X3=2/5 and E(X1 X3)=1/5, so Cov(X1,X3)=1/25>0. The conditioning event is decreasing, but creates positive correlation. This is a counterexample to a proof route, not to the forest conjecture: the forbidden pairs do not form the circuit family of a graphic matroid. Circuit elimination would require another circuit, which is absent.

Exact unresolved obligation

To extend this argument one needs a valid inequality controlling the interaction of overlapping cycle constraints, using graphic-matroid structure. Arbitrary decreasing-event conditioning cannot provide it. The target is not promoted beyond a partial attempt.

Sources

- https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.07318 (Conjecture 4.2, page 8).
- https://arxiv.org/html/2603.10738v1 (Conjecture 1.3 and the discussion immediately following).

The calculations and proof above were derived during this campaign. They are not attributed to the cited papers.

Proof appendix: P0004_quenched_limsup

Candidate and author-check labels inside this appendix govern its status. Mathematical source notation is preserved.

P0004: what can be proved about the limsup

Status: author-checked elementary partial result. No novelty claim. The source's question about existence of a limit remains unsolved here.

Target source: Geoffrey Grimmett, *Selected problems in probability theory*, arXiv:2205.07318, Question 2.3, PDF pages 3-4. The target is the exponential growth limit of self-avoiding walks on the infinite supercritical bond-percolation cluster of the square lattice.

Deterministic root-independence lemma

Let G be an infinite connected graph of maximum degree Delta < infinity. Let c_n(v) count n-step self-avoiding walks starting at v, with c_0(v)=1. Then the quantity

M(v) = limsup as n tends to infinity of c_n(v)^(1/n)

does not depend on v.

Proof. First let v and w be adjacent. Partition the walks from v according to whether they visit w. A walk which avoids w becomes an (n+1)-step self-avoiding walk from w after prepending the edge wv. If a walk visits w, let k in {1,...,n} be its unique visiting time. Reverse its prefix from v to w and retain its suffix from w. These are respectively k-step and (n-k)-step self-avoiding walks from w. Forgetting their disjointness only enlarges the number of pairs. Thus

c_n(v) <= c_(n+1)(w) + sum_(k=1)^n c_k(w)c_(n-k)(w).

An infinite connected locally finite graph contains a ray from every vertex, by the elementary finite-branching tree argument applied to finite self-avoiding walks. Hence 1 <= M(w) <= Delta. For every epsilon>0 there is a finite C>=1 such that c_m(w)<=C(M(w)+epsilon)^m for every m>=0, by the definition of limsup and enlarging C for the finitely many exceptions. The displayed inequality gives

c_n(v) <= [C(M(w)+epsilon) + n C^2](M(w)+epsilon)^n.

Take nth roots and limsup, then let epsilon decrease to zero. This gives M(v)<=M(w). Interchanging the roots gives equality. A finite path connects any two roots. QED.

Independent check: apply the argument to a tree. The visiting decomposition still holds although the exact counts can vary dramatically by root at finite n; the polynomial factor disappears at the exponential scale. The proof never uses transitivity or stationarity and therefore cannot, by itself, prove convergence of c_n(v)^(1/n).

Consequences in percolation and an annealed upper bound

Using uniqueness of the infinite cluster and translation ergodicity of independent supercritical bond percolation, the common limsup is translation-invariant and therefore deterministic. These two standard percolation theorems are dependencies, not proved here. This conclusion is already in the literature: Hubert Lacoin, *Non-coincidence of quenched and annealed connective constants on the supercritical planar percolation cluster*, arXiv:1203.6051.

There is also an elementary upper bound. For a fixed lattice root, let c_n^omega count open n-step self-avoiding walks, including environments where the root has a finite cluster. Every lattice self-avoiding walk uses n distinct edges, so

E c_n^omega = p^n c_n^lattice.

Write mu for the lattice connective constant. For any epsilon>0, eventually c_n^lattice<=(mu+epsilon)^n. Markov's inequality yields

P(c_n^omega >= [p(mu+2epsilon)]^n)
        <= ((mu+epsilon)/(mu+2epsilon))^n.

The right side is summable. Borel-Cantelli, followed by a countable epsilon sequence tending to zero, proves limsup(c_n^omega)^(1/n)<=p mu almost surely. It remains valid after conditioning the root to belong to the infinite cluster. Lacoin proves a strictly smaller bound in the planar supercritical regime p<1; that strict improvement is not derived here.

Why the obvious subadditivity route stops

Cutting a walk at time m only gives

c_(m+n)^omega(v) <= c_m^omega(v) max_(u reachable in m steps) c_n^omega(u).

The endpoint maximum depends on the same environment and is not c_n^omega(v), nor a single predetermined stationary translate. Fekete's lemma therefore does not apply to log c_n^omega(v). Kingman's theorem needs a specified stationary subadditive process; no such process has been constructed by this inequality. Replacing the maximum with a typical-root value would require a uniform estimate over the growing set of endpoints and cannot be justified by an almost-sure statement at each fixed root alone.

Exact obstruction: prove that the liminf has the same exponential rate as the deterministic limsup. Root-independence, annealed expectations, and subexponential prefactors in the displayed comparison do not resolve it.

Proof appendix: P0032_brownian_prefix_candidate

Candidate and author-check labels inside this appendix govern its status. Mathematical source notation is preserved.

P0032: a Brownian-prefix embedding argument

Written 2026-09-06. Status: complete candidate proof, undergoing author checks and a prior-literature search. No independent review and no novelty claim. Do not promote this file merely because a ledger validator accepts it.

Source problem: Burdzy and Scheutzow, *Forward Brownian motion*, arXiv:1302.6958, Problem 7.5, PDF page 26 (published 2014). The source asks whether a process on nonnegative time whose law is equivalent to Wiener measure can be preceded by a random finite path segment so that the concatenated process has Wiener law.

The interpretation here allows an extension of the probability space and dependence between prefix and suffix. The joining time is an arbitrary finite random time, not a stopping time for the ordinary Brownian filtration. Requiring a Brownian stopping time would immediately force the suffix itself to have Wiener law by the strong Markov property, so that different requirement cannot hold for a general equivalent law.

A general lemma

Let (E,F,P) be a standard Borel probability space, and let theta:E->E be an invertible, measurable, measure-preserving, ergodic transformation with measurable inverse. Let Q have density f>=0 with respect to P, with integral f dP=1. On an extension supporting independent uniform random variables, there exists an almost surely finite integer N>=0 such that, for Z with law P, theta^N Z has law Q.

This is a filling construction related to classical work of Chacon-Ornstein, Rost, and Thorisson. The following proof is supplied in full; claiming its application as a new research result requires a separate literature check.

Construction of remaining densities

For an integrable density h let Lh=h composed with theta^(-1). Thus pushing hP forward under theta gives (Lh)P. Set

a_0=1,  b_0=f,
    m_n=min(a_n,b_n),
    a_(n+1)=L(a_n-m_n),
    b_(n+1)=b_n-m_n.

All these functions are measurable and nonnegative. Inductively 0<=a_n<=1. The b_n decrease and are bounded above by f, so they have an integrable pointwise limit b_infinity. Since L preserves integrals, both remaining densities have the same mass r_n:

integral a_n dP = integral b_n dP = r_n,
    r_n decreases to r = integral b_infinity dP,
    sum_(n>=0) integral m_n dP = 1-r.

The remaining mass must be zero

Iteration gives the exact identity

a_n = 1 - sum_(k=0)^(n-1) L^(n-k)m_k.

For each fixed k, the ergodic theorem for the measure-preserving transformation theta^(-1) gives

(1/M) sum_(n=k+1)^(M-1) L^(n-k)m_k
       -> integral m_k dP

in L1(P). The L1 assertion follows from the usual almost-sure ergodic theorem and equality of the integrals for nonnegative averages, or by approximation from bounded functions. Initial omitted terms do not affect the limit.

Truncate the sum in k at K. Its tail has L1 norm at most sum_(k>=K) integral m_k dP, uniformly in M. That tail tends to zero as K grows. Therefore

(1/M) sum_(n=0)^(M-1) a_n -> r

in L1(P), where r on the right is the constant function.

Suppose r>0. There is a delta>0 such that B={b_infinity>=delta} has positive P-measure. On B,

m_n=min(a_n,b_n) >= min(a_n,delta)
        >= min(1,delta) a_n,

where the last step uses a_n<=1. Averaging and integrating over B gives

liminf_M (1/M) sum_(n<M) integral_B m_n dP
        >= min(1,delta) r P(B) > 0.

But the entire sum of the integrals of m_n is at most one, so its Cesaro average tends to zero. This contradiction proves r=0. In particular sum_n m_n=f in L1 and almost everywhere.

Realizing the matching as a random shift

Sample Z with law P and independent U_0,U_1,... uniform on [0,1]. At stage n, if not previously accepted, inspect theta^n Z and accept when

U_n <= h_n(theta^n Z),
    h_n=m_n/a_n on {a_n>0}, and h_n=0 elsewhere.

By induction, the subprobability distribution of theta^n Z before this test is a_n P. The mass accepted at stage n has distribution m_n P, and the surviving mass pushed forward has distribution a_(n+1)P. Hence P(N>=n)=r_n tends to zero, while the law of theta^N Z is sum_n m_n P=fP=Q. Every U_n is independent of Z and of preceding U's, so the induction is valid even though h_n can inspect an entire trajectory. This proves the lemma.

Application to Brownian motion

Let E=C_0(R) carry two-sided Wiener measure P. Define

(theta w)(t)=w(t+1)-w(1), t in R.

This is an invertible measurable measure-preserving transformation. It is ergodic: the path is measurably equivalent to its bi-infinite sequence of unit-length Brownian increment segments, and theta shifts this independent identically distributed sequence by one. Ergodicity of that shift follows by approximating an invariant event by finite-coordinate cylinder events and using independence of sufficiently separated cylinders.

Let Q_plus be the desired law of X on C_0([0,infinity)), and let f_plus=dQ_plus/dW_plus. Define f(w)=f_plus(w restricted to [0,infinity)). Its integral under P is one. Apply the lemma to Q=fP. The forward part of theta^N W,

Y_t=W_(N+t)-W_N, t>=0,

has exactly law Q_plus, and N is finite and nonnegative. The path obtained by taking W on [0,N] and continuing with W_N+Y_(t-N) is identically the original path W on nonnegative time. It therefore has Wiener law.

If the requested suffix X already lives on a specified probability space, use the regular conditional distribution of (W,N) given Y in this coupling to extend that space. Standard Borel path spaces ensure this disintegration exists. In the resulting coupling Y=X almost surely, so the prescribed X receives a prefix without changing its law.

Thus the argument gives the requested embedding under the stated interpretation, and even uses only absolute continuity Q_plus<<W_plus rather than equivalence. It does not require a finite expectation for N and does not give a computable finite-lookahead rule.

Checks and strongest objections

1. Pushforward direction: Lh=h composed with theta^(-1), so removing m_n and then moving forward really gives the stated a_(n+1). Using h composed with theta would reverse the density transport.
2. The bound a_n<=1 is indispensable in the contradiction. It follows from stationarity of the starting probability P; an arbitrary initial distribution would need a different argument.
3. No independence of the sampled future blocks is asserted. The density recursion replaces the invalid idea that infinitely long Brownian futures can be used as disjoint rejection trials.
4. The proof preserves the unconditional Wiener marginal of W. Selecting a suffix changes its law but does not condition the original W on the success of an event; acceptance occurs almost surely.
5. A two-state cyclic transformation with uniform P and target density (2,0) matches half the mass at n=0 and the other half after one shift. The density recursion yields Q exactly and checks its direction and normalization.
6. The mathematical and interpretive risk is that Problem 7.5 imposes an additional implicit restriction on the prefix. The displayed source wording contains no independence or stopping-time restriction. Check the surrounding paper and subsequent literature before labeling the problem solved.
7. A pre-existing embedding/filling theorem may already imply this conclusion. The argument would remain a valid explanation, but novelty and open-at-start status would then need to be withdrawn.

Prior-literature leads being checked: Pitman-Tang (2015), *The Slepian zeros and Brownian bridge embedded in Brownian motion*, Theorem 5.7 (a two-sided random shift), and Chacon-Ornstein/Rost filling schemes. Theorem 5.7 alone allows a shift of either sign; the nonnegative direction is established directly above.

A determinism obstruction and why the auxiliary uniforms matter

A counter-check communicated by the parent agent identifies a real boundary of the lemma. If an integer shift n(z) is a deterministic measurable function of z alone, the full shifted law has density

g(y) = sum_(k in Z) 1_{n(theta^(-k)y)=k}

with respect to P. Indeed, for every measurable A, partitioning by n=k and applying invariance gives the integral of 1_A times this sum. Tonelli applies because the summands are nonnegative. Thus g is integer-valued almost everywhere; its integral is one, so the sum is finite almost everywhere. Arbitrary noninteger f cannot arise this way. This is a counterexample to a stronger, deterministic version of the general lemma, not to the displayed randomized construction.

The present argument explicitly uses independent uniforms. Averaging their conditional acceptance probabilities yields real-valued densities m_n, so the integer-valued obstruction does not apply. Alternatively, a future marginal may hide randomness in the unobserved past; a statement about that projection must be distinguished from a statement about the full two-sided law. No deterministic full-path transport is claimed here.

This check was independently suggested but does not constitute an independent review of the complete candidate proof. The author has re-derived the density formula above. Holroyd and Peres, *Extra heads and invariant allocations* (2005), arXiv:math/0306402, Theorem 17 and the following remark, provide another prior-literature lead: their greedy transport can restrict displacements on Z to nonnegative integers. Their displayed setting uses binary target capacities; the density-matching proof here supplies its own general-density argument.

Proof appendix: P0033_stationary_flow_counterexample_candidate

Candidate and author-check labels inside this appendix govern its status. Mathematical source notation is preserved.

P0033: a stationary Galton-Watson flow that is not ergodic

Written 2026-09-06. Complete candidate counterexample; author checking and prior-literature search are in progress. No independent review or novelty claim. This file is not counted as a solved open problem merely because it exists.

Source: Lyons and Peres, *Probability on Trees and Networks*, version 19 August 2026, Question 17.18, printed page 575 (PDF page 592). The question asks whether a flow rule admitting a stationary measure equivalent to Galton-Watson measure must induce an ergodic Markov chain. Proposition 17.17 proves this under strict positivity of all child weights. Exercise 17.22 gives a nonergodic example with a merely absolutely continuous stationary measure; equivalence is the important extra requirement here.

Construction and claim

Let GW be the law of a rooted Galton-Watson tree with independent offspring numbers D satisfying

P(D=2)=3/5,   P(D=3)=2/5.

The tree has no leaves and mean offspring 12/5. We construct an isomorphism-invariant measurable color c(T) in {0,1}, each with probability 1/2, such that almost surely:

* at a vertex with two children, its color is the AND of its child colors;
* at a vertex with three children, its color is the OR of its child colors.

Both Boolean rules preserve unanimity. Consequently every vertex has at least one child of its own color. At every vertex, the flow chooses uniformly among those children. The resulting flow is consistent under restriction to a descendant subtree. GW itself is stationary for the Markov chain that follows this flow; nevertheless the root color is constant along the chain and has probability 1/2 for each value. Thus the chain is not ergodic, despite a stationary measure equal to GW.

The color is a function of the uncolored tree

For a finite truncation of T at level n, give the vertices at that level independent Bernoulli(1/2) colors and propagate colors upward using AND at two-child vertices and OR at three-child vertices. Let q_n(T) be the probability, over these temporary boundary colors, that the root color is one. Thus q_0=1/2. It is a measurable, isomorphism-invariant function of the uncolored tree through level n, with values in [0,1].

The mean output probability when the children independently have probability p of color one is

F(p)=(3/5)p^2+(2/5)[1-(1-p)^3].

Direct substitution gives F(1/2)=1/2. Let F_n denote the sigma-field of the tree through level n, meaning that the offspring numbers at depths below n have been exposed. Conditional on F_n, the offspring numbers of the level-n vertices are independent with the displayed law. The expected probability of a one at each such vertex after one additional level is 1/2. The root Boolean formula is a multilinear polynomial in the probabilities at its boundary vertices, because it is a read-once formula on a tree. Taking conditional expectations therefore gives

E[q_(n+1) | F_n]=q_n.

Hence q_n is a bounded martingale. It converges almost surely and in L2 to a measurable q_infinity, with E q_infinity=1/2.

We must show that q_infinity is {0,1}-valued; otherwise a random boundary color would remain and would not define the required flow on uncolored trees. Put r_n=E[q_n^2]. Equivalently, use two independent boundary colorings of the same uncolored truncation: r_n is the probability that both root outputs are one. Each output has marginal probability 1/2. For a two-child AND, the probability of two one outputs is r_n^2. For a three-child OR, inclusion-exclusion gives

1-2(1/2)^3+r_n^3=3/4+r_n^3,

since for a pair with both marginals 1/2 the probabilities of 00 and 11 agree. Different child subtrees are independent. It follows that

r_0=1/4,
    r_(n+1)=3/10+(3/5)r_n^2+(2/5)r_n^3.

For 1/4 <= r < 1/2, the increment factors as

3/10+(3/5)r^2+(2/5)r^3-r
      =(r-1/2)[(2/5)r^2+(4/5)r-3/5] > 0.

The quadratic factor is negative throughout [1/4,1/2], and the recursion maps this interval into itself because it is increasing and fixes 1/2. The only fixed point in this interval is 1/2: the other positive root is (sqrt(10)-2)/2 > 1/2. Thus r_n increases to 1/2. By L2 convergence,

E[q_infinity(1-q_infinity)] = 1/2-lim r_n = 0.

Nonnegativity forces q_infinity in {0,1} almost surely. Define c(T)=q_infinity(T) on this probability-one set. The recursion for q_n at the root is a continuous polynomial in the q_(n-1) values of its child subtrees, so passage to the limit proves the AND/OR color recursion. Apply this simultaneously to all vertices, a countable set. For a global Borel definition on leafless locally finite rooted trees, set c(T)=0 outside the Borel set where the displayed limit exists and is binary (and outside the offspring-2-or-3 tree space). At each vertex apply the same local same-color rule; if there are no same-color children, use equal weights on all children. Defining ray probabilities by products of these local weights gives consistency everywhere, including the null exceptional set, and leaves all GW claims unchanged.

Stationarity with no density change

Write A_c={T:c(T)=c} and GW_c=GW(. | A_c), for c=0,1. Both events have probability 1/2. Conditional on the root offspring number D=d and the vector of its child colors (c_1,...,c_d), the child subtrees are independent, and child i has law GW_(c_i). This follows directly from independent Galton-Watson child subtrees and the fact that each color is a measurable function of its own subtree.

The color of the parent is a deterministic function of d and this vector. Let I_c={i:c_i=c} when the parent color is c. This set is nonempty by the AND/OR rules. Choosing uniformly from I_c uses no further information about the internal shape of the chosen subtree. For every bounded measurable function h,

E[ sum_(i in I_c) h(T_i)/|I_c| | D=d, (c_i) ]
       = integral h dGW_c.

Averaging conditional on the parent color c proves GW_c K=GW_c for the child-selection kernel K. Finally,

GW K = (1/2) GW_0 K + (1/2) GW_1 K
         = (1/2) GW_0 + (1/2) GW_1 = GW.

Thus the stationary measure is exactly GW, not merely an absolutely continuous measure supported on a smaller set of trees.

Consistency and failure of ergodicity

The local rule assigns probability 1/|I_c| to each same-color child and zero to the other children. Products of these transition weights assign mass to all finite initial ray segments. Their consistency defines a Borel probability measure on the infinite boundary by the usual cylinder extension. At a descendant receiving positive mass, the conditional continuation is the identical rule applied to that descendant subtree. This is a flow rule in the sense of the source.

The event A_1 has GW measure 1/2, and K(T,A_1)=1_(A_1)(T) almost surely. Therefore it is a nontrivial invariant event for the stationary Markov chain. Equivalently, in its stationary path space the root-color event is invariant under the time shift and has probability 1/2. The chain is not ergodic.

Adversarial checks and remaining status work

1. A recursive distributional fixed point alone is insufficient: it might require extra randomness after the full tree is known. The second-moment calculation above proves this does not happen here.
2. The two boundary trials are independent conditional on the tree, so their joint probability really is E q_n^2. Their unconditional correlation is retained in r_n; treating them as independent would destroy the proof.
3. Merely conditioning GW on the existence of an n-ray does not give equivalence to GW, as the source's Exercise 17.22 illustrates. Here the two invariant classes partition a GW-full set and GW itself is stationary.
4. The selected child may depend on all child colors, but these are exactly the conditions used in the disintegration argument. No unconditional independence after selection is asserted.
5. Rooted isomorphisms permute children; both Boolean rules, q_n, its limit, and uniform selection of eligible children are invariant under those permutations. No hidden labels are used.
6. Strict positivity of every child weight fails on some configurations, as it must in view of Proposition 17.17. At a two-child vertex with colors 0,1 only the zero child is chosen; at a three-child vertex with both colors only the one children are chosen.
7. All arithmetic is exact rational algebra. Independent symbolic or finite-depth checks are diagnostics only; the argument proves all depths and the infinite-tree limit.
8. Search prior work on endogeny and interpretable tree automata before calling this a new resolution. Johnson, Podder, and Skerman, *Random tree recursions: which fixed points correspond to tangible sets of trees?*, arXiv:1808.03019, is a relevant primary source. Its general endogeny criteria are not assumed in this self-contained argument.

Exact scope check: the definition in Section 17.5, printed pages 573-574, explicitly permits nonnegative child probabilities and imposes conditional-flow consistency only at children with positive mass. Equivariance is required and was checked above. Thus zeros in this construction are permitted by the source definition; they are not a silent relaxation of it.

Proof appendix: P0096_FPP_minimum_bound_counterexample_candidate
